# Phase 1 decisions, 2026-10-17

Continues `phase-1-decisions-2026-10-16.md`: more decisions taken while implementing Phase 1 that change or refine `phase-1-plan-2026-10-16.md`. Each one links to the issue holding the full discussion.

## CLI and RPC (#17)

- **Socket.** `<home>/bridge.sock` is served by `internal/bridge/rpc.go`, with one newline-delimited JSON request and one reply per connection. A request is `{"cmd","args"}`. A reply is `{"ok":true,"result":{...}}` or `{"ok":false,"error":"...","code":"..."}`. The `code` field extends the plan. Its values are:
  - `bad_request`, `unknown_cmd`
  - `not_found`, `exists`, `unavailable`, `hail_closed`
  - `shutting_down`, `forbidden`, `internal`

  Unknown fields in the request or its args are a `bad_request`. Requests are capped at 1 MiB, and each connection gets a 10s read and write deadline. At most 64 connections are served at once, and any more get `unavailable` at once. Handlers run on the daemon's context with a 30s timeout. The Go client is `bridge.Call`, and the wire types (`CrewAddArgs`, `CrewView`, `LogTailResult`, ...) are exported from `internal/bridge`.
- **Lifecycle.** `Run` starts the server after `Launch`, `ensurePumps` and the tour re-attach, and stops it first on shutdown. Stopping waits for in-flight requests, and the socket file is unlinked. A request still being read when the server closes, or one arriving after the daemon's context is cancelled, gets `shutting_down`. A stale socket is removed on start, because the bridge lock already rules out a second daemon, but anything at `bridge.sock` that isn't a socket makes `Run` fail. A socket path of 104 bytes or more fails `Run`, and the client checks the same limit.
- **Access.**
  - `Run` creates the home `0700`, and tightens an existing one to `0700`, logging that it did.
  - It refuses a home owned by another uid, and a sticky (shared) directory such as `/tmp`.
  - The socket is `0600`.
  - On Linux every connection's peer is checked with `SO_PEERCRED`, and a uid other than the bridge's effective uid gets `forbidden`. Other platforms skip the check (`peercred_other.go`, build tag `!linux`) and rely on the file modes.
- **Commands.**
  - `crew.add {id,name,rank,division,spec,dossier}` returns the crew row. The CLI's flags are `--name` (required), `--rank`, `--division`, `--spec` and `--dossier`. `dossier` must be an absolute path, and the CLI makes it one.
  - `crew.list` returns `{crew}`.
  - `order.create {title,body,division,assignee,base_ref,budget_tokens}` returns `{id}`. A blank title is refused. `assignee` is required in Phase 1, so every order is directed. `created_by` is `captain`.
  - `order.list` returns `{orders}`.
  - `hails.list {status?}` returns `{hails}`. An empty status lists every hail. The CLI defaults to `--status open`.
  - `hails.answer {hail_id,option?,decision?}` returns the decided hail.
  - `log.tail {since_id,limit}` returns `{entries,last_id}`. It holds the oldest entries above `since_id`. `limit` defaults to 500, with a cap of 1000. `last_id` stays at `since_id` when nothing is new.
- **Store.** New `ListHails(ctx, status)`, and `ListLogLimit(ctx, sinceID, limit)`. `ListLog` keeps its signature.
- **CLI** (`cmd/bmus`, Cobra).
  - Global `--home`: default `$BMUS_HOME`, else `~/.bmus`.
  - A missing or dead socket gives "bridge not running (start it with `bmus bridge run`)" and exit code 3. Any other error exits 1.
  - The list commands print a table, or JSON with `--json`.
  - `log tail` prints `<ts> <kind> <crew> <order> <text>`, or NDJSON with `--json`. With `--follow` it polls every 500ms, or again at once after a full page, and exits 0 on SIGINT or SIGTERM. Once it has connected, it waits out a bridge that is stopping or gone, and notes on stderr that it is waiting, instead of failing. `bridge.IsBridgeDown` decides this. It covers a missing or dead socket, a `shutting_down` reply, and a connection lost mid-request (`ErrConnLost`: a reset, or EOF without a reply). `TestFollowSurvivesBridgeRestart` checks it across a SIGTERM and a restart.
- **`bmus bridge run`.**
  - It owns signal handling. The first SIGINT or SIGTERM cancels `Run`'s context, which starts a graceful shutdown. Default handling is then restored, so a second signal kills it at once.
  - `--combadge`, else `combadge.mjs` next to the executable, else `./combadge/dist/combadge.mjs`. The path is made absolute, and startup fails if the file is missing.
  - `--transporter tsh|holodeck`, default `tsh`.
  - `--holodeck-script` is required with holodeck and is made absolute. It is passed as `--harness holodeck --script <abs>`.
  - `--holodeck-root`, default `<home>/holodeck`. A root over 60 bytes logs a warning.
  - `--combadge-arg`, repeatable, feeds `Config.CombadgeArgs`.
  - `--log-level`; logs go to stderr through slog.
- **Order YAML** is decoded strictly with `gopkg.in/yaml.v3`, so unknown fields are an error. It must hold a single document, though a trailing `---` is accepted, and the title must not be blank. The fields are `title`, `division`, `assignee`, `base_ref`, `budget_tokens` and `body`.
- **Tests.** `cmd/bmus/e2e_test.go` builds the binary and runs `bridge run --transporter holodeck` as a subprocess. It is also the acceptance test, and takes about 5s. It requires the progress entry and the hail to reach `log tail --follow` while the mission is blocked on the hail. The rest of the mission must arrive after the answer, and log ids must rise strictly. It skips if `BMUS_COMBADGE` is unset and the bundle can't be built.

## RPC follow-ups (#40)

- **An existing home others can reach is refused.** This replaces the "tightens an existing one to `0700`" rule under Access above. With `--home ~`, a chmod would change the user's home directory. `Run` now fails with a message naming the mode and suggesting `chmod 700 <home>` or another `--home`, and leaves the mode as it was. A home `Run` creates is still made `0700`.
- **The connection deadline is set in `accept`.** The 10s deadline is set under the server's lock, before the `closed` check, and `serveConn` no longer sets it. Before, a connection accepted while `Close` ran could have `Close`'s read deadline overwritten by `serveConn`, holding shutdown for up to 10s.
- **`log tail --follow` retries `unavailable`.** When every RPC slot is taken, the follower notes on stderr that the bridge is busy and retries, doubling its poll interval up to 5s. A successful poll resets the interval to 500ms. Unlike a down bridge, this applies to the first poll too: an `unavailable` reply means the bridge is running.

## Comms pump

- **An attach that never sends its first line is recycled too (#38).** The pump makes a session its live connection only when the session's first line arrives. Before #38, a healthy probe with stale heartbeats recycled only that live connection. The pump could therefore hang for good while the probe still reported healthy, and nothing reconnected. Two cases cause this: `tsh beams exec` stalls before attach prints anything, or serve's event loop is wedged but the kernel still accepts the Unix-socket connect from its backlog. The pump now also tracks the attach attempt in progress (`Pump.attempt`). It is set just before `Link.Attach` and cleared when `connectOnce` returns (changed by #81 below: cleared at the first line). When no connection is live, `recycle()` cancels that attempt, but only after the attempt has run for `HeartbeatStale`. A slow attach gets the same grace as a quiet live connection, so the pump doesn't kill it at every check. Such an attach is detected within about `HeartbeatStale + SickbayCheckEvery`, plus the probe's own time. A gone beam still fails the probe and reaches sickbay as before.

## Exit-criteria tests (#18)

- **Placement.** `TestMissionSurvivesBridgeRestart` and `TestCrewSickbayOnBeamLoss` live in `cmd/bmus/exit_criteria_test.go`, not `internal/bridge/holodeck_test.go` as plan §11 says. Both run the built `bmus` binary as a subprocess, and the harness for that (binary build, short home, CLI helpers, log follower, beam cleanup) is in package `cmd/bmus` (#17). The acceptance command is `go test ./cmd/bmus/ -run 'TestMissionSurvivesBridgeRestart|TestCrewSickbayOnBeamLoss' -v`. `e2e.bridgeArgs` passes extra `bridge run` flags; serve gets `--heartbeat-interval 100` and `--step-log <abs>` as one `--combadge-arg` per argv element.
- **Restart.** The test uses `pause.json` and SIGKILLs `bridge run` once the "halfway, pausing" progress entry reaches `log tail --follow`. That happens about 0.4s into the 10s pause, also with every core busy. A precondition then requires the step log to hold steps 0 and 1 only: the sleep (step 2) is logged only when it completes, so this proves the kill landed inside the pause. A new bridge on the same home completes the mission. The test asserts that serve's pid is unchanged, every step ran exactly once, there is exactly one `done` entry carrying the report, and each progress line was logged once.
- **Sickbay.** The test uses `crash.json`. Before calling `Kill` it requires the `checkpoints` row, read straight from `bridge.db` read-only since no command lists checkpoints, within 30s. It calls `Kill(current_beam)` from a fresh `transporter.NewHolodeck` on the same root; `Kill` needs only the pid file. Sickbay lands 30-35s after the kill (the pump's 30s staleness plus its next 5s check), then 2-3s more of DBOS notification polls. The test allows 45s. That deadline relies on serve's `--heartbeat-interval 100`, which puts the last heartbeat at the kill; the default 10s interval could add up to 10s. The order fails with a single `fail` entry `{reason: "beam lost", code: "beam_lost"}`, and the checkpoint's three files are in `<home>/cargo/<order>/<seq>/`.
- **Cost.** About 13s and 38s. The `cmd/bmus` package takes about 60s under `-race`, well inside `go test`'s 10m default.

## Workflow follow-ups (#35)

- **The tour's backstop closes a stray open hail.** A mission that settles through `bridge_error` calls `order-failed` without knowing its open hail, and a mission that panics settles nothing. Either left the hail `open`, so a later `AnswerHail` "succeeded" into a workflow that was gone. `Daemon.backstop`, run by `settle-mission` and `tour-end`, now first closes any hail still open for the order (`store.OpenHailForOrder`, new) as `timed_out`, logged "hail H-0001 closed unanswered", whatever the order's state. Then it fails the order if it is still `away` or `hailing`, as before.
  - It never closes a hail a live mission still waits on. `settle-mission` runs only after the mission's `mission_done`, which is its last step, or once `mission-alive` has found the mission no longer PENDING or ENQUEUED. `tour-end` runs after the tour sent the mission a synthetic error. That error is queued ahead of anything the mission hasn't read, so its next Recv fails the order, and no answer to a hail open at that point could reach it.
  - Replay and races: `closeHail` uses the new `store.TimeOutOpenHail`, one transaction that moves the hail strictly from `open` to `timed_out` and appends the "closed unanswered" entry only when that update changed the row. Of the mission's `order-failed` and the tour's backstop racing on the same hail, exactly one closes and logs it, and a step re-run can never find the hail closed but unlogged. The order update is conditional too, so a re-run of the backstop finds nothing left to do.
- **The pump start is recorded.** The tour no longer branches on an unrecorded `EnsurePump` result, and no longer waits with `time.After` in workflow code. A `start-pump` step calls `EnsurePump`, retries a failure up to 5 times with backoff (500ms doubling) inside the step, and returns `pumpStart{Failed}`: empty when the pump started, else the last error's text. The tour goes to sickbay (`pump_failed`) only when the recorded `Failed` is set, so a replay takes the original path.
  - `ErrDaemonStopping` inside the step waits for the step's context and returns its error. DBOS records nothing for a step whose workflow was cancelled, and the tour returns and stays PENDING.
  - After a recorded success the tour still calls `EnsurePump` on every run, outside any step (`rearmPump`), since the pump is process-local. Its result never changes the path. `ErrDaemonStopping` waits for the context and returns as above, and any other error is only logged. Run's `ensurePumps` has normally started the pump already, and without one, sends fail with `send_failed`.
  - The new step changes the tour's step sequence. `workflowVersion` stays `"1"`, because bridge databases made before the first release are disposable (see "DBOS wiring" in `phase-1-decisions-2026-10-16.md`).
- **Dismissal sets the crew row `dismissed`.** The tour's `tour-end` step does it on the dismiss path with `UpdateCrewStatus`, which is idempotent. Before this, the row stayed `on_deck`/`away` with its `current_beam`, so a restarted daemon's `ensurePumps` attached a pump to it. `attachCrewTours`, `ensurePumps` (`pumpCrewStatuses`) and `CreateOrder` (`ErrUnavailable`) already skip a dismissed crew member. `AddCrew` refuses its id with `ErrExists`, as for any taken id. `current_beam` is kept.
- **`isRecvDecodeError` is pinned to DBOS:** `TestIsRecvDecodeErrorMatchesDBOS` sends a workflow a message that fails a real `Recv` decode, so a DBOS upgrade that rewords "decoding received message" fails it.

## Conditional crew-status writes (#43)

- **A mission never overwrites `sickbay` or `dismissed`.** A tour that ends waits at most 5 minutes for its mission to settle. A mission still running after that can process a hail it already had queued, and its `record-hail` and `decide-hail` steps used to set the crew row `hailing` or `away` unconditionally. That overwrote the tour's `sickbay` or `dismissed`, and a restarted daemon's `ensurePumps` would attach a pump to the crew member again.
  - Both steps now go through `setMissionCrewStatus`, which calls the new `store.UpdateCrewStatusIf(ctx, id, status, from)`. It updates the row only when its current status is in `from`, here `away` or `hailing`, and reports whether it did. A skipped write logs a warning; the hail and its log entry are still recorded.
  - A hail recorded after `tour-end` is closed by the mission's own `order-failed` when its next Recv reaches the queued synthetic error. A mission that dies before that left the hail open; fixed in #47 for `bridge_error` (see below).
  - The tour's own writes stay unconditional: `claim-order` (`away`), `settle-mission` (`on_deck`), the beam-up (`on_deck`) and its terminal `sickbay` and `dismissed`. They run only while the tour is the crew member's authority, and only the tour writes the terminal states.
- **Store.** `MarkHailTimedOut` is removed: `TimeOutOpenHail` (#35) replaced its last caller.

## Mission closes its own hail (#47)

- **`order-failed` with no known hail closes the order's open hail.** `settleFailed` with a nil hail now calls the new `Daemon.closeOpenHail` (`store.OpenHailForOrder`, then `closeHail`) inside the `order-failed` step. A mission that ends through `bridge_error` no longer depends on a live tour to close its hail. This covers a hail recorded after `tour-end` and a `record-hail` that failed for good after its `InsertHail` committed. `Daemon.backstop` uses the same helper.
  - The lookup is a store read inside the step: a replay reuses the recorded step, and a re-run finds the hail closed (`TimeOutOpenHail` is a strict `open` -> `timed_out` update that logs only when the row changed).
  - The other nil-hail exits (`send-order` and `send-answer` failing) have no open hail and find none.
  - A panicking mission still settles nothing. DBOS turns the panic into a workflow error, and `missionWorkflow`'s settle code never runs. Its hail is closed only by the tour's `settle-mission` after `mission-alive`, while the tour is running. A panic is a bug to fix, not an exit to settle, and is left at that.

## SDK harness (#11)

- **Finding `claude`.** `serve --harness sdk` takes `--claude-path <path>`, checked at startup. Without it, `claude` is looked up on PATH when an order starts, and a miss fails that order with `error` "claude executable not found on PATH; pass serve --claude-path". The miss reaches the captain's log as an order failure, where a startup failure would look like a crash-looping beam.
- **New serve flags.** `--repo <dir>` is the agent's working directory, by default serve's cwd. Phase 1 doesn't say how code reaches a beam, so #19 has to pick it. `--work-dir` is now shared by holodeck and sdk, and defaults to `<outbox dir>/<harness>`. Each harness-specific flag is refused with any other harness.
- **Push policy.** A PreToolUse hook classifies the `command` of any tool that has a string one (Bash, Monitor). It drops backslash escapes and quotes, splits at command separators (`;`, `&&`, `||`, `|&`, `|`, newline, a lone `&`; the `&` in `>&`, `<&` and `&>` is a redirection, not a separator), and checks each git invocation:
  - deny: `push` or `send-pack` with `-f` (alone or in a short-flag cluster), `--force`, `--force-with-lease[=...]`, `--mirror`, a prefix of one of these (git accepts unambiguous prefixes), or a `+refspec`. `--force-with-lease` is denied too: it still overwrites remote work, only more safely.
  - ask: any other `push` or `send-pack`, remote deletes (`:branch`, `--delete`) and `--prune` included, which the captain approves.
  - ask: a subcommand built at run time, holding `$` or a backtick (`git $(echo pu)sh`, `git pu${X}sh`), whatever the rest of the command says.
  - ask, failing closed: a command that mentions `push`, `send-pack` or `mirror` and has a git invocation with no readable subcommand or one outside a short list of builtins that never push. That catches aliases (git ignores an alias that shadows a builtin), `git config`, and `git remote ... --mirror`.
  - no decision: everything else, `git` with no subcommand at all included when none of those words appear (`which git`).

  Indirection through variables (`$GIT push`) or decoded scripts isn't followed. `mirror` is a trigger word because `remote.<name>.mirror` makes a plain `git push` mirror. The beam holds no push credentials (D6), so the hook is a guard rather than the security boundary.
- **AskUserQuestion is forced to ask.** A second PreToolUse hook answers `ask` for it, so its questions reach `canUseTool` under `bypassPermissions` whatever the CLI's own default for that tool.
- **Hails.** Refs are `sdk-<order>-<n>`. A `tool_approval` hail's question quotes the Bash command, or reads "Allow <tool>?" for other tools, and its context holds the tool, its input and the SDK's reason. An AskUserQuestion answer is the answer's `decision`, or the picked option's label when the decision is empty. `multiSelect` is passed in the context only; the captain answers it as text.
- **Transcript.** Every hook input carries the main session's `transcript_path`. A matcher-less PreToolUse hook records it, and it runs before `request_handover` itself. The fallback is `<CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR or ~/.claude>/projects/<cwd with non-alphanumerics as '-'>/<session id>.jsonl`. A transcript that can't be found fails the tool call, and the agent sees the error.
- **Checkpoint.** `request_handover` writes `<work-dir>/<order>/<n>/` (n from 1 per order). The bundle holds HEAD and its branch ref, minus `base_ref`. When `base_ref` doesn't resolve or HEAD has no commits beyond it, the bundle holds the whole history, because git refuses an empty bundle. Uncommitted changes aren't bundled; the tool description tells the agent to commit first. `commit` is `git rev-parse HEAD`. Handovers run one at a time; the dir is created only after the repo answers, and removed if writing fails, so a failed call leaves nothing and doesn't use up its `n`.
- **Report.** The run resolves when the query ends. The summary and artifacts come from the `report` tool, else the summary is the agent's final result text. `branch` is the checked-out branch, else `bmus/<order>`. The prompt asks the agent to work on `bmus/<order>` from `base_ref` and not to push. `usage` sums the result's `modelUsage` over every model, which covers subagents and compaction (`usage` is the main loop only); input includes cache reads and writes. A result other than success rejects the run with its subtype and errors.
- **Not done here.** `budget_tokens` isn't enforced, and `checkpoint`, `stand_down` and `recall` are logged and ignored, as in holodeck.
- **Tests.** `combadge/test/sdk.test.ts` injects a fake `query` (`sdkHarness(opts, {query, env})`) that calls the harness's hooks and `canUseTool`, and calls its MCP tools through a real MCP client over an in-memory transport. No model or `claude` runs. The real-order acceptance in #11 is manual, on a real beam, and belongs to #19.
- **Bundle size and startup.** Inlining the SDK grows `dist/combadge.mjs` from 26.5 KiB to about 2.2 MiB. The registry imports the sdk harness only when `--harness sdk` is chosen, so serve with another harness, and attach, don't evaluate the SDK. `node dist/combadge.mjs --help` takes about 115ms instead of about 190ms; parsing the file is the rest.
- **`@modelcontextprotocol/sdk` is a direct dependency.** It was only a peer of the Agent SDK. The tests import its client, and the Agent SDK's types refer to it.

## Repos in and out of beams (#51, #53, #54)

These refine D6's mechanics. The "#19 has to pick it" note under the SDK harness is settled here.

- **No credentials on beams, no agent forwarding.** Delegation still inherits the captain's full permissions, and a forwarded SSH agent would let crew push anywhere the captain can. Only the bridge talks to a git server, through its own clone of each repo in `~/.bmus/repos/<name>` and the captain's usual git setup.
- **Code goes in as a bundle (#51).** Before sending an order, the bridge fetches its clone, bundles `base_ref` and copies the bundle onto the beam. combadge clones it into `<work-dir>/<order>/<name>` and checks out `bmus/<order>` before the harness starts. Phase 1 bundles the full history. Incremental bundles against a mirror in the beam image come later, as D6 planned.
- **Repos are a list from the start.** Orders, `order`, `report` and `checkpoint_ready` carry `repos: [...]`, one entry per repo with its name, ref, bundle and commit. Phase 1 refuses anything but one entry. Moving the protocol from one repo to many later would cost far more than a loop does now.
- **Work comes back as bundles (#53).** `report` carries one bundle per repo, and the order is `done` only once the bridge has pulled them into the cargo bay. The bridge then fetches each into its clone as `bmus/<order>`, automatically: it adds a local branch and touches no remote. Pushing stays the captain's own action until Phase 2 adds a bridge-side push behind a captain-only hail (#55).
- **No platform is hardcoded.** A repo is any URL `git` can fetch. Ticketing follows the git pattern, and I think it's the only safe shape while beams hold no credentials: reads go into the order, and writes come out as requests the bridge runs after Regulations allow them. The tracker is an optional, generic interface on the bridge, with adapters behind it and none in the core. A ship with no tracker refuses those requests and works otherwise unchanged (#54, Phase 3, #56).

## Harness and model per crew member (#58, #59)

- **Harness per crew member, fixed for a tour.** A harness is a different process (`claude` through the Agent SDK today, `codex` later), so it is a serve flag at provisioning. The tour passes the existing `crew.harness` to `StartCombadge`, and changing it takes a new tour.
- **Model per crew member, overridable per order.** `order` carries an optional `model`, set to the order's own model or else the crew member's (`crew.model`, empty for the harness default). It changes without re-provisioning a beam, and a small order can ask for a cheaper model than its crew member's.
- **The model string is opaque to the bridge.** Harnesses name models differently and the LLM proxy decides which exist, so only the harness checks it, when the order starts. An unknown model fails that order. `hello` reports the harness's default model.
- **Division defaults come with the Phase 2 roster (#59).** The model resolves from the order, then the crew member, then the division, then the harness default. The harness resolves from the crew member, then the division, then `sdk`. Phase 1 adds only the protocol field and the per-crew settings (#58). The protocol is the costly part to change later.

## Repo bundles in (#51)

Details beyond the issue, under "Repos in and out of beams" above.

- **Registry.** `bmus bridge run --repo <name>=<url>`, repeatable, like `--combadge-arg`. The bridge has no config file yet. A URL naming an existing local directory is made absolute. Names match `^[A-Za-z0-9_-][A-Za-z0-9._-]*$`, and `in` and all-digit names are reserved: `<work-dir>/<order>/in/` holds the bundles and numbered dirs hold checkpoints.
- **The bridge's clone.** `<home>/repos/<name>` is a bare repo. Origin's branches are fetched into `refs/remotes/origin/*` and tags into `refs/tags/*`, both forced and pruned, so a moved upstream tag doesn't break later fetches. The clone's own branches are only `bmus/<order>`. Git runs with the captain's config, with `--git-dir` and without the variables `git rev-parse --local-env-vars` lists, so an inherited `GIT_DIR` can't point it at another repo. Nothing can wait on a prompt: stdin is `/dev/null`, `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0`, and git runs in its own session with no controlling terminal, so ssh fails rather than asking on `/dev/tty`. A fetch is cut off after 10 minutes, and waiting for a clone's lock ends with the step's context. A cancelled git's process group gets SIGTERM, which lets git remove its lock files, and whatever of the group is left (ssh, say) is killed once git has exited.
- **base_ref.** It resolves as origin's branch first (`refs/remotes/origin/<ref>`), then as git resolves it in the clone: a tag, a commit id, `origin/<branch>`, or another order's `bmus/<order>`, which suits a follow-up order. One that doesn't resolve fails the order with `repo_failed` at once. Fetch and copy failures retry 5 times, as `pull-checkpoint` does.
- **The bundle carries `bmus/<order>`.** The step points `refs/heads/bmus/<order>` in the clone at base_ref's commit and bundles that one ref with its full history. `git clone --branch bmus/<order>` on the beam then works for any base_ref, and #53 fetches the work back into the same branch. Both the bridge's and the beam's copy of the bundle are removed once used.
- **Order storage.** A new `order_repos` table, not a column: the schema is applied with `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` and nothing migrates columns, so an existing database gains it on open. `orders.base_ref` stays, unused.
- **Mission.** `ship-repos` runs before `send-order`, which sends the repos it recorded. A re-run, after a retry or a bridge restart mid-step, fetches again and follows a moved base_ref. The copy on the beam is replaced, and nothing reads it before `send-order`. `workflowVersion` stays `"1"`, as in #35.
- **A tour ending during ship-repos.** The tour waits 5 minutes for a running mission to settle, then backstops the order. ship-repos can run longer, so `endTour` marks its tour ended (in memory, never unmarked; a replayed `endTour` marks it again after a restart) once the synthetic error is queued, before stopping the pump. ship-repos checks the mark (the mission's `TourID`) before anything else on every attempt, retries included, watches it while working, and also treats a crew member already in sickbay or dismissed as ended. It then cancels its work and records `TourEnded`; the mission sends no order and reads the queued error, so the order fails with `beam_lost` or `dismissed` inside the wait. A daemon shutdown racing the end records nothing, and the re-run after the restart decides. The usual mission-alive check has no deadline, so a long healthy ship-repos is never backstopped.
- **Where the bundle lands.** `Provider.WorkDir(beam)` is new, and each provider passes it as serve's `--work-dir`: `/tmp/bmus/work` for tsh, `<root>/<alias>/work` for holodeck. `--work-dir` is now a serve flag for every harness. Tsh's `CopyTo` runs `mkdir -p` first.
- **combadge.** serve checks the repos out before `harness.run`, for every harness, and holds commands that arrive meanwhile until the harness has registered its listeners. A failed checkout fails the order. A clone is renamed into place when complete, and a re-delivered order keeps an existing checkout, commits included. Harnesses get `order.repos: [{name, dir, base_ref, base_commit}]`. The sdk harness needs exactly one and bundles checkpoints from `base_commit`.

## Harness and model wiring (#58)

Details beyond the issue, under "Harness and model per crew member" above.

- **Flag precedence.** serve's command line is the provider's own flags, then `--harness <crew.harness>` (`CombadgeSpec.Harness`), then the provider's configured harness flags (`HolodeckConfig.Harness`), then `--combadge-arg`s. serve keeps the last value of a repeated flag. A configured `--harness` or a `--combadge-arg=--harness` therefore overrides the crew member's. Tests use this. Tsh has no configured harness flags.
- **`bmus bridge run --holodeck-script` is optional now.** With it, every holodeck beam runs `--harness holodeck --script <abs>`, overriding the crew member's harness as before. Without it, each beam runs its crew member's harness. The earlier "required with holodeck" rule under "CLI and RPC (#17)" no longer holds.
- **Harness validation.** `bridge.Harnesses` (`sdk`, `holodeck`, `echo`) is the list serve accepts. `AddCrew` refuses any other name as a bad request, and `crew add` checks it before calling the bridge. `crew add --harness` defaults to `sdk`.
- **Provisioning reads the harness from the store.** `provision` reads `crew.harness` on every attempt. Nothing changes it after `crew add`, which keeps it fixed for the tour. A reused tsh beam keeps the harness it started with: tsh's restart path reruns the service's original command.
- **Schema.** `crew.model` and `orders.model` are columns, `TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT ''`. `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` leaves an older table without them. `Open` adds each missing column listed in `store.addedColumns` with `ALTER TABLE ADD COLUMN`, checked against `pragma_table_info` first. A concurrent open that loses the race gets "duplicate column", ignored once the column is seen. A future column added to an existing table goes in that list and needs a constant default.
- **The model string.** The bridge stores and forwards it as given, with no trimming or checks. An empty model is omitted from `order`. The schema requires a non-empty `model` when present, on both `order` and `hello`.
- **combadge.** `Harness.model` is the default model reported in `hello`, omitted when undefined. No harness sets it today: the sdk harness can't know the SDK's default without running a query, and echo and holodeck run no model. The sdk harness passes `order.body.model` to `query()` as `options.model` when set. holodeck records the order's model in its step log as `model` and otherwise ignores it. This is how the holodeck tests see what reached the harness.

## Work back as bundles (#53)

Details beyond the issue, under "Repos in and out of beams" above. This supersedes two sentences of "SDK harness (#11)" above: the Checkpoint bullet's bundle of HEAD and its branch, and the Report bullet's `branch` (the checked-out branch, else `bmus/<order>`). `report` carries no `branch` now; each entry of its `repos` names `bmus/<order>`.

- **What a bundle holds.** Reports and checkpoints both bundle `bmus/<order>` and nothing else, minus the order's base commit, with the full-history fallback when the branch has no commits past it. `commit` is read back from the bundle. Work on other branches, a detached HEAD and uncommitted changes don't come back; the sdk prompt and the `request_handover` description already point the agent at `bmus/<order>`. A missing `bmus/<order>` fails the order (report) or the tool call (checkpoint).
- **Who bundles.** serve, for every harness, once the run resolves: `<work-dir>/<order>/out/<name>.bundle`, written to a temporary name and renamed. Harnesses return the report without `repos`. A failed bundle fails the order. `out` is now reserved next to `in` and numbers, in the bridge, combadge and the schema.
- **Checkpoints.** `<work-dir>/<order>/<n>/<name>.bundle` per repo replaces `work.bundle`, in sdk and holodeck alike. Holodeck checkpoints are now real git bundles, and its scripts gain a `commit` step: one commit per repo on the checked-out branch, recorded in the step log as `commits`. The new `work.json` fixture uses it.
- **Matching.** The bridge pairs a report's or checkpoint's `repos` with the order's repos: exactly one bundle each, on `bmus/<order>`, at an absolute path. Anything else is permanent. A report fails the order at once with `cargo_lost`, and a checkpoint is logged as `checkpoint_failed`. The repo names come from the order, never from the beam, so a beam can't pick a path on the bridge.
- **Storage.** A new `checkpoint_repos` table holds each checkpoint's bundles, for the same reason as `order_repos`. `checkpoints.commit` and `checkpoints.bundle_path` stay, unused. Final bundles get no table: they live at `<home>/cargo/<order>/final/<name>.bundle`, and the done entry's payload names them.
- **fetch-work.** It checks `git bundle list-heads` shows the reported commit, fetches with `--no-tags` and without writing `FETCH_HEAD`, and checks the branch after. The refspec is forced, so a branch the agent rewrote still lands. A commit mismatch, a file that isn't a bundle, a bundle whose prerequisites the clone lacks, or a fetch out of retries doesn't fail the order: its work is safe in the cargo bay. The order is still done, an `order` entry with code `fetch_failed` gives the first line of each repo's error, and the done entry reads "not fetched, bundle at <path>" for that repo.
- **Done entry.** Text: `<summary> (<name>: bmus/<order> at <12-char commit>; ...)`. Payload: `{summary, repos: [{name, branch, commit, bundle_path, fetch_error?}], artifacts, usage}`, with cargo bay paths instead of the beam's.
- **Tour end.** pull-work doesn't watch the tour-end mark. Work already reported is worth pulling while the beam answers, and a beam that is gone fails the order as `cargo_lost`. Each copy is cut off after 15 minutes and retried, so a pull can outlast the tour's 5-minute settle wait, and the backstop may fail the order meanwhile.
- **Settling is conditional.** `store.SettleOrder` moves an order to done or failed only from away or hailing, with its log entry in the same transaction. order-done, order-failed and the backstop all use it. An order already in the target state is left alone with no entry. An order the backstop failed stays failed when its work arrives later: the work stays in the cargo bay and the clone, and one `order` entry reads "the order's work arrived after the order had ended (<name>: bmus/<order> at <commit>)". Each order keeps exactly one terminal entry.

## Harness guard (#64)

- **What a bridge can run.** `Config.Harnesses` lists the harnesses this bridge can run; nil means `DefaultHarnesses` (sdk, echo). `bmus bridge run` passes all of `bridge.Harnesses` only with `--holodeck-script`, the one setup where holodeck can start. `AddCrew` refuses any other harness with `bad_request` before writing anything, where it used to start a tour that failed provisioning until sickbay.
- **No drift with serve.** `bridge.Harnesses` stays the list `combadge serve --harness` accepts. A test runs the bundle's `serve --harness nope` and compares its "available:" list.

## Atomic log entries (#69)

Details beyond the issue. This supersedes "Captain's log entries are the one non-idempotent write" in `phase-1-decisions-2026-10-16.md` for every change to `crew`, `orders`, `hails` and `beams`.

- **No-ops log nothing.** `SetCrewStatus` and `UpdateCrewStatus` change only a row that differs, and `MarkHailDecided` repeating the same decision changes nothing. A step re-run that changes nothing writes no entry.
- **Two rows, one entry.** A change that moves the crew member with it commits both rows and one entry naming both: `ClaimOldestDirectedOrder` sets the crew member away, `InsertHail` sets them hailing (only from away), and `MarkHailDecided` and `DecideOpenHail` set them back away (only from hailing). A crew member in sickbay or dismissed is left alone (#43). `setMissionCrewStatus` and `store.UpdateCrewStatusIf` are gone; the #43 guard lives in those store methods.
- **Generated ids.** `InsertOrder` and `ClaimOldestDirectedOrder` set the entry's `order_id` to the order's id. `InsertHail` adds `hail_id` to the entry's payload.
- **The answer is logged by AnswerHail.** The decision, the crew member back away, the "hail <id> decided" entry and the message to the mission commit together. The mission's decide-hail finds it decided and writes nothing.
- **New entries.** Kind `crew`, for a crew member's own status: "crew member added: <name>" (AddCrew), "crew dismissed" (tour-end), and "crew back on deck" (settle-mission, with the order's id). Kind `beam`: "beam <alias> created", payload `{beam, expires_at}`, when provisioning records a new beam.

## Bridge status (#70)

- **RPC.** `bridge.status {}` returns `{condition, reasons, crew: {on_deck, away, hailing, sickbay}, orders: {open, away}, hails_open, beams_live, last_log_id}`. `Daemon.Status` computes it (`internal/bridge/status.go`), and the console's `status` event is to call the same method.
- **Condition.** `red` with anyone in sickbay, else `yellow` with anyone hailing, else `green`. `reasons` lists only the crew members that set the condition, by id and in id order: `"sorell in sickbay"`, `"sorell hailing"`. Green has `[]`.
- **Counters.** `crew` counts by status, and dismissed crew are not counted. `orders.open` is orders not yet claimed (`pool` or `directed`). `orders.away` is orders on a mission (`away` or `hailing`). `hails_open` counts hails with status `open`. `beams_live` counts crew members with a `current_beam` who are neither in sickbay nor dismissed. A crew member gets its beam once serve is up and keeps the alias in sickbay and when dismissed. `last_log_id` is the log's last id at the read, 0 for an empty log.
- **One read.** `store.ReadStatus` reads crew, order counts, open hails and the last log id in one read-only transaction. It opens with a deferred `BEGIN` and takes no write lock. Each change commits with its log entry (#69). The counters are therefore the state as of `last_log_id`.
- **CLI.** `bmus status` prints one line each for condition (with its reasons), crew, orders, hails, beams and log. `--json` prints the RPC result. A bridge that isn't running exits 3.

## Pump recycle fix (#81)

- **Cause.** `TestBusyTourClaimsAfterMission` lost ORD-0002's order, not its reconnect. In the failing CI run, the sickbay check saw stale heartbeats and a quiet connection (the bridge was busy settling ORD-0001 and claiming ORD-0002) while the beam answered its probe, so it recycled the live connection. The re-attach worked: `--since 79`, a new hello, fresh heartbeats. The `recycled=false` lines lasted under a second, while the new attach started. But `recycle()` SIGKILLed attach. The mission's `send-order` had just written the order to attach's stdin, and `Send` had returned nil. attach hadn't read the order yet, so it died with it. serve never saw the order, and the mission waited on `from-crew` until the test gave up. `TestPumpHolodeckRecycleDeliversWrittenCommand` reproduces it: it SIGSTOPs the real attach, sends, and recycles, and the order is lost every time. Without the stop, a recycle right after a `Send` lost the order in 2 of 10 runs.
- **Fix.** `recycle()` ends a live connection gracefully. It unpublishes the connection, so a `Send` after it fails with `ErrNotConnected` and its step retries on the next connection. Then it closes attach's stdin (`Session.Close`). attach passes on what it read, gets the EOF, and exits. The reader keeps recording lines until EOF. If attach hasn't exited `WriteTimeout` later (half-open, or not relaying), it is killed as before. An attach attempt that never sent a line is still killed at once (#38), because nothing can have been written to it. An attempt stops being one at its first line, when it becomes the live connection, rather than when `connectOnce` returns. This keeps the #38 attempt check from killing a connection that is ending gracefully.
- **Scope.** This doesn't change the Phase 1 rule that commands lost to a connection that really dropped aren't redelivered. It stops the pump's own recycle, which fires on a healthy beam, from losing commands it has already reported as sent. A write timeout still kills attach at once: attach isn't reading its stdin then, so closing it would deliver nothing.

## Serve's environment on a beam (#86)

- **Cause.** The first real-beam run (#19) failed its order with `Not logged in`. beam-init services start with `HOME`, `PATH` and `USER` only. `tsh beams exec` and SSH sessions also get the LLM proxy settings (`ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL`, `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN`, `CLAUDE_CODE_USE_MANTLE` and others), and the sdk harness's `claude` needs them.
- **Fix.** Before every `beamctl start` or `restart`, the tsh transporter runs `sh -c 'umask 077 && env -0 > /tmp/bmus/beam.env'` over `tsh beams exec`. serve starts with `--env-file /tmp/bmus/beam.env` and loads it before building the harness. A variable serve already has keeps its value, and the ones describing the capturing shell (`SSH_*`, `PWD`, `OLDPWD`, `SHLVL`, `_`, `MOTD_SHOWN`, `TERM`) are skipped. I'd rather copy the whole exec environment than name the proxy's variables: the list differs between harnesses (codex has its own) and will change with the beam image.
- **Captured once.** serve keeps the values from its last start or restart. If the proxy token turns out to be short-lived, a long-running serve will hold an expired one; #19 checks its lifetime.
- **Old services.** A combadge service that is already running is left alone, and `beamctl restart` reruns the original command. A beam whose service predates this fix keeps running without the environment until its service is pruned (`beamctl stop --prune combadge`) or the beam is replaced.

## Pushes from a beam are denied (#88)

- **Deny, don't ask.** This replaces the push policy's `ask` cases from #11 (a plain push, a subcommand built at run time, an alias or unknown subcommand near a push word), and D4's "route plain git push to a hail". A beam holds no push credentials and the bridge collects `bmus/<order>` (D6), so allowing the push could only fail. The PreToolUse hook now denies all of them. A real push, force or not, gets a reason telling the agent to commit on its order's branch and call `report`. A command `classifyGitPush` can't tell apart from a push (`git config push.autoSetupRemote true`, an alias) is a new `maybe` kind, denied with a reason that says so and suggests running it another way. The captain's push stays a Phase 2 bridge action.
- **What still hails.** AskUserQuestion becomes `clarifying_question` hails as before. The `tool_approval` path in `canUseTool` stays for any future ask, but no hook raises one today.
- **README.** The quick-start order body ("Walk the script.") is meant for holodeck. On #19 a real agent read the holodeck fixture and acted it out, including its push hail. The README now has a separate order example for real beams. The holodeck fixture's tool_approval hail now asks about `make deploy`, the shape the sdk harness would use for any future ask.

## Roster config (#95)

Details beyond the issue and `spec/phase-2-plan-2026-10-19.md` §2.

- **No divisions, no checks.** A roster without divisions (no file, an empty file, or no `divisions` key) means divisions aren't configured. `AddCrew` and `CreateOrder` then take any division, as in Phase 1, so existing homes and the README's `--division science` keep working. Once the roster names any division, every non-empty division must be one of them (`bad_request`). An empty division always passes.
- **Missing files.** The default `<home>/roster.yaml` may be absent. A path given with `--roster` must exist: a typo there shouldn't quietly mean an empty roster. An empty file is an empty roster.
- **Division names** match `^[A-Za-z][A-Za-z0-9_-]*$`. Negative transporter limits are an error, and a zero limit takes its default. `Run` checks `Config` the same way (`CheckRoster`), as it does `CheckRepos`.
- **The seed is logged.** The first `StandingOrders` read writes the roster's default to `settings` with a `bridge` entry, "standing orders on (roster default)" or "... off ...", like every other write (#69). `SetStandingOrders` logs "standing orders on" or "standing orders off", and nothing when the value doesn't change. A mutex in the daemon orders the seed and the toggle, so a seed can't overwrite the captain's choice.
- **New log kind** `bridge`, for bridge-wide settings.
- **Empty harness.** `crew add --harness` now defaults to empty rather than `sdk`. The crew row keeps `''`, `provision` passes no `--harness`, and serve runs sdk. `AddCrew` checks `sdk` against `Config.Harnesses` for an empty harness until #59 resolves the division's. Older databases keep `DEFAULT 'sdk'` on `crew.harness`, which is harmless: `InsertCrew` always writes the column.
- **Not yet:** `RouteOrder`'s division check (#101), waking divisions when standing orders turn on (#96), and the transporter limits' use (#97); the roster only loads and checks them for now.

## Transporter queue (#97)

Details beyond the issue, `spec/phase-2-plan-2026-10-19.md` §5 and the amendment's §3.

- **Retries are workflow steps.** `transporterWorkflow` runs `provision`, and on a failed Create (an outcome, `CreateFailed`, not a step error) sleeps with `dbos.Sleep` and runs `provision` again. Each attempt and each wait is recorded, so a restart resumes the wait where it was instead of starting the budget over, and the workflow branches only on recorded outcomes. DBOS step retries would restart their count in-process after a crash and can't later skip the minutes a login wait (#102) shouldn't count. Other `provision` failures (Exists, StartCombadge, the store) keep the step's own `provisionRetries`, as before.
- **Budget.** Waits of 1, 2, 4, then 5 minutes, while the waits stay within 30 minutes: eight attempts over 27 minutes of waiting. Only the waits count, not the time `beams add` itself takes. Every failed attempt logs `beam creation failed: <err>` (kind `beam`), so the captain sees why a crew member has no beam yet. When the budget runs out, the tour goes to sickbay as before: `provisioning failed: create beam: <err>`, `provision_failed`.
- **Waiting on the queue.** DBOS's handle to a queued child polls on a context its shutdown doesn't cancel, and one `GetResult` would hold up shutdown until the child ended. The tour calls `GetResult` in a loop with a short timeout. A timeout records nothing and takes no step id, so a replay sees only the one recorded result.
- **Queue config.** The queue's limits are stored in DBOS's system database. It is registered with `QueueConflictAlwaysUpdate`, so the roster's values replace the stored ones on every start.
- **Beam cap.** `AddCrew` counts crew members who aren't dismissed, sickbay included (a crew member in sickbay keeps its beam until re-energize or dismissal). The count and the insert share one transaction (`store.InsertCrewWithin`, `ErrRosterFull`), so two concurrent adds can't both take the last slot. The refusal is `bad_request`: "beam cap reached (transporter.max_beams N): dismiss a crew member or raise max_beams in the roster".
- **remove-previous** logs `beam <alias> removed`, or `beam <alias> not removed, left to its TTL: <err>` when Exists or Remove fails. It does nothing when the latest beam is this tour's (a re-run) or already gone.
- **Dismissed before a beam.** `provision` returns `Dismissed` without writing anything, and the tour ends `dismissed`. Its final `SetCrewStatus(on_deck)` is still unconditional: #100's dismissal must make it conditional, or a dismissal landing during provisioning is overwritten.
- **Old databases.** `store.Open` refuses a `bridge.db` whose `beams` lacks `tour_no`, like the inbox check from #22 (the checks share one list, `requiredColumns`).
- The transporter workflow keeps DBOS's default recovery-attempt limit: unlike a tour, it ends within the hour.
- **Only a recorded outcome ends the tour.** After a failed `GetResult`, the tour reads its own steps and the child's status outside any step. It acts (sickbay) only on an outcome `GetResult` recorded: the transporter's own error or its cancellation. It waits on while the child runs. An unrecorded failure on an ended child (a dead letter, a result that can't be read) is returned from the tour, which then ends in ERROR rather than recording a step where a replay expects the `GetResult`.
- **Queue slots.** A transporter waiting between Create retries keeps its queue slot (`max_provisioning`) for up to about 30 minutes. The rate limiter caps workflow starts on the queue, not individual Create retries.

## Stand down, recall and handover (#98)

Details beyond the issue and `spec/phase-2-plan-2026-10-19.md` §6.

- **Serve remembers stopped orders.** `state.json` gains `stopped`, the orders a stand_down or recall ended. Only an order on that list gets its dir cleared (everything under `<work-dir>/<order>/` but `in/`) before it runs again, and starting it takes it off the list. A resend of an order serve has no record of (lost state) still keeps an existing checkout, as `checkoutRepos` documents.
- **Errors during a stop stay resendable.** Relaying a stand_down or recall to the running order records it in `state.json` as `stopping`. A run that then ends in `error` (its checkpoint failed, say) emits the error as before but records the order `stopped`, not `completed`, and so does a restart: it emits `combadge_restarted` as today. The bridge parks or recalls such an order on its earlier checkpoints (§7) and may send it to this beam again, which must run it rather than answer "already done".
- **Which checkpoint stops.** The sdk harness marks `stopping` on the first checkpoint emitted after the command, whether the agent started it before or after. The fallback timer starts when the command arrives. A failed fallback checkpoint rejects the run with its error, so serve emits `error`.
- **Hails are withdrawn on a stop.** An agent held in `canUseTool` can't read the pushed request. Taking a stop denies the tool of every waiting hail, and of any later one without hailing, with "standing down: no answer is coming. Commit your work on bmus/<order> and call request_handover." A late answer is ignored as an unknown ref. The bridge closes the open hail when it parks.
- **Ignored stops.** A stand_down or recall is ignored once the outcome is decided: after the sdk query's first result (its input is closed; the report wins), after the holodeck walk reached its report, after a belay, or after an earlier stop. Once the sdk harness took a stop, a result closes the input only if the agent had called `report`: the report then wins (the fallback is cancelled and no stopping checkpoint goes out). Otherwise the input stays open and the run ends Stopped, by the agent's checkpoint or the fallback.
- **Belay during a stop.** In every harness, a belay that arrives before the stopping checkpoint is emitted wins: the run ends `belayed` and no stopping checkpoint goes out.
- **Checkpoint dirs.** Holodeck writes its stopping checkpoint to `<order>/<script length>`, an index no step has; echo to `<order>/1`; sdk to the next numbered dir, as `request_handover` does. Echo now takes `--work-dir` like the others.
- **Pushed message.** The stop request (only it, not the order prompt) goes into the session with `priority: "next"`, meant to reach the agent mid-turn rather than after it. The SDK doesn't document the field; if the message waits for the turn to end, the fallback covers it. Worth checking on the first real watch changes.

## Watch change, park and resume (#100)

Details beyond the issue and `spec/phase-2-plan-2026-10-19.md` sections 7 and 8.

- **Schema.** `checkpoints.beam` and `checkpoint_repos.beam` join the keys and are in `requiredColumns`. `orders.parked_at` is nullable, so it is an `addedColumns` entry instead: an older database gains it on `Open`.
- **Checkpoint order.** `ListCheckpoints` orders by `seq, rowid`. `ship-repos` walks `store.CheckpointsNewestFirst` (insertion order); `LatestCheckpoint` is its first row.
- **ship-repos from a checkpoint.** It doesn't fetch origin when a checkpoint lands; base_ref still does. A checkpoint whose bundle is missing from the cargo bay counts as rejected, like one the object check refuses. Each rejection logs `checkpoint <beam>-<seq> of repo <name> not shipped, trying an older start: <err>` (kind `order`, code `work_rejected`). Orders take one repo, so the handover is the note of the checkpoint the first repo shipped from. The shipped entry names its start: `(main)` or `(checkpoint <beam>-<seq>)`.
- **The backstop never fails a parked order.** `store.SettleAssignedOrder` requires the assignee and `parked_at IS NULL`. A parked order's mission is alive, so a mission that parks after a settle wait timed out keeps its order away for the next claim. Hails are closed only when the tour's crew member raised them.
- **Parking.**
  - A stand_down whose order never reached the beam (ship-repos found the tour ended) parks at once.
  - The stopping checkpoint parks the order (the `park` step) before its pull, and `mission_parked` goes out after the pull. A pull slower than the tour's waits then finds the order parked, not failed: the backstop leaves a parked order alone. If tour N+1 has meanwhile removed the old beam, the pull fails and the order resumes from the earlier checkpoints.
  - A stopping checkpoint parks even when its pull failed: the mission parks on the earlier checkpoints.
  - pull-checkpoint and pull-work copy from the beam ship-repos shipped to (recorded in its result), not `crew.current_beam`: next-tour clears that, and the next provision overwrites it, while a pull can still be running.
  - A park that finds the order already ended (backstopped) returns failed, code `unsettled`, reason `the order was <state> before its mission could park`.
  - Log texts: `order parked: <reason>` (`watch change`, `beam lost`, ...), `hail <id> closed unanswered: mission parked`, `order resumed on <crew>`.
- **StaleBeam.** In this issue only a failed `send-stand-down` sets it, on `mission_parked`. The watch-change wait ignores it: the next tour's `remove-previous` removes the beam. In the main loop a `mission_done` or `mission_parked` with StaleBeam (#101's recall and belay) ends the tour in sickbay, code `send_failed`.
- **Late mission_parked.** `mission_parked` carries the tour the mission parked under (`CrewSignal.TourID`). A tour ignores one naming another tour: a park from tour N that reaches tour N+1 after it resumed the order is not a recall.
- **Missing tour.** `mission_done` and `mission_parked` go through a `current-tour` step (`crew.tour_no`) and a workflow `dbos.Send`. Its `ErrNonExistentWorkflow` is recorded with the send, so a replay takes the same path. That error counts as delivered.
- **resume.** A resume is dropped when the mission isn't parked, its body doesn't decode, or `unpark` finds the order isn't away with the resume's crew member. `UnparkOrder` reads a re-run (already unparked, same assignee) as resumed.
- **Claim.** `ClaimOldestDirectedOrder`'s already-away branch sets an on-deck crew member away and logs `order away: mission started` again. `claimOrder` reads `parked_at` after the claim.
- **Watch change.**
  - A beam with a zero `ExpiresAt` never changes the watch.
  - Once the watch is due, `claim-order` claims nothing (`WatchDue`) and the tour changes the watch, rather than take an order only to stand it down.
  - A beam created with `ExpiresAt` less the lead already past is logged (kind `beam`, "beam X expires at T, within the watch-change lead L: its tour changes the watch at once"): its tours would rotate one beam after another.
  - The next tour is a child of the old one, started from its body under `tourWorkflowID`.
  - `next-tour` logs `watch change: tour N hands over to tour N+1` (kind `crew`).
  - `crew_error` during the stand-down wait is logged. `order_assigned` and stale signals are dropped.
  - `Config.watchChangeLead`, `standDownTimeout` and `settleTimeout` are unexported, for tests.
- **Dismissal is recorded first.** A signal alone was lost on several paths: a watch change's settle wait reads only mission signals, a tour ending in `tourSickbay` never reads its queue, and a send to a tour that has just ended succeeds unread.
  - `DismissCrew` now sets the crew member dismissed, logs it and sends the dismissed error to a parked order's mission, in one transaction. Then it signals a live tour so it ends promptly.
  - `dismissed` is terminal in the store: `SetCrewStatus`, `UpdateCrewStatus` and `NextTour` leave a dismissed crew member alone, and `ClaimOldestDirectedOrder` gives it nothing.
  - Every step that ends or hands over a tour reads the status: `tour-end`, `tour-sickbay` and `next-tour`. If dismissed, the tour ends dismissed, sends its running mission the dismissed error (one that parked after DismissCrew looked would keep its order) and removes its beam (`remove-beam`; with no beam given, the latest one this tour made). No next tour starts.
  - `DismissCrew` checks again after its send. If the tour has ended meanwhile, it removes the beam itself.
  - `provision` puts the crew member on deck only if it isn't dismissed (`SetCrewStatusUnlessDismissed`). Otherwise its outcome is `Dismissed` with the beam it made, which the tour removes.
  - Without a live tour, `DismissCrew` removes the crew member's latest `beams` row, whatever tour made it.
  - Dismissing a crew member already dismissed does nothing.
- **Re-energize.** A crew member not in sickbay is a `bad_request`, a dismissed one included. While tour `tour_no` is pending, which includes a sickbay tour still ending, the call does nothing. Its `NextTour` logs `crew re-energized: tour N` (kind `crew`).
- **RPC and CLI.** `crew.dismiss` and `crew.reenergize` take `{id}` and return `CrewActionResult`: the `CrewView` plus `outcome`.
  - `done`: complete.
  - `requested`: a tour finishes the job. The dismissed tour removes the beam; the re-energized one provisions.
  - `under_way`: an earlier re-energize is still going, and nothing was done.
  - `bmus crew dismiss <id>` and `bmus crew reenergize <id>` print a line per outcome ("dismissed; its tour is ending and will remove its beam", "re-energize ... requested"), or JSON with `--json`.
- **Missions** register with `WithMaxRecoveryAttempts(-1)`. The checkpoint entry's payload gains `beam` and `stopping`.
- **Holodeck.** The step log records the order's `handover` when it has one, so tests can see the resumed order command carry it. `HolodeckConfig.Lifetime` defaults to 24h.
- **Tests.** The watch-change test mints every beam from one Holodeck with a short `Lifetime` and extends the later beams' `ExpiresAt`. Two Holodecks would reuse a removed beam's alias. The restart variant holds the stopping checkpoint's pull (`CopyFrom`), so the bridge stops during the tour's stand-down wait.
- **Known limitations.**
  - `StopPump` is keyed only by crew member, so a stop meant for one tour can stop the next tour's pump; the window is a crash between next-tour and the new tour's start-pump, and is negligible.
  - A harness error already in flight when a stand_down lands parks the order (any error while parking does), so the order runs once more on the next beam.
  - A ship-repos re-run repeats its work_rejected entries, as it already repeats "repo shipped".
  - Holodeck aliases can repeat in tests when two Holodecks share a root.

## Dispatcher (#96)

Details beyond the issue, `spec/phase-2-plan-2026-10-19.md` sections 3, 4 and 12, and the amendment's section 2.

- **Directed before pool, whatever the priority.** `claimOrder` takes the crew member's directed orders first, as spec section 5 says, so a red pool order waits behind a routine directed one of the same crew member. Priority orders each claim on its own.
- **Wake-ups don't read the switch.** `CreateOrder` wakes the division for every pool order, and the tours' claims check standing orders. A pool order whose division has no on-deck crew member, or none at all, just waits. `SetStandingOrders(true)` wakes every crew member with a division even when standing orders were already on, so "on" doubles as a nudge. Crew members without a division have no pool and aren't woken.
- **Wire format.** `order.create` takes `priority` as the store's integer (0-2); only the order file has the names. `Daemon.CreateOrder` requires an assignee or a division and a priority of 0-2 (`bad_request`), so the RPC and the console (#78) share the checks; the order file checks the same before sending.
- **The pool claim keeps `parked_at`**, and logs the same `order away: mission started` as a directed claim. A recalled order (#101) is then resumed by whoever claims it, through `claimOrder`'s existing `Parked` read.
- **Red alert includes parked orders.** A recalled red order sits in the pool and still counts.
- **Test hook.** `Config.wakeSend` replaces `wakeDivision`'s send, so a test can lose wake-ups.
- **Upgrades mid-tour.** A tour already waiting when the bridge is upgraded keeps the old 24h idle wait for one cycle, because DBOS recorded that Recv's deadline. Wake-ups still reach it.
- **Red alerts that can't clear yet.** A red directed order whose assignee is dismissed, or a red pool order whose division has no crew, keeps the bridge red until #101 adds withdraw and route.
- **Reason order.** Red-alert reasons sort by order id as text (`ORD-%04d`), which misorders only past ORD-9999.
- **Cost of an idle re-check.** An empty claim runs a couple of short transactions and records a DBOS step row every 10 minutes per idle tour, about 600 rows per tour over a beam's lifetime. That is fine.

## Division defaults (#59)

Details beyond the issue and `spec/phase-2-plan-2026-10-19.md` §2.

- **Resolved, not stored.** `crew.harness` and `crew.model` keep only the crew member's own settings. `CrewView.harness` and `model` are the resolved values, from the roster the bridge runs with now, with `harness_source` and `model_source` (`crew`, `division` or `default`; with `default` the value is empty). A tour keeps the harness it was provisioned with, so after a roster change `crew list` may show the next tour's harness. `resolveModel` also returns `order`, which a crew view never shows.
- **No opting out.** A crew member in a division with a default can't ask for serve's default harness or the harness's default model except by naming them.
- **A division that left the roster** gives no defaults: its crew members resolve to `default`.
- **Roster check.** `CheckRoster` refuses a division harness that isn't one of `bridge.Harnesses` (a typo). One this bridge can't run is allowed: the division may have no crew members here.
- **Unrunnable at provisioning.** A roster changed at a restart, or a bridge restarted without `--holodeck-script`, can leave a crew member with a resolved harness this bridge can't run. `provision` checks it (`checkHarness`, as `AddCrew` does) before making a beam, and returns it as the outcome's `Failed`: the tour goes to sickbay at once with `provision_failed`, "provisioning failed: this bridge can't run harness "holodeck", division ops's default (it runs sdk, echo)...", rather than retrying something that can't succeed. The captain fixes the roster or flags, restarts, and re-energizes. A tour already on its beam is untouched until its next provisioning. `ReenergizeCrew` makes the same check before starting a tour and returns `AddCrew`'s `bad_request`, so the captain sees it at once rather than a tour that goes straight back to sickbay.
- **Crash mid-provision, then a roster edit.** The re-run `provision` resolves against the new roster: it may now refuse the harness and leave the beam it already made to its TTL (until the next tour's remove-previous), or reuse a beam whose serve still runs the previous harness until the next tour. Nothing branches on which harness a beam runs, so either is harmless.
- **The model resolves on every send-order**, so a resumed order (#100) runs on the new crew member's model or division's, unless the order names one.
- **AddCrew order of checks.** The division is checked before the harness, so an unknown division is reported as such rather than through a default it doesn't have.
- **CLI.** `crew list` gains `HARNESS` and `MODEL` columns, each `<value> (<source>)`: `echo (division)`, `m2 (crew)`, `sdk (default)`, `- (default)` for the harness's own model.

## Recall, belay, withdraw and route (#101)

Details beyond the issue and `spec/phase-2-plan-2026-10-19.md` sections 3, 7 and 9.

- **Requests commit with their check.** `BelayOrder` and `RecallOrder` read the order and its assignee's status, log `belay requested[: <reason>]` or `recall requested[: <reason>]` and send the mission its synthetic message in one transaction (`store.RequestOrder`). A refused request logs and sends nothing. The synthetic `belay` and `recall` name no crew member. The pump forwards only `hail`, `checkpoint_ready`, `report` and `error` from a beam, so a beam can't fake them.
- **What each takes.**
  - Belay takes `away`, `hailing`, and an unclaimed order with `parked_at` set: a recalled order in the pool, or one routed to a crew member since. An unclaimed order without a parked mission is refused with a pointer to withdraw.
  - Recall checks the state first ("too late" beats "no division"). An unclaimed order gets a pointer to route. An order without a division gets a pointer to `bmus order belay` and to `bmus order route --division`, which only takes unclaimed orders. A parked order whose crew member was dismissed is refused as "being failed": the dismissal has sent its mission the dismissed error.
  - Withdraw and route take only `pool` and `directed`. Route keeps `parked_at`, so a routed recalled order is resumed by its next claimer.
- **Route.** An absent assignee or division keeps the order's, and an empty one clears it, both resolved against the row inside `store.RouteOrder`'s transaction. The order must keep one of them. `bmus order route <id> --assignee ""` returns a directed order to its division's pool. A kept assignee is checked like a new one, so moving an order directed to a crew member in sickbay to a division takes `--assignee ""` as well as `--division`. A route that changes nothing writes and logs nothing. The assignee's tour is signalled, or the division woken, best effort as in `CreateOrder`.
- **Recall in the mission.**
  - The stopping checkpoint runs `recall-order` before its pull, as `park` does (#100), and `mission_parked` goes out after the pull. Other crew members may claim the order during the pull. Their `resume` waits behind the pull, so the claim resumes from that checkpoint.
  - A recall that arrives while a stand_down is pending sends nothing: the beam ignores a second stop (#98), and the pending stop's checkpoint recalls instead. A stand_down while recalling is dropped.
  - Any error while recalling, `beam_lost` included, recalls on the existing checkpoints.
  - `recall-order` re-runs `wakeDivision`, which is harmless. If the order had ended first (the backstop), the mission returns failed `unsettled`, as `park` does.
- **Dead missions** (review). A recalled mission that fails through `bridge_error` settles its order failed from the pool as well (`store.SettleOpenOrder`, the predicate belay uses). `settle-mission`, which runs only once the mission is over (after its `mission_done`, or when `mission-alive` reads it ended), now fails a parked order too (`SettleAssignedOrder`'s `evenParked`). Before, a recalled order whose mission died would be claimed and resumed into a dead workflow every re-check. The tour's other backstops (`tour-end`, `next-tour`) still leave a parked order alone: its mission may be alive.
- **A resume that a recall beat.** `RecallOrder` reads the crew member in sickbay. A re-energized tour can still claim the parked order before the mission reads the recall. Its `resume` then finds the order back in the pool, and its `mission_parked` names the old tour. The mission therefore sends `mission_parked` to the resuming tour (named in the `resume`) whenever `unpark` refuses one, and that tour goes back on deck. Without this, the tour would hold the order until a later claim elsewhere. A running mission that drops a `resume` naming a tour other than its own sends `mission_parked` to that tour too, which releases a tour whose `resume` lost a race.
- **Belay in the mission.** `order-belayed` closes an open hail (`closed unanswered: order belayed`) and settles the order in one step. Then `send-belay` goes out only when the order is on a beam and the mission isn't parked. `MissionResult` gains status `belayed` and `StaleBeam`, which `mission_done` carries. An order that had already ended returns failed `unsettled`.
  - **The mission waits for the run to end** (review). serve holds a belay while it checks the order's repos out, so the run can end after the bridge has moved on. A mission that returned right after `send-belay` freed its tour, the tour sent its next order, and serve refused it as `busy`. After `send-belay` the mission now waits, up to `belayTimeout` (10 minutes, a recorded deadline), for any of: the run's error or report, a stopping checkpoint (a stop that won the race, after which serve drops the belay), or the tour's `beam_lost` or `dismissed`. Past the deadline the result is `StaleBeam`, and the tour goes to sickbay. I fixed this on the bridge rather than in serve. Queueing the next order in serve would need new restart state, or a queued order would be lost when serve restarts. The wait also covers any slow end of a belayed run, and it reuses the tour's `StaleBeam` path.
  - A mission with no crew member tells the tour of the assignee `order-belayed` found. That is a recalled order claimed whose `resume` it hasn't read, so the claimer goes back on deck. A recalled order still in the pool tells no tour.
- **Earlier open items.** The red alerts that #96 couldn't clear now can be: withdraw or belay the order, or route it to a crew member or a division that has crew. `RouteOrder` checks the division against the roster, the check #95 left to this issue.
- **Log texts** (kind `order`): `order recalled: <reason>` (default reason `recall`), `order belayed[: <reason>]` (the order's one terminal entry), `order withdrawn[: <reason>]`, `order routed to <crew>` or `order routed to the <division> pool`, and `hail <id> closed unanswered: order recalled`.
- **RPC and CLI.** `order.belay`, `order.recall` and `order.withdraw` take `{id, reason?}`, and `order.route` takes `{id, assignee?, division?}`. Each returns the `OrderView` as it is once the request is queued. The CLI prints one line per command, or JSON with `--json`.
- **Known limitations.**
  - A belay or recall in the window between `claim-order` and the mission's start fails as an internal error (no mission to send to). Retrying works.
  - `TestBelayAfterRecall` doesn't pin whether the mission reads the belay while recalling or after parking. `TestBelayOrder` covers the parked case.

## Bridge push (#99)

Details beyond the issue and `spec/phase-2-plan-2026-10-19.md` section 10.

- **Where the commit comes from.** `PushOrder` reads the order's newest `done` entry (`store.LastOrderLog`) and pushes the repos of its payload, in order. No new table: the payload already records each repo's commit and fetch outcome.
- **Refusals first.** Before anything is pushed, every repo must be registered, have no `fetch_error` or `reject_error` (the refusal names the bundle in the cargo bay), and have `bmus/<order>` in the clone at the recorded commit. That check runs without the lock, so a multi-repo push doesn't push some repos and then find a later one moved. Each push checks again under the repo's lock. Refusals are `bad_request` and log nothing.
- **Many repos.** Each is pushed in turn, and the first failure stops the push. The error names the repos pushed before it. Phase 1 orders have one.
- **What is pushed.** `git push --no-follow-tags --no-recurse-submodules [--force] <registered URL> <commit>:refs/heads/<target>`. The source is the verified commit, not the branch, so nothing can move between the check and the push. The destination is fully qualified, so a target named like a remote tag isn't ambiguous and no tag is created. `--no-follow-tags` matters: the clone mirrors upstream's tags, and the captain's `push.followTags` would otherwise send annotated tags reachable from the work. The URL is passed directly, not `origin`, so `remote.origin.pushurl` can't redirect it; the captain's `url.*.pushInsteadOf` still applies. The bridge puts the URL in no message of its own; git's output, which it relays, hides credentials.
- **`--force` overwrites unconditionally**, as section 10 says: plain `--force`, not `--force-with-lease`. The bridge's clone doesn't track the target, so a lease would have nothing to compare against.
- **Target names.** Refused when starting with `-`, equal to `HEAD` or `@`, or failing `git check-ref-format refs/heads/<name>`. Not `--branch`, which expands `@{-1}` in whatever repository the bridge was started from.
- **Rejected pushes** (git's `! [rejected]`: not a fast-forward, or the remote has work the clone lacks) are `ErrPushRejected`, RPC code `conflict`, and the CLI adds that `--force` overwrites the target. Other push failures are `internal`, carrying git's message.
- **Log.** Kind `order`, one entry per repo pushed or failed: `repo <name> pushed to <target> at <12-char commit>[ (forced)]` or `repo <name>: push to <target> at <commit> failed`, payload `{repo, target, commit, force, reason?}` with git's error as `reason`. The entry is written even when the caller's context ended, since the push may have reached the server. When the success entry can't be written, the repo still counts as pushed: it is in the returned list and the error says it was pushed, with the commit.
- **Timeout.** `order.push` gets a 10-minute handler timeout instead of 30 seconds (`rpcHandlerTimeouts`), and `Call`'s deadline follows it. `PushOrder` bounds itself the same way for other callers.

## Follow-ups (#110, #113)

Dated 2026-10-23. Each amends the section named.

- **Transporter queue (#97): an unrecorded failure is retried, then sickbay (#110).** After an unrecorded failure on an ended child, or a failed read of the child's status (`childOutcome`), `awaitBeam` calls `GetResult` again, a poll interval later, up to `awaitEndedRetries` (4) times. Like the other retries, these record nothing. If it then gives up, the tour puts its crew member in sickbay (`awaiting its beam failed: <err>`, code `bridge_error`). It does this outside any step, because an ERROR tour is never replayed. Then it returns the error and ends in ERROR, as before. Re-energize (#100) starts the next tour. A crew member dismissed meanwhile stays dismissed, and `DismissCrew` removes the beam once it finds the tour ended. `attachCrewTours` still only warns about a tour in ERROR. Sickbay is written before the tour ends, so a tour that could end in ERROR this way has already moved its crew member. The one gap: if DBOS can't record the ERROR status, the tour stays PENDING and is replayed at the next start. That run can then find its beam with its crew member in sickbay. The next watch change puts the crew member back on deck. `Config.getResultFault` injects the failure in tests.
- **Watch change, park and resume (#100): the dismissal races (#113).**
  - `store.DismissCrew` returns the `tour_no` that its `UPDATE ... RETURNING` read, and `DismissCrew` signals that tour. It no longer reads the crew row first, so a `NextTour` that commits just before the dismissal can't make it signal the old tour. A missing crew member is still `not_found`.
  - A tour can end without removing its beam: its terminal step (`tour-end`, `tour-sickbay`, `next-tour`) read the crew member before the dismissal committed, but the tour still read live. After its signal, `DismissCrew` now waits up to `dismissWait` (3 seconds) for the tour to end, then reads its result once it has. If the tour ended dismissed, it removed the beam, and the outcome is `requested` as before. If it ended otherwise (sickbay, watch change, ERROR), `DismissCrew` removes the beam itself (`done`). If it is still running after the wait, it reads the signal or the dismissal in its last step (`requested`). A busy tour can make the call take up to those 3 seconds longer.
  - `store.NextTour` reports whether it changed the row. If it didn't, `next-tour` reads the status again, and a dismissed crew member ends the tour dismissed. This covers a dismissal between the step's dismissed read and its `NextTour`, so no tour N+1 starts. `ReenergizeCrew` ignores the flag. A dismissal landing there starts a tour that provisions nothing.
  - Test hooks: `Config.dismissHook` runs just before `DismissCrew`'s transaction, and `nextTourHook` runs inside `next-tour`.
- **Recall, belay, withdraw and route (#101): a dead recalled mission tells its claimer (#113).** The `order-failed` step of a mission with no crew member now records the assignee that `SettleOpenOrder` returned. The step's output is a struct, so the empty output recorded by earlier versions still decodes. On `bridge_error`, the mission sends that crew member's tour `mission_done`, as `belayMission` does. That claimer, which claimed the order but whose `resume` the mission never read, goes back on deck at once, without waiting for `mission-alive`. An order routed to a crew member but still unclaimed makes the signal stale, and that tour ignores it.
- **Review of the follow-ups (#110, #113).**
  - **A spent step id isn't retried.** In DBOS v1.7.0, `GetResult` takes the child's step id (`workflowState.nextStepID()`) and then writes the result with `RecordOperationResult`. If that write fails, the id is used up and nothing is stored, and the error is a `WorkflowExecutionError` ("recording child workflow result"). A retry would record `DBOS.getResult` one step later than a replay would, and the replay would end in ERROR at `start-pump` with the crew member on deck. `stepIDSpent` recognises that error code, and `awaitBeam` gives up on it at once, which leads to sickbay. Other unrecorded failures return before any step id is taken: a timeout, a dead letter, a result that doesn't decode, the await's own database errors, and a failed `childOutcome` read. Those are still retried. A few other `WorkflowExecutionError`s (an invalid context, a result that won't serialize) take no step id but always fail the same way, so giving up on them loses nothing.
  - **awaitFailed removes a dismissed crew member's beam.** The retries can take about 4 seconds, longer than the 3 seconds `DismissCrew` waits. So `awaitFailed` checks for a dismissal after its sickbay write and removes the tour's latest beam, best effort.
  - **Gaps, in full.** A tour that gives up writes sickbay outside any step and then returns its error.
