There are two ways it can then stay PENDING instead of ending in ERROR: DBOS fails to record the ERROR status, or DBOS shuts down after the `ctx.Err()` check and the sickbay write. Until the next start, `ReenergizeCrew` answers `under_way`. At that start the tour is replayed. Its `GetResult` can then succeed, so a healthy tour runs for a crew member in sickbay. Its first directed claim moves the crew member away, and the next watch change puts it back on deck.
  - **DismissCrew's cost.** The up-to-3-second wait applies to any signalled tour that doesn't end within it. Examples are a tour waiting on its transporter, or one settling a running mission.

## tsh login expiry (#102)

Dated 2026-10-23. Details beyond the issue and `spec/phase-2-plan-amendment-2026-10-19.md` §1.

- **How tsh prompts.** Read from gravitational/teleport at master (1283425b, August 2026):
  - The password and OTP prompts (`AskPassword`, `AskOTP` in `lib/client/api.go`) check that stdin is a terminal first, and `prompt.ContextReader.ReadPassword` refuses a non-terminal (`ErrNotTerminal`). `RetryWithRelogin` then gives up and returns the original error. tsh reads `/dev/tty` instead of stdin only for `git_ssh`.
  - So with stdin a pipe or `/dev/null`, tsh can't take a password, and attach's commands can never be read as one. The fake that reads stdin in the tests is the worst case, not tsh.
  - Nothing turns re-login off for a user login: `tc.NonInteractive` is set only with an identity file. An SSO connector (OIDC, SAML, GitHub) or passwordless login starts a browser without needing a terminal. The gate is what stops that: with the login expired, no `tsh beams` call starts.
  - tsh now runs in its own session (`Setsid`), so it has no controlling terminal and can't open `/dev/tty`. A side effect: Ctrl-C in the bridge's terminal no longer reaches tsh. The bridge's shutdown kills it through the call's context, as before.
- **`tsh status --format=json`.** `{"active": {..., "valid_until": "<RFC 3339>"}, "profiles": [...]}` (`serializeProfiles`, `profileInfo` in `tool/tsh/common/tsh.go`).
  - An expired profile still prints the JSON, then exits 1 ("Active profile expired."): the past `valid_until` is what counts.
  - "Not logged in." on stderr, or JSON with `profiles` but no `active`, is `ErrLoginExpired`: there is no login.
  - Text around the JSON object is ignored. Anything else is "unknown": an object with neither key, or an active profile without `valid_until` (or with `null`). An unknown status leaves the gate as it was, so a tsh whose output changed can't stop beam work. The gate fails open: it starts as "valid", and a status that never parses keeps it so. The daemon logs a warning each minute, and after two failed reads in a row the status says `tsh login unknown: <err>` (the error's first line, at most 120 bytes).
  - Only an explicit zero `valid_until` never expires, as in tsh.
  - Not checked on a real cluster yet (TODO in `tshStatus`). `--client` would skip the cluster-alerts request a valid login makes, but it isn't used, because it isn't known which tsh versions have it.
- **Expiry margin.** A login counts as expired `transporter.LoginExpiryMargin` (2 minutes) before its `valid_until`, in the gate and in the daemon's state, for clock skew between the bridge and the cluster and for calls admitted just before the expiry. The status counts the minutes to `valid_until` itself, so it goes from `expires in 3m` to `expired`.
- **The gate.** `Tsh` keeps the last status. A known `valid_until` takes effect the moment it passes (less the margin), without another status. A new login is seen at the next read: at most a minute later, or sooner because `LoginValidUntil` (the daemon's minute check) updates the same state.
  - After a failed call, status is read again on a detached context bounded by 10 seconds, unless a read that started after the call is newer. A call killed by its own timeout may have been waiting on a login.
  - `Exec` reads status right before it starts, because its stdin is the bridge's command pipe. With a pump reconnecting, that is one status per attach attempt.
  - `loginMu` is held while status runs, so concurrent calls wait for one read.
- **Status.** The login's reason sets yellow and comes last. Unlike other yellow causes, it is listed when the bridge is red too (the condition stays red), so a sickbay can't hide an expired login. Minutes are rounded up: 9m59s reads `expires in 10m`.
- **Claims.** `claimOrder` claims nothing while the daemon's login state is expired. The check is inside the claim-order step, so it is recorded. An idle tour would otherwise claim, its ship-repos would fail about 30 seconds later (`repo_failed`), and it would claim the next order: a long expiry would drain the pool into failed orders. When the daemon sees the login valid again, it signals every crew member on deck to claim (`wakeOnDeck`).
- **Provisioning.**
  - `provision` maps `ErrLoginExpired` from `Exists`, `Create` or `StartCombadge` to the recorded outcome `LoginExpired`. `transporterWorkflow` then sleeps `loginWait` (1 minute) with `dbos.Sleep` and tries again.
  - Whether to log is decided by the workflow from the previous recorded outcome (`waitingLogin`), so a replay or a restart doesn't log again. The entry is kind `beam`, with payload `tour_no` and `error`.
  - The waits don't count against #97's budget and don't advance its backoff. A dismissal during the wait ends it at the next attempt (`Dismissed`).
  - The transporter workflow keeps its queue slot (`max_provisioning`) while it waits. Other provisions would wait on the login too.
  - Each minute of waiting records about two steps (a provision attempt and a `DBOS.sleep`), so an overnight wait adds about a thousand rows to the transporter workflow's history.
- **Mission beam steps.** An attach opened before the expiry can keep delivering, since Teleport keeps established sessions past the certificate's expiry. So a report or checkpoint can arrive while every new tsh call is gated. ship-repos, pull-checkpoint and pull-work then wait instead of failing (`loginWaiter`):
  - After the step fails (its own retries, about 30 seconds), a recorded `login-expired` step reads the login afresh (`checkLogin`), so a login lost before its `valid_until` (a `tsh logout`) isn't missed for up to a minute. If the login is expired, or the daemon saw it expired since the failed attempt started, the workflow sleeps `loginWait` and runs the step again. Otherwise the failure stands, as before.
  - A round that finds the login valid again after a round that waited runs the step once more: the attempt may have failed on the old login with nothing reading the login meanwhile. Only if that attempt fails too does the failure stand. Without this, a login renewed during the step's retries failed the order in about 3 of 20 test runs.
  - The workflow branches only on that recorded result, never on the step's error. The `login-expired` step logs `<step> waits for a tsh login` (kind `order`, payload `step`) unless the previous check of this wait was already expired, so once per wait.
  - Each round of the wait is the step's retries, the check and a sleep: about 90 seconds and three recorded steps.
  - The step waits whatever made it fail, as long as the login is expired then. A failure for another reason during an expiry is retried after the login comes back.
  - While a mission waits in one of these steps, it reads no messages; they queue for it.
  - Missions in flight across the upgrade: the new `login-expired` step only runs after a step failed. A mission that recorded such a failure under the old code and then took its fail path (for example an `order-failed` step) but is still PENDING would replay with `login-expired` where its history has the old next step. DBOS v1.7.0 refuses that with `ErrorCodeUnexpectedStep`, and the mission ends in ERROR. The window is a bridge restart in the middle of an order failing. Every other mission replays as before, because its recorded steps succeeded.
- **Known limitations.**
  - Apart from beam removal (next item), no other step calls the transporter. Commands go through the pump, which backs off on its own.
  - A watch change or a dismissal during a mission's login wait doesn't interrupt the wait. The tour's stand-down and settle waits run out first, and the backstop fails the order after `standDownTimeout` plus `missionSettleTimeout` (30 + 5 minutes). If the wait then ends and the beam has outlived the expiry, the work lands late: it is kept in the cargo bay and the bridge's clone and logged as late (`lateText`), like any work that arrives after its order ended.
  - A belay or recall sent during a ship-repos login wait takes effect only once the login is back and the order has been sent. #101's belay wait then applies.
  - A login renewed within a minute of expiring, while the daemon's check wasn't looking, can still fail a step that had only that one round. The `since` rule above covers it only when the daemon saw the expiry.
  - `removeBeam` (remove-previous, a dismissal) logs `beam <alias> not removed, left to its TTL: ... tsh login expired` and moves on. The beam counts against the tenant's cap until its TTL.

## Console address and flags (#71)

Dated 2026-10-28. Supersedes the address and `bmus console` parts of `console-plan-2026-10-18.md` (Shape, Security).

- **Address.** The console binds 127.0.0.1 only, on a free port by default. `bmus bridge run --console-port <n>` sets the port, checked against 0-65535 before anything is written. `--no-console` runs the bridge without the console. `console.Listen` refuses any host but 127.0.0.1. Host must be `127.0.0.1:<port>` or `localhost:<port>`; an Origin, when present, must be one of the same two. The Host check covers every path, not only `/api/`.
- **Bind before launch.** `Run` binds the console after the config checks and the lock, before it opens the store and launches DBOS, and serves it once the tours re-attach, before the RPC socket starts. A taken port fails the start before any workflow resumes.
- **`bmus console`.** `bridge.status` gains `console`, the resolved address, `""` with `--no-console`. `bmus console` takes the address from it (no `--addr`), fails with "the console is off" without one, and exits 3 when the bridge is down.
- **Token file.** Removed right after the lock is taken, with or without the console, and when the console closes. Written to a temp file and renamed into place when serving starts. `bridge.status` reports the address only after that. `bmus console` never prints a stale token, and refuses an empty file.
- **Mutations and Origin.** Every method but GET and HEAD is a mutation and needs Origin and CSRF. A request carrying an Origin other than the console's own is refused, reads included.
- **Cookie.** Named `bmus_console_<port>`: two bridges on one machine keep separate sessions. At most 64 sessions are kept; a new one evicts the oldest.
- **Accepted limitations, for the Phase 4 security review.**
  - Cookies ignore ports, and there is no TLS: another local service on a different 127.0.0.1 port receives the session cookie.
  - `bmus console` passes the token to `xdg-open` or `open` as an argument, readable by other local users in the process list.

## Console frontend scaffold (#74)

Dated 2026-10-28. Fills in the "Frontend" section of `console-plan-2026-10-18.md`.

- **Fonts.** Taken from the `@fontsource` npm packages (devDependencies), latin subset, weights 400 and 700. esbuild copies the woff2 files into `internal/console/assets/fonts/` for the `url()`s in `app.css`; the build copies each package's license next to them. Syncopate is Apache-2.0, not OFL; its license is vendored as shipped.
- **Deterministic build.** No content hashes in file names, no source maps. The build empties `internal/console/assets/` (keeping `.gitkeep`) before writing. The CI `web` job fails when `git status --porcelain` on that directory is not empty after `npm run build`.
- **Missing build.** `go:embed all:assets` with a committed `.gitkeep`, so the embed compiles with no build. With no `index.html` every non-API path returns 503 and a page saying how to build. `console.Config.Assets` overrides the embedded build (tests).
- **Serving.** Non-API paths serve GET and HEAD only, through the same Host, Origin and header checks. Directory listings and dotfiles are 404s. Assets and the missing-build page carry `Cache-Control: no-cache` (file names have no hash). Every response carries `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`.
- **Licenses.** The bundle strips legal comments; the build copies Preact's MIT license to `assets/preact-LICENSE.txt`.
- **Session exchange.** The page removes the `#t=` fragment with `history.replaceState` before posting it, so a failed exchange still leaves no token in the URL or history. The CSRF token lives in page memory: a reload keeps the cookie but loses the CSRF token. Mutations (#73) will need to re-obtain it; left open.
- **Store.** One reducer over `crew`, `order` and `hail` maps keyed by id: `snapshot` replaces all three, `upsert` and `remove` touch one entity. Its actions are not the wire format; the feed client (#72) maps events onto them.

## Console change feed (#72)

Dated 2026-10-28. Fills in "Live data" and "HTTP API" (reads) of `console-plan-2026-10-18.md`.

- **Hook.** `store.SetLogHook(ctx, fn, onErr)`. After each successful commit the store reads `log WHERE id > <last handed over>` and passes the result to `fn`, in the committing goroutine, one call at a time. Entries aren't collected inside the transaction. SQLite's single writer, with the write lock taken at `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`, gives log ids in commit order. A rollback hands nothing over.
  - `appendLog` bumps a counter. `withTx` reads the log after its commit only if the counter moved while it held the write lock. `AppendLog` always reads.
  - A failed read goes to `onErr` (the daemon logs it at Warn) and leaves its entries to the next commit that appends. Open streams also catch up at their heartbeat.
  - The hook is set only with the console on.
- **Feed.** `store.Feed` with `Subscribe`, `Publish` and `Subscription.C()`. Each subscriber buffers 256 entries. A commit's batch that doesn't fit drops the subscriber and closes its channel after what it already had.
- **Dependency direction.** `internal/console` defines `Source` (`Subscribe`, `Snapshot`, `Status`, `LastLogID`, `Log`, `LogBefore`, `LogView`, `Views`), and `Server.Serve(src)` takes it. The bridge implements it with `consoleSource` (`internal/bridge/console.go`). The console imports `internal/store` for `LogEntry` and `Feed` only, and never reads the store. Without a Source the data endpoints answer 503 `unavailable`.
- **Views.** `CrewView`, `OrderView`, `HailView` and `LogEntryView`, with their constructors, moved to `internal/bridge/views.go`. Their JSON is unchanged, except that a log payload that isn't valid JSON is now `null`, in `log.tail` too. Before, such a row failed the whole reply.
- **Snapshot.** `{last_log_id, crew, orders, hails, status, log}`. `crew` is every crew member, dismissed ones included. `orders` is pool, directed, away and hailing. `hails` is open hails. `status` is `bridge.status`'s result. `log` is the last 200 entries (`console.SnapshotLog`) up to `last_log_id`, oldest first. `last_log_id` is read first and the rest after it. The views can be newer than it. The events of those later entries still follow, and resending a whole view does no harm.
- **Events.**
  - A stream opens with `retry: 1000`.
  - `snapshot` and `log` carry the log id as the SSE `id`. A snapshot of an empty log sends an empty `id`, which resets the client's.
  - `crew`, `order` and `hail` follow their `log` event, in that order, with no id. They are named by `crew_id`, `order_id` and the payload's `hail_id`, and an entity that is gone is skipped.
  - Writes are flushed after the snapshot, after each replay page and after each burst of live entries.
  - Every 15s the stream checks the log's last id and catches up through `Log` if it is ahead of the last id sent. It then checks the status and sends a `: heartbeat` comment.
- **Status.** Sent when `bridge.status` changes in anything but `last_log_id`. It is checked after the snapshot or replay, after each burst of live entries, and at each heartbeat. The heartbeat check catches tsh login changes, which log nothing. A resumed stream always gets one.
- **Resume.** `Last-Event-ID` must be a non-negative integer, else 400 `bad_request`. An id past the log's end, or more than 10,000 entries behind it, gets a snapshot. Otherwise the stream replays in pages of 1000. The stream subscribes before it reads, and skips live entries at or below the last id sent. A dropped subscriber's stream ends, and the browser reconnects with `Last-Event-ID`.
- **First read.** The snapshot, or the first replay page, is read before the response starts. Its failure is a 500 `internal`.
- **Write deadline.** Each write and flush gets a deadline 30s away; a write that can't complete by then ends the stream. A stream ended that way, or by a read or encoding error, is logged at Warn. A client that disconnects (a cancel, `EPIPE`, `ECONNRESET`) or the server closing is a normal end, logged at Debug.
- **`/api/log`.** `since=<id>` returns the entries above it, as `log.tail` does. `before=<id>` returns the newest entries below it, exclusive, for scrolling back. Both are oldest first, and passing both is a 400. `limit` defaults to 500 and is clamped to 1000. A negative or non-integer value is a 400. The body is `{entries, last_id}`. `last_id` is the newest entry's id, or `since` when there are none (0 with `before`).
- **Routing.** The data routes sit on an inner mux behind the Origin, session and CSRF chain. A wrong method gets 405 only with a valid session.
- **Shutdown.** `Server.Close` first cancels a server-scoped context that every stream watches, then calls `Shutdown`. Open streams don't hold `Close` to its 5s fallback. `Run` sets the console address on the daemon before `Serve`, because console handlers read it through `Status`.

## Console actions (#73)

Dated 2026-10-28. Fills in "HTTP API" (actions) of `console-plan-2026-10-18.md` and settles the CSRF-after-reload question left by #74.

- **Endpoints.** `POST /api/orders` (order.create), `POST /api/hails/{id}/answer` (hails.answer), `POST /api/crew` (crew.add). They sit on the inner `/api/` mux, behind Origin, session and CSRF like every other `/api/` route.
- **Interface.** `internal/console` defines `Actions` (`CreateOrder`, `AnswerHail`, `AddCrew`), embedded in `Source`, so `Serve(src)` still takes one value and a nil Source answers 503 `unavailable`. Each method takes the request body as raw JSON. The bridge (`consoleSource`) decodes it with the RPC's `decodeArgs` into the RPC's args types, unknown fields refused, and calls the same `Daemon.addCrew`, `createOrder` and `answerHail` the RPC handlers now call. Validation and defaults (e.g. `created_by: captain`) live there once. The console never sees the args types and still doesn't import `internal/bridge`.
- **Hail id.** `{id}` is the hail's id, checked as `hail_id` is. The body takes `option` and `decision` only; a `hail_id` in it is a 400.
- **Errors.** The bridge returns `*rpcError`, which gains `Code()`. The console reads the code through an interface (`Code() string`) and answers `{"error": {"code", "message"}}` with the error's message, as the RPC does, for every status but 500. A 500 (code `internal`, a code with no status, or no code) is logged and answered `internal` with `<action> failed`, as #72's read errors are: raw SQL, git and path errors never reach the browser. A `shutting_down` error is logged and answered with the fixed text `bridge is shutting down`: it wraps whatever the shutdown cut short. `codedError(ctx, err)` is the one mapping from a Daemon error to a code for both the RPC server and the console, including the "bridge is shutting down" message.
- **Statuses.** `bad_request` 400, `forbidden` 403, `not_found` 404, `exists`, `hail_closed`, `conflict` and `unavailable` 409, `shutting_down` 503, `internal` and any code not listed 500. The bridge's `unavailable` only means an assignee in sickbay or dismissed. The console's own `unavailable` (no Source) stays a 503.
- **Results.** Crew: 201 and the `CrewView` crew.add returns. Hail answer: 200 and the decided `HailView` hails.answer returns. Order: 201 and the new order's `OrderView`, the shape order.list and the order actions return; order.create's RPC result stays `{id}`. The console reads the order back after creating it: a failed read-back reports an error although the order exists, and the feed still delivers it.
- **Context.** An action runs as an RPC handler does: refused with `shutting_down` once the daemon's context ends, on that context with the 30s handler timeout. The request's context isn't used, so a closed tab doesn't cut an action short.
- **Shutdown.** `Server.Close` doesn't wait for a handler past its 5s. `Run` tracks in-flight actions and, after closing the console and the RPC socket, refuses new ones and waits for those running before it stops the pumps and DBOS.
- **Not optimistic.** The endpoint returns once the Daemon call does. The page learns of the change from the feed's `log` and view events (#72), like any other change.
- **Bodies.** `Content-Type` must be `application/json` (parameters allowed), else 415. At most 64KB (`http.MaxBytesReader`), else 413. The body must be one JSON object, else 400. All three use code `bad_request`.
- **`GET /api/session`.** Returns `{"csrf"}` of the session the cookie names, behind the same Host, Origin and session checks; no cookie or a stale one is a 401. On a load without `#t=`, `web/src/session.ts` calls it and keeps the token in memory; a 401 shows "open the console with `bmus console`". Another origin can't read the token: the console sends no CORS headers, a request carrying a foreign Origin is refused, and the Host check stops DNS rebinding.
- **Wrong method.** Corrects #72's "a wrong method gets 405 only with a valid session": the inner `/api/` catch-all answers a wrong method with a JSON 404 instead, on the #72 and #73 routes alike.

## Console panels (#75)

Dated 2026-10-28. Fills in "Frontend" of `console-plan-2026-10-18.md`.

- **Store.** One reducer (`web/src/store.ts`) over crew, orders, hails, the status, the recent log and progress. Its actions are `snapshot`, `log`, `older`, `status` and one per view kind; `remove` is gone, since the feed only sends whole views. A snapshot replaces everything, progress included. A `log` entry at or below the last applied id is ignored.
- **Log buffer.** The live log keeps the last 500 entries (`logLimit`). "Load older entries" asks `/api/log?before=<oldest id>&limit=200` and prepends the page; a short page marks the log's start. Each loaded page raises the cap by its size, so live entries don't drop it; a snapshot resets the cap to 500. A page is applied only if its `before` is still the oldest entry's id: a snapshot that replaced the log while the request was out makes it stale.
- **Progress.** An order's progress is its newest log entry with an `order_id` and a numeric `payload.pct` (the comms pump's progress entries, kind `order`, `{seq, pct}`), clamped to 0-100; the entry's text is the note. It is kept per order as entries arrive, so it outlives the log buffer, but a snapshot rebuilds it from its 200 entries only: an order whose last progress is older shows `--` until its next report.
- **Mission record.** Snapshots carry no finished orders. An order the store has a view of is decided by its state (the feed sends terminal views), placed at its newest entry in the recent log. An order without a view is decided by its newest `done` or `fail` entry, not its newest entry: a mission's done or fail is followed by "crew back on deck" (kind `crew`, same `order_id`). A done entry's `payload.summary` is shown as the summary.
- **Composer.** The design has no repo field, but order.create needs one repo. The composer adds Repo (seeded once from the repos of orders the store knows, then left to the captain, who can clear it) and Base ref (default `main`). The console has no repo list; a missing or unknown repo gets the server's error, which names the registered repos. A pool order needs a division: the division select offers the divisions of the crew on the manifest, or a text field when there are none.
- **Phase 2 controls.** Belay, Beam up (recall), Withdraw, Route, Re-energize and Standing orders are shown with `aria-disabled` (still focusable), a `title`, and the same text as their `aria-describedby` description: "<Action> is not wired into the console yet (#78). Use bmus ...". The backends exist; only the console endpoints are missing. Standing orders' state isn't in the snapshot or status, so the switch shows no on/off.
- **Agent text.** Hail questions and context, progress notes, summaries and log text are plain text children (`white-space: pre-wrap`), never HTML, markdown or links. A hail with no options gets a free-text decision field.
- **Feed client.** `EventSource` on `/api/events`; the browser's own reconnect sends Last-Event-ID. When the browser gives up (a non-200 reply), the page checks `GET /api/session`: a 401 shows "session lost", anything else reopens the stream after 3s, which starts with a snapshot. The header shows the link: connecting, live, reconnecting or session lost.
- **Phone width.** Below 900px the column wrappers become `display: contents` and the panels are ordered hails, away missions, crew, issue order, open orders, log, mission record, transporter room. The lamp panel is hidden. The order is visual only: keyboard and screen reader order follows the DOM, which keeps the desktop columns (crew, issue order, hails, open orders, away missions, record, transporter room, log). Putting hails first in the DOM would take it out of the middle column on desktop.
- **CSP.** No inline styles in HTML. The only dynamic style is the progress bar width, set through Preact's style object (CSSOM), which `default-src 'self'` allows; Chromium reports no violations.
- **Tests.** `preact-render-to-string` (dev dependency) renders panels in vitest to check that agent text comes out escaped.

## Console end-to-end test (#77)

Dated 2026-10-29. Fills in "Testing" (end to end) of `console-plan-2026-10-18.md`.

- **Where.** `web/e2e/`, run by `npm run e2e`, with `@playwright/test` a dev dependency of `web/`.
- **bmus and combadge.** The suite's global setup builds bmus with `go build`. Combadge must be built beforehand: the setup takes `BMUS_COMBADGE`, else `combadge/dist/combadge.mjs`, and fails up front when it's missing.
- **The bridge.** Each test runs its own holodeck bridge in a short temp dir (`/tmp` when the system's is long), keeping beam socket paths under the Unix limit. Teardown stops the bridge (SIGTERM, then SIGKILL) and kills each beam's serve process group, which outlives the bridge.
- **Browser.** `@playwright/test` is pinned to exactly 1.56.1, the version whose Chromium the dev container preinstalls. CI installs that version's headless shell. `BMUS_E2E_CHROMIUM` points the suite at another Chromium executable.
- **Finding.** No store write ever sets an order to `hailing` (#134): the Away missions panel's "Hailing" never shows, so the test checks the crew card instead.

## Order hailing state (#134)

Dated 2026-10-29. Amends "Two rows, one entry" and closes the #77 finding.

- **Three rows, one entry.** `InsertHail` also sets the order `hailing`, only from `away` and only while the hail's crew member is its assignee: a late hail from a mission whose order was recalled, claimed by someone else or settled leaves the order alone (#48). `MarkHailDecided`, `DecideOpenHail` and `TimeOutOpenHail` set the hail's order back `away`, only from `hailing`. `TimeOutOpenHail` now also sets the hail's crew member back `away`, as the two decide methods do, with the same #43 and #48 guards: before, a hail closed unanswered left its crew member `hailing` (during a belay, up to `belayTimeout`). The change rides the hail's own entry ("hail ..." or "hail <id> decided"/"closed unanswered", which name the order), so the feed sends the order's view after it. No new log entry.
- **Close, then move.** Park, recall, belay, the mission's order-done and order-failed, and the tour's backstop all close the open hail (timed_out) before they move the order, so the order is `away` by then; every one of them also accepts `hailing` as before. Settling from `hailing` (done, failed) leaves a settled order as it is when its hail closes later.
- **Readers.** Status already counts `away` and `hailing` as `orders.away`, and the console's Away missions panel already shows "Hailing" for a hailing order. `bmus order list` now prints `hailing` for an order with an open hail.
- **Restart.** An order `hailing` at restart stays so: its mission recovers waiting on the hail, and the hail's answer or close moves it back.

## Console hail notifications (#76)

Dated 2026-10-29. Fills in "Notifications" of `console-plan-2026-10-18.md`.

- **New hail.** A hail notifies when a live `hail` event, or a snapshot after the first (a reconnect: a gap, a reopen without Last-Event-ID), brings an open hail whose id the store doesn't have. The first load's snapshot never notifies, nor does a re-sent view of a known hail or a hail first seen decided or timed out. The reducer queues such ids in `fresh`; the app raises the notifications and acknowledges them with an `announced` action, whether or not permission is granted, so each hail is announced at most once. A queued hail that is no longer open by then is skipped.
- **Focus.** Notifications show whether or not the tab is visible or focused. The spec asks for none of that filtering, and a notification for a hail on screen is cheap.
- **Content.** Title "Hail from <crew name, else crew id> · <order id>", body the question as given (the API renders text), `tag` the hail id so repeats collapse. Clicking focuses the window, scrolls to the hail's card (`id="hail-<id>"`, `tabindex=-1`, labelled by its meta line and question), focuses it and closes the notification. The card shows a focus outline only when a notification focused it, not on a mouse click.
- **Permission.** A "HAIL ALERTS" readout in the header: a "Notify me" button while permission is `default`, which calls `Notification.requestPermission()`; "On" when granted; "Blocked in the browser's site settings" when denied; nothing without the API. Never asked on load. Where `navigator.permissions` answers for notifications, its change event keeps the readout current when the captain changes the site settings. No CSP change: notifications load no resources.
- **Test.** Chromium's headless shell reports `Notification.permission` as `denied` even when the context grants it, and shows no OS notifications. The e2e test replaces `window.Notification` in an init script with a stand-in that reports `granted` and records each notification.

## Console Phase 2 controls (#78)

Dated 2026-10-29. Fills in the action table's Phase 2 rows of `console-plan-2026-10-18.md` and closes #75's "Phase 2 controls" item.

- **Endpoints.** `POST /api/orders/{id}/belay`, `/recall` and `/withdraw` take `{reason?}`; `POST /api/orders/{id}/route` takes `{assignee?, division?}` (absent keeps, empty clears, as order.route); `POST /api/crew/{id}/reenergize` takes `{}`; `PUT /api/standing-orders` takes `{on}`. Each is behind the #73 checks and body rules, and an `id` in the body is a 400: the id comes from the path. Every one answers 200 with the RPC's result: the `OrderView` for the order actions (as the request is queued, for belay and recall), the `CrewActionResult` (the `CrewView` plus `outcome`: `requested` or `under_way`) for re-energize, `{on}` for standing orders.
- **Shared helpers.** The RPC handlers' inline logic moved to `Daemon.orderAction`, `routeOrder`, `crewAction` and `switchStandingOrders`, which the RPC and the console both call. The RPC's wire behaviour is unchanged.
- **Conflict.** A `conflict` (only a rejected push, which wraps git's output) is logged and answered with the fixed `<action> failed: conflict (details in the bridge's log)`, as `shutting_down` and 500s are. No console action returns it today.
- **Standing orders in status.** `bridge.status` gains `standing_orders`: the setting, else the roster's default. Reading it never seeds the setting, so a status read writes nothing. Toggling logs `standing orders on/off`, and the status check after that entry sends the `status` event. `bmus status` prints a `standing orders` line.
- **Snapshot.** `orders` also carries every order an entry of the snapshot's 200-entry log names, whatever its state. The mission record then shows belayed and withdrawn orders after a reload, which have no done or fail entry. A finished order whose entries have all left the window drops out, as before.
- **Confirm step.** Belay, Beam up and Withdraw ask first. The button is replaced inline by the prompt (a `role=group` labelled by it) with a confirm and a Cancel button. The confirm button takes focus; Cancel or Escape puts the first button back, focused. A refusal shows as an inline `role=alert` and puts the button back. Once sent, the button stays disabled and focus moves to "Sent. Waiting for the bridge." (`tabindex=-1`) until the feed moves the row. No `window.confirm`. Re-energize, Route and the switch don't ask: none of them loses work.
- **Route.** The picker offers the division's pool and the crew on the manifest, those in sickbay disabled (route refuses them). The pick goes out with a Route button, not on change: browsing a select with the keyboard would send each option. A crew pick sends `{assignee}`, the pool `{assignee: ""}`; the division is kept, as `bmus order route --assignee` keeps it. After a sent route the button stays inert until the feed brings the new route, and the picker then starts from it. The pool option is disabled for an order without a division, which route would refuse.
- **Standing orders switch.** `role=switch` with `aria-checked` from `status.standing_orders`, disabled until the first status. Its visible on/off is `aria-hidden`. While a press, a route or a re-energize is out, its control is `aria-disabled` with an early return, not `disabled`, so focus stays on it. The composer's pool hint and the switch's hint follow it.
- **Tests.** Re-energize's e2e test kills the beam's serve and waits out the pump's 30s heartbeat staleness, about 35s of the suite.

## Order progress on the order (#141)

Dated 2026-10-29. Supersedes "Progress" of #75 and amends "Store" there.

- **Stored on the order.** `orders` gains `progress_pct` (NULL: no report), `progress_note`, `progress_tour` and `progress_seq`, added to older databases by `addedColumns`. The comms pump's progress handler calls `InboxTx.SetOrderProgress` before appending the progress entry, in the inbox transaction: the entry is the change's one entry (#69), and the feed's `order` event after it carries the new view. Every report is still logged, applied or not.
- **When a report applies.** Only while the order is `away` or `hailing`, assigned to the reporting crew member and not parked, and only if it is newer than the stored one: a later tour, or a later seq of the same tour. A tour's beam numbers from seq 1 and the pump can record seqs out of order after a reconnect, so seq alone doesn't order reports. Reports a resuming beam sends before `UnparkOrder` commits are logged but don't update the order.
- **Reset.** Every update that moves an order out of `away` and `hailing` clears its progress: settle (done, failed), belay and recall. A recalled order's next claim, by anyone, starts with none. Parking keeps it: the parked mission is the same mission, and the resuming tour's first report replaces it. Reports from the parked beam are dropped.
- **Views.** `OrderView` gains `progress_pct` and `progress_note`, omitted without a report (`progress_pct` is a pointer: 0% is a report). `bmus order list --json` carries them; the table gains a PROGRESS column (`40%`, `-` without one).
- **Console.** `orderProgress` in `web/src/view.ts` reads the order view, clamped to 0-100. The reducer's progress map and its log scan are gone: a reload shows a report older than the snapshot's 200 entries. The log panel's "(40%)" still comes from each entry's payload (`progressPct`, moved to `view.ts`).

## Regulations engine (#148)

Dated 2026-10-29. Fills in `phase-3-plan-2026-10-29.md` §2 where it left a choice open.

- **Inputs.** The caller builds both inputs; `regs` imports neither `comms` nor `store`.
  - `regs.Hail{Category, Question, Options, Tool, Command, Values, Malformed, Priority}`, from `store.Hail` and the order's priority.
  - `regs.Reply{Decision, Option, Answer, Confidence, RiskFlags}`, from `comms.ConsultReplyBody`.
  - The order's division isn't carried: no rule reads it, and the hail workflow handles hails from Command itself (§3 step 2).
- **Command.** `Hail.SetToolContext(context)` reads a `tool_approval` hail's context, `{"tool", "input"}` as combadge's sdk harness writes it, into `Tool`, `Command`, `Values` and `Malformed`.
  - `Command` is `input.command` whenever that is a string, whatever the tool (`Bash`, `Monitor`, ...). It is the only thing `auto_allow` matches.
  - For any tool but `Bash`, `Values` are every string in the input, decoded and walked in sorted key order. Decoding keeps `\t` from gluing words, and the strings take in a command's arguments, a `Write`'s content and the rest.
  - `Malformed` is set for a context that isn't such an object, names no tool, or has an `input.command` that isn't a string. A `tool_approval` hail that is `Malformed`, or has an empty `Tool`, routes to the captain.
- **Methods.** `Classify` and `CheckAnswer` are methods on `Config`. The YAML key stays `classify`, and the field is `ClassifyRules`. An unchecked `Config` (one `CheckRegs` didn't pass) routes every hail to the captain.
- **Regexes.** Go RE2, case-sensitive unless a rule says `(?i)`. `classify` and `captain_only` are unanchored. `auto_allow` compiles the raw expression first and only then wraps it as `^(?:...)$`, so `go test)|(.*` is an error, not a way out of the anchors.
- **What the rules see.** `classify` and `captain_only` regexes read `Command` followed by `Values`, separated by blank lines, so a value ending in a backslash can't join the next one as a line continuation. They run over three forms of that text and fit if any matches:
  - the text with format characters (`unicode.Cf`) dropped, NFKC applied and every whitespace run, newlines and Unicode spaces included, collapsed to one space;
  - the same with `'`, `"` and `\` removed, so `g''it push` and `gi\t push` read as `git push`;
  - the normalized text with expansions removed, then de-quoted, so `g$()it push` reads as `git push`. The expansions removed are `$(...)`, `${...}`, backtick pairs, `$""`, `$@`, `$*` and `$0`-`$9`, `$#`, `$?`, `$!`, `$_`, `$-`.

  A backslash-newline (line continuation) is removed before any of this, so `gi\<newline>t push` reads as `git push`.
- **Size cap.** Hail text is the command, values, question and every option label; past 1 MB of it, `Classify` routes to the captain with "too large to classify". So does text that passes 1 MB once normalized, since NFKC can grow text up to 18 times (U+FDFA). Normalizing stops at the cap rather than finishing. `CheckAnswer` rejects any question, picked label or answer that passes 1 MB, raw or normalized, the same way.
- **Question first.** `Classify` routes to the captain when the hail's question alone fits a `captain_only` rule as `CheckAnswer` reads answer texts. "Should I git push now?" then reaches the captain without a consult. `CheckAnswer` still checks the question too, as a backstop.

  `$'...'` (ANSI-C quoting, e.g. `$'\x67it'`) isn't decoded; the default tags `$'` itself as `external_effect`.
- **Every tag counts.** `classify` still reports the first rule's tag, and `auto_allow` sees only that one. `captain_only`'s `Tag` rules see every tag that fits, so a harmless rule listed first can't hide `git push` behind it.
- **Auto-allow fails closed.** Only a `tool_approval` hail is ever `RouteAuto`, and `CheckRegs` rejects an `auto_allow` rule naming any other category. An auto-allowed hail is decided by policy (`decided_by=policy`), not skipped.
  - Auto-allow refuses a command holding `;`, `&`, `|`, a backtick, `$(`, `<` or `>`, which covers the plan's `&&` and `||`, plus a lone `&` and redirection or process substitution.
  - It also refuses any byte outside printable ASCII, space and tab. That covers newline, `\r`, `\v`, `\f`, NBSP and Unicode spaces or letters.
  - What remains has only spaces and tabs to collapse. `auto_allow` matches against the original command collapsed that way, never the de-quoted form.
- **Rule fields.** A `MatchRule` fits when every field set fits. `Alert` means "at least this priority". An `Option` rule's other fields scope it, and `Classify` skips it. `CheckRegs` rejects these:
  - an empty rule;
  - an `auto_allow` rule that sets none of `Tool`, `Match` or `Tag` (category or alert alone would allow every tool);
  - `Option` in `auto_allow`;
  - a `Tag` no `classify` rule sets;
  - an unknown `Alert`;
  - `min_confidence` outside 0-1;
  - negative durations;
  - an unknown `on_timeout`, which defaults to `hold`;
  - `default_option` without a value.

  Errors name the rule, e.g. `captain_only[1]: match "git (push": ...`.
- **Safety defaults.** `min_confidence` and `countermand_window` are pointers, set by `CheckRegs`. Missing means 0.75 and 5m; an explicit 0 stays 0 (any confidence, no window). `captain_reminder_every` and `captain_timeout` default to 0 (never), and `on_timeout` to `hold`: those zeroes don't weaken anything.
- **CheckAnswer.** It rejects:
  - a hail `Classify` routes to the captain;
  - any decision but `answer`;
  - any risk flag, toward caution;
  - a confidence that is NaN, below the minimum or above 1;
  - an option out of range;
  - a reply with neither option nor answer.

  `Option` rules see the hail's question, the picked label and the free-text answer, so "Yes" to "Delete the prod DB?" escalates. The classify rules also run over each of those texts, ignoring their `Tool`. A `captain_only` `Tag` rule whose tag one of them gets rejects the reply, so picking "rm -rf the dir", or answering `git push --force`, escalates. So does a `captain_only` `Match` rule whose regex matches one of the texts. This includes the question as well as the answer, toward caution. Each text has `unicode.Cf` dropped and NFKC applied first: zero-width spaces, soft hyphens and fullwidth letters don't hide a match. Cross-script lookalikes (Cyrillic "е" in "dеlete") aren't folded. NFKC doesn't map them, and a test pins the gap.
- **Durations.** `time.Duration` decoded by yaml.v3: Go duration strings (`5m`, `1h30m`). A bare number is an error.
- **Default.** The classify rules err toward matching too much.
  - `\bgit\b.*\bpush\b`, case-insensitive, on any tool. It catches `/usr/bin/git push`, `git -C dir push`, `FOO=1 git push`, `command`/`env`/`xargs`/`nohup git push`, `git "push"` and `GIT PUSH`. Git's subcommands are case-sensitive, but a case-insensitive filesystem (macOS) runs `GIT`.
  - `rm` with `-r`, `-rf`, `-fr`, `-R` or `--r...`, on any tool.
  - `DROP` or `TRUNCATE` of a table, database or schema, on any tool.
  - These three also read a `Write` or `Edit`'s content: a `Makefile` gaining `git push`, or a script gaining `rm -rf`, asks the captain.
  - Inline code applies only on `Bash` and `Monitor`, the tools that run a command, so a `Write` of Python holding `eval(` goes to Command. It covers `bash`/`sh`/`zsh`/`dash`/`ksh -c`, `eval`, `$'`, `python[0-9.]* -c`, `node -e/-p/--eval/--print`, `perl -e/-E`, `ruby -e` and `php -r`. A script file (`python x.py`) can't be covered this way.

  `min_confidence` is 0.75, `countermand_window` 5m and `captain_reminder_every` 30m, from the initial plan's example. `on_timeout` is `hold`.
- **`bmus regs init`.** It writes the embedded file as-is: the header is its leading comment. It creates `<home>` (0700) if needed and opens with `O_EXCL`. It has no `--force`: remove the file to start over.
- **Bridge.** `bridge.Config.Regs` and `RegsConfigured` carry the loaded file to `Daemon.regs`/`regsConfigured`. `Run` re-checks them. Nothing reads them until #149.

## Hail timestamps and Command guidance (#147)

Dated 2026-10-29.

- **Timestamp source.** `created_at` and `decided_at` are the `ts` of the log entry `InsertHail` and `decideOpenHail` append, in UTC (now when the entry has none), so a hail's times and its log lines agree. A timeout sets neither `decided_at` nor `original_decided_by`. `countermanded_at` is added now and written by #153.
- **First decision.** `decided_at` and `original_decided_by` record when and by whom the hail was first decided (closed). `decideOpenHail` sets each only while it is unset, in the same `UPDATE`; a countermand (#153) leaves both alone and sets `countermanded_at`. `HailView` omits `created_at` on older hails and `decided_at`/`original_decided_by` until decided.
- **Guidance.** The `command_guidance` setting is trimmed of surrounding space and capped at 4096 bytes (`bridge.MaxCommandGuidance`, a `bad_request` over it): every consult carries it. Empty is valid and clears it. Setting it logs `command guidance set` (the text in the payload) or `command guidance cleared` (no payload). The same value, including empty on a bridge that never had any, changes and logs nothing.
- **`show` output.** `bmus command guidance show` prints the text alone, or nothing when none is set, so its output can be fed back to `set`. `--json` prints `{"guidance": ...}`.

## Consult reply routing (#151)

Dated 2026-10-29.

- **Finished hail workflows.** `pumpSink.ToHail` checks the hail workflow's status (`workflowLive`) before sending, and returns `comms.ErrUndeliverable` for a missing or finished one. `ToMission` relies on the send's foreign key alone, which only catches a missing workflow: a reply sent to a finished hail workflow would be acked and lost silently. The check runs outside the inbox transaction; a hail workflow that ends between it and the commit only loses a reply it had stopped waiting for.
- **Bad replies.** A `consult_reply` whose body doesn't decode, or has no `hail_id`, is logged as a warning and dropped, its inbox row recorded, like a bad `progress` body. It isn't an undeliverable log entry: no destination exists to retry.

## Command's consult lane (#152)

Dated 2026-10-30.

- **Concurrency.** `serve` runs consults beside the order and beside each other, up to 4 at once (`MAX_CONSULTS`). One more is answered at once with `escalate`, "Command busy (4 consults in flight)", confidence 0: it goes to the captain rather than queueing behind the others. Each sdk consult is its own `claude` process. A consult is recorded handled on arrival, like an order: one lost to a restart gets no reply, and the bridge's `consultTimeout` routes the hail to the captain.
- **Fallbacks.** A harness without `consult` (echo, a holodeck script without one) gets `escalate`, "harness <name> does not run a consult lane", confidence 0, from `serve`. A harness call that throws or rejects gets `escalate`, "consult failed: <message>". `serve` sets `hail_id` from the request on every reply. Closing `serve` aborts consults in flight and drops their replies.
- **sdk query.** One fresh `query()` per consult: `tools: []`, `allowedTools: []`, `mcpServers: {}` with `strictMcpConfig`, `permissionMode: "dontAsk"`, `settingSources: []` (no CLAUDE.md, user hooks or settings), `persistSession: false`, `maxTurns: 1`, a replacing system prompt and the SDK's default model (a consult carries none). The session's init must carry `tools` and `mcp_servers` as empty lists; anything else, missing or mis-shaped included, escalates. A failed query, an init offering tools or missing, a missing `claude`, the 2-minute timeout (`SdkDeps.consultTimeoutMs`) and `serve`'s abort each answer `escalate` with confidence 0 and say which in the rationale. A failure logs the tail of `claude`'s stderr.
- **Prompt framing.** Only `guidance` and `regulations` are trusted: they go into the system prompt, each under its own heading as a JSON-encoded string, so their text can't forge a heading. `order_spec` is data: a split child's body comes from Command's own agent, not the captain. It goes into the user message with the hail's fields (crew, order, category, question, options as `{index, label}`, context, recent_log) as one pretty-printed JSON document, so quoted text can't fake a delimiter. The system prompt says all of it is data, not instructions.
- **Parsing.** The reply is one JSON object: the whole trimmed text, or the content of exactly one code fence with only whitespace outside it (any `[A-Za-z0-9_+-]` language tag, CRLF line ends allowed). No object is dug out of prose, and a second fence fails: the model may be quoting an injected reply from the hail before declining it. `decision` must be `answer` or `escalate`, `confidence` a number in [0, 1], `option` (null counts as absent) an integer indexing the hail's options, `answer` and `rationale` strings (a missing rationale is ""). An `answer` carries exactly one of the two: `option` when the hail has options, a non-empty trimmed `answer` when it has none. An `escalate` keeps neither. Non-string `risk_flags` entries are dropped. Anything else is `escalate`, "could not parse Command's reply", confidence 0.
- **Holodeck.** A script file may be `{"steps": [...], "consult": <reply>}`, so #150 can script Command: every consult gets the same reply at once (a `consult_reply` body without `hail_id`), or never on `"silence"`. A plain steps array still works, and runs no consult lane. A silent consult holds one of the 4 slots until `serve` closes, so a fifth gets "Command busy".

## Hail workflow (#149)

Dated 2026-10-30. Builds `phase-3-plan-2026-10-29.md` §3 steps 1, 2 and 6, without Command (#150).

- **Start.** The mission starts `hail/<id>` (`internal/bridge/hail.go`, registered as `hail`, no recovery-attempt limit) right after `record-hail`, as a child workflow it never awaits. The id makes a replay, or a hail `InsertHail` found already open, start nothing new. `hailWorkflowID` and `consultReplyTopic` moved there from `pumps.go`.
- **Routing.** `classify` reads the hail, its order and its crew member, in this order:
  - no `regs.yaml`: the captain, "regs.yaml not configured";
  - `RouteAuto`: decided at once, but only when option 0 is `allow` (option 0 is what allows a tool); otherwise the captain, as captain-only;
  - `RouteCaptain`: the captain, with Classify's rule;
  - a crew member in the `command` division (`bridge.DivisionCommand`, the crew member's division, not the order's): the captain, "from Command";
  - `RouteCommand`: the captain, "Command does not take hails yet", until #150.

  Auto-allow applies to Command's own tool approvals: it is the captain's written policy whoever asks.
- **Route column.** `InsertHail` still defaults `route` to `captain`. `decide-policy` sets it to `auto` in its decision's transaction; `route-captain` sets `captain` and logs "hail H-0001 for the captain: <why>" in one transaction, only while the hail is open (`store.UpdateOpenHail`).
- **Auto decision.** `decide-policy` is `AnswerHail`'s transaction with `decided_by=policy`, decision `allow`, option 0, the matched rule as rationale and the answer sent to the mission under `dbos.WithSendTransaction` (`store.DecideOpenHailAs`, which also takes route, confidence and `countermand_until`). No `countermand_until`. The decided entry's payload carries the rationale when there is one.
- **decided_by.** The mission no longer overwrites an answer's `decided_by`: `AnswerHail` sends `captain`, the hail workflow `policy`, and the mission relays it to the beam. The holodeck's step log records each hail's `decided_by`.
- **Closing notification.** The workflow learns its hail closed on topic `hail-closed`. `AnswerHail` and `closeHailAs` (every `TimeOutOpenHail` path: park, recall, belay, order done or failed, the tour's backstop, via `store.TimeOutOpenHailThen`) send it in the transaction that closes the hail, only to a live workflow (`workflowLive`, checked inside the transaction). The store's `_txlock=immediate` holds SQLite's one write lock for the whole transaction, so no workflow can start between the check and the commit; one started later finds the hail closed in `classify`. The message only wakes the workflow: on every wake it reads the hail's row (`check-hail`) and ends once the hail is no longer open. With nothing else due it still wakes every 24h (`hailRecheckEvery`), so a close that slipped past the notification can't leave it waiting for good.
- **Settings are recorded.** `classify` records the escalation settings and the countermand window with its result. A `regs.yaml` changed across a restart applies to new hails only, and a replay takes the recorded path.
- **Reminders.** Every `captain_reminder_every` from `classify`, a `remind` step logs "hail H-0001 still open after 30m", only while the hail is open. Reminders missed while the bridge was down coalesce into one; the next beat is computed, not stepped to. `CheckRegs` rejects a non-zero `captain_reminder_every` or `captain_timeout` under 1s (`regs.MinEscalation`): each beat is a log entry.
- **Re-runs.** `route-captain`, `remind`, `on-timeout`'s hold note and its belay request each write in their own transaction, then DBOS records the step. A crash between the two re-runs the step and logs the line again ("belay requested" too, which the mission then ignores). Duplicate lines are the only effect.
- **Timeout.** Past `captain_timeout`, the `on-timeout` step applies `on_timeout` once:
  - `hold` logs "hail H-0001 unanswered after <t>: holding for the captain";
  - `belay` calls `BelayOrder` with reason "hail H-0001 unanswered after <t>"; the mission then closes the hail as it does for any belay. A bad request (the order moved on) is logged and ignored;
  - `default_option` decides through the policy path: decision the option's label, rationale "captain timeout: unanswered after <t>, default_option N", entry "hail H-0001 decided on the captain timeout: <label>", route left `captain`, `countermand_until` = the decision's time plus `countermand_window` (none for a 0 window).

  `default_option` holds instead, logging why, for:
  - any hail Classify routed to the captain: a `captain_only` rule, and also a malformed or oversized `tool_approval` hail or one whose option 0 isn't `allow`, which fail closed the same way;
  - a default option `regs.CheckAnswer` rejects as an answer (decision `answer`, confidence 1). Classify skips `captain_only` option rules, so without this a hail routed to the captain only for want of Command could time out into "force push over it". `classify` runs the check and records the reason (`DefaultBlocked`);
  - a missing `default_option`, or one that indexes past the hail's options ("default_option 5 is not one of the hail's 2 options").

  It applies to hails routed to the captain only for want of Command, including hails from Command itself. When `on-timeout` finds the hail already closed (closed meanwhile, or decided by a run whose result a crash lost), the workflow reads the hail at once rather than waiting again.
- **Status.** `BridgeStatus.Info` (`info` in JSON, always a list) holds "regs.yaml not configured" on a bridge without one. It never changes the condition. `bmus status` prints each as an `info` line; the console's `BridgeStatus` type carries it, and nothing shows it yet.
- **decide-hail.** The mission's `decide-hail` (`MarkHailDecided`) doesn't wake the hail workflow: every answer's sender decided the hail first, and either woke the workflow (`AnswerHail`) or is the workflow (`decide-policy`).
- **Consults (from the #151 review, for #150).** A hail workflow receives on `consult-reply` at most once: `ConsultReplyBody` has no consult attempt id, so a second consult of the same hail could read the first one's stale reply. A reply that arrives after the consult timeout is acked and never read, by design: Command's late opinion no longer matters.
- **Upgrade.** A mission that recorded a hail under the old code and is still PENDING replays with the new child workflow start where its history has the next step, and DBOS v1.7.0 refuses that (`ErrorCodeUnexpectedStep`). Bridge databases from before the first release stay disposable; drain missions that have hailed before upgrading one.
- **Result.** `HailResult{Decided, By}` is recorded and read by nothing yet.

## Command consult round trip (#150)

Dated 2026-10-30. Builds `phase-3-plan-2026-10-29.md` §3 steps 3 to 5 on #149's hail workflow.

- **Command's crew member.** `Daemon.commandCrew` is the first crew member of the `command` division, by id, whose status is `on_deck`, `away` or `hailing` (`pumpCrewStatuses`: anyone with a live beam) and whose pump is connected; with none connected, the first of them. Command's mission lane and consult lane run at once (D7), so a Command crew member on an order still takes consults. A roster with divisions must list `command: {}` before `crew add --division command` passes (`checkDivision`, unchanged); a roster without divisions takes it as any other.
- **Steps.** `classify` now routes a routine hail `RouteCommand` and records `consult_timeout` with the other settings. Then:
  - `find-command` picks Command and, only while the hail is open, sets its route to `command` and logs "hail H-0001 for Command: vance". Nobody on deck: the captain, "Command not on deck".
  - `transmit-consult` builds the consult and sends it with `SendCommand` (no order on the message), with `sendRetries`. A send still failing after them goes to the captain, "could not send the consult to <crew>: <err>".
  - One `Recv` on `consult-reply` for `consult_timeout` (`bridge.Config.ConsultTimeout`, `bmus bridge run --consult-timeout`, default 10m). A timeout goes to the captain, "Command did not reply within 10m". DBOS records the deadline, so a restart waits only the rest.
  - `decide-command` runs `regs.CheckAnswer` and decides in one step.
- **The consult.** The hail's fields; `order_spec` is the order's title, a blank line and its body, cut to 32 KiB; `recent_log` the order's last 20 log lines, oldest first, as `<RFC 3339 UTC> <kind>: <text>`, each cut to 2 KiB (`store.ListOrderLogLast`, over a new `log (order_id, id)` index); `guidance` the `command_guidance` setting (already capped at 4 KiB); `context` left out when it is empty or `{}`. A context over 32 KiB goes as `{"truncated": "<its first 32 KiB of JSON text>"}`: cut JSON isn't JSON. Cut text ends in ` [truncated]`, within the cap, on a UTF-8 boundary. `regulations` is a short excerpt from the current `regs.yaml`: that no captain-only or auto-allow rule fits, the classify tag if any, `min_confidence`, and each `captain_only` option rule, since those read Command's answer.
- **Who may reply.** `pumpSink.ToHail` delivers a `consult_reply` only from a crew member of the `command` division, read in the inbox transaction. Anyone else's (or an unknown crew member's) is final: it is logged as a warning and dropped at once, its inbox row committed, so a stray reply can't use up the workflow's single read or hold the sender's stream. `decide-command` also requires the reply's crew to be the one `find-command` consulted: another Command crew member's goes to the captain, "Command's reply came from <x>, not <y>".
- **The reply.** Replies reach the workflow by `hail_id` (#151), so one naming another hail can't really arrive. If one did, it goes to the captain ("Command's reply is for hail H-0002"): the workflow reads `consult-reply` once and doesn't wait again. So does a message of another type, or one that doesn't decode. A rejection is logged with its reason: "Command escalated: <rationale>" (or just "Command escalated"), else "Regulations rejected Command's answer: <CheckAnswer's reason>". A captain-only reason from CheckAnswer outranks the escalate wording. Risk flags are passed to CheckAnswer and reject. Command's rationale is cut to 2 KiB wherever it goes: the reason, the hail's rationale, the answer to the beam.
- **Command's objection holds.** When Command escalated or flagged risks, the reason is also recorded as `DefaultBlocked`: `on_timeout default_option` then holds for the captain ("holding for the captain (default_option 0: Command escalated: ...)") instead of answering by policy over Command's objection. Other rejections (low confidence, a captain-only answer) leave `default_option` as #149 has it.
- **The decision.** `decided_by=command`, `route=command`, `confidence` from the reply, the reply's rationale, `countermand_until` = the decision's time plus `countermand_window` as recorded at classify (none for 0). The decision is the picked option's label, else the free-text answer, with the reply's option. The log reads "hail H-0001 decided by Command: <decision>". `decideByPolicy` and `decide-command` share `Daemon.decideOpen`. `CheckAnswer` reads the current `regs.yaml` inside the recorded step.
- **Closing during the consult.** For a hail whose route is `command` (read in the closing transaction), `notifyHailClosed` also sends a message with no type on `consult-reply`, in the same transaction as its `hail-closed` send. `find-command` sets that route before the consult goes out, and `route-captain` replaces it on a fallback. A workflow waiting for Command wakes on it, reads the hail closed and ends without routing it; one past that point never reads it. A real reply queued before the close is read first; `decide-command` then finds the hail closed and decides nothing. A hail closed after its consult fell back to the captain gets no such message. One closed after the reply was read, before `decide-command`, leaves it unread.
- **One consult.** Each workflow sends at most one consult and reads `consult-reply` once. A `transmit-consult` re-run after a crash can send the consult twice; the second reply is never read. Late replies (after the timeout) are acked and never read, as #149 recorded.
- **Restart.** A bridge restarted during the consult wait resumes the `Recv` with the deadline DBOS recorded; neither the consult nor `find-command` runs again.
- **Captain fallback.** Every fallback runs `route-captain` with the reason and then #149's captain wait unchanged. Reminders and `captain_timeout` count from `classify`, consult wait included: they measure how long the agent has waited. `default_option` applies as for any hail routed to the captain for want of Command, subject to the `DefaultBlocked` check.
- **Status.** `StatusSnapshot.CommandDown` is set by `Daemon.Status` (`ReadStatus` knows neither regs nor divisions): `regs.yaml` configured, someone not dismissed in `command`, nobody of them on deck. Its reason, "Command not on deck", goes after the crew and order reasons and before the login's, and is listed whatever the condition. It sets yellow on its own, but the only status that downs Command today is sickbay, which is red. With Command in sickbay `bmus status` therefore reads red, "vance in sickbay", "Command not on deck", not yellow as #150's acceptance put it. Nobody in the division: no reason.
- **Holodeck.** The holodeck step log records each consult as it arrives, `{"action": "consult", "ts", "consult": <body>}`, so bridge tests can check what Command was sent.
- **Step lists in tests.** dbos-transact-golang v1.7.0's `Recv` records a `DBOS.sleep` step only when no message is waiting yet. Tests must not expect `DBOS.sleep` after a `DBOS.recv`; hail tests compare `hailStepNames`, which drops it.
- **Console.** The console lists only open hails and shows neither `route` nor `decided_by`, so `command` needs nothing new there.

## Consults on Command's crew model (#162)

Dated 2026-10-30.

- **Protocol.** `ConsultBody` gains an optional `model` (omitempty in Go, an optional non-empty string in the schema). Opaque to the bridge, like an order's.
- **Bridge.** `transmit-consult` reads Command's crew row and fills `model` with `resolveModel(store.Order{}, crew)`: the crew member's model, else its division's default, else none. The same function orders and `crew list` use.
- **Combadge.** The sdk harness passes a set `model` to the consult's `query()` as `model`, as it does an order's. Without one the SDK's default applies. The model isn't part of the consult prompt.

## Countermand (#153)

Dated 2026-10-30. Builds `phase-3-plan-2026-10-29.md` §6 and the countermand control of §11.

- **Store.** `store.CountermandHail(ctx, id, decision, reason, entry, fn)` reads the row and writes in one transaction, with now taken inside it (also the entry's TS): `status = decided`, `original_decided_by` in `{policy, command}`, `countermand_until` set and not before now, else `ErrCountermandExpired` (wrapped, with the reason). It sets `decided_by = captain`, `decision`, `rationale = reason` and `countermanded_at`, and leaves `decided_at`, `original_decided_by`, `route`, `confidence` and `countermand_until` alone. `Hail.CountermandedAt` is read with the rest.
- **Countermand before decide-hail.** The mission's `decide-hail` (`MarkHailDecided`) can run after a countermand committed: the answer and the correction were both queued by then. `MarkHailDecided` takes `status = decided`, `original_decided_by` = its decider and `countermanded_at` set as a no-op, like the same decision repeated. The agent gets the first answer, then the correction.
- **Again.** A second countermand inside the same window is taken. The window belongs to the first decision, not to who decided last.
- **Bridge.** `Daemon.CountermandHail(ctx, hailID, decision, option, reason)`. An open hail is a bad request ("answer it"), a missing one `ErrNotFound`, an option out of range a bad request, as `AnswerHail`. `regs.CheckAnswer` doesn't apply: its captain-only rules reserve answers for the captain, who is the one countermanding.
- **Log.** "hail H-0001 countermanded: <reason>", or the decision when there is no reason, kind `hail`, payload `hail_id`, `decided_by` captain, `decision`, `option`, `rationale` (the reason, when given) and `countermanded` (who decided before).
- **Correction.** A new bridge-to-beam `correction`, `CorrectionBody{HailID, Ref, Note}`, order-bound: `ref` and the envelope's `order` are the hail's order. It goes to the crew member that hailed. `note` is the decision, plus " (<reason>)" when there is a reason.
- **Delivery.** Through the order's mission, as `AnswerHail`'s answer: `fn(tx)` sends a synthetic `correction` to `order/<id>` on `from-crew` with `dbos.WithSendTransaction(tx)`, a row write in the countermand's transaction. The mission's `send-correction` step passes it to the beam with `sendRetries`, behind whatever was queued before it: the first decision's `send-answer`, and an earlier countermand's correction. Repeat countermands reach the agent in commit order.
- **Undelivered.** Nothing fails the mission or the countermand. The mission drops a correction for a crew member it no longer runs on, or out of send retries, with a warning and "correction for hail H-0001 not delivered: <why>" (step `log-correction-dropped`). Parked, stopping or off the beam, it holds it for the resume instead (see "Corrections for parked orders (#167)"). A mission that has ended, checked in the transaction (`workflowLive`), is sent nothing: a message to it would never be read. That line, "the order's mission has ended", is logged after the commit, and `Daemon.CountermandHail` returns `ended` true.
- **Combadge.** `serve.ts` relays `correction` (`RELAYED`); one naming no live run, or another order, is logged and dropped by `relay`. The sdk harness pushes "The captain overrides the earlier answer to '<question>': <note>." onto the streaming input with priority `next`, as a stop's request goes in. It knows the question by the answer's `hail_id`, recorded when this run's answer arrived; a hail it didn't answer reads "hail H-0001" instead. Once the run is ending (a belay, a stop, the input closed at the first result) it is logged and dropped. The holodeck changes nothing and logs `{"order", "action": "correction", "ts", "correction": <body>}`. Echo ignores it.
- **CLI and RPC.** `bmus hails countermand <id> (--option <n> | --decision <text>) [--reason <text>] [--json]`, RPC `hails.countermand` (`HailsCountermandArgs`), answering `HailsCountermandResult`: the hail's view and a `note` when its order isn't away or hailing or its mission has ended (an order left away by a mission that ended in error) ("order ORD-0001 is done: no live run, the agent won't be told"), which the CLI prints as `note: ...`. A parked order's note is #167's. New RPC and console code `countermand_expired`, a 409 in the console.
- **Views.** `HailView` gains `confidence`, `countermand_until` and `countermanded_at` (UTC, omitted when unset). `original_decided_by` is #147's. `bmus hails list` shows them with `--json`; the table is unchanged.
- **Console visibility.** The snapshot's hails are the open ones plus decided hails still inside their window (`store.ListCountermandable`, decided by policy or command with `countermand_until` not before now). An index on `hails (countermand_until)` bounds the read to windows ending after a minute before now; the exact check runs on the times read back. Live `hail` events already carried decided views. Nothing else changes in what the console lists; #150's "lists only open hails" no longer holds.
- **Console control.** A "Decided without you" panel (`CountermandPanel`) under the hails panel lists `canCountermand` hails: decided, first by Command or policy, `now <= countermand_until`. Each card shows who decided ("Command", "the captain timeout", "you (countermanded)"), the decision, Command's confidence, the rationale and the window's end, an optional reason field, and one `ConfirmAction` per option other than the current decision (a decision field and one `ConfirmAction` for a free-text hail). The confirm asks 'Replace "<decision>" with "<option>"?'. A card whose order isn't away or hailing (or isn't in the console's state: a finished order) says the agent won't be told, and so does its prompt. After a confirm the card shows the reply's `note`, "Countermanded. <note>.", kept on the card: the confirm control resets when the feed brings the new decision. `ConfirmAction` gains `disabled`. The controls are keyed on the decision: the countermand the feed brings back resets them.
- **Window end.** `now` is the console's existing 15s header clock: a card leaves at the first tick past its window, with no timer of its own. A countermand sent in that gap gets `countermand_expired`, shown inline.
- **Tests.** The bridge test countermands a Command decision twice while a captain-only second hail keeps the order running, and checks the holodeck's correction entries and their order. Another queues a decision and its countermand in one transaction, before `decide-hail` runs. No test drops the pump's connection mid-countermand: `send-correction`'s retries across a reconnect are `sendRetries`', as `send-answer`'s. The Playwright test uses its own script (`e2e/countermand.json`), whose hail carries the sdk harness's `{tool, input}` context: `holodeck.json`'s isn't one Regulations reads, and such a hail holds for the captain.

## Order splitting (#155)

Dated 2026-10-30. Builds `phase-3-plan-2026-10-29.md` §8 and the split part of §11.

- **Protocol.** `comms.CategoryOrderSplit` (`order_split`), with `OrderSplitContext{parent, children}` and `OrderSplitChild{title, body, division, priority}` (division and priority optional, priority 0-2). The schema checks an `order_split` hail: options exactly `["allow", "deny"]`, a context with 1 to 10 children, a title of 1 to 200 characters, a non-empty body, no unknown fields. Still one golden per type: `TestSchemaOrderSplit` covers the shape.
- **Role.** `serve --role command`, taken by every harness (only sdk uses it); an unknown role fails startup. `transporter.CombadgeSpec.Role` passes it after `--harness`; `provisionBeam` sets it when `crew.Division == DivisionCommand`.
- **Tool.** The sdk harness registers `split_order` and adds it to `allowedTools` (`allowedTools(role)`) only for `--role command`: under `bypassPermissions` a registered tool runs whether pre-allowed or not. Its input is checked by zod: 1 to 10 children, a trimmed one-line title of 1 to 200 characters, a non-empty body, an optional division, priority `routine`, `yellow` or `red` (sent as 0-2). It raises the hail through the run's `hail()`, with context `{parent: <order>, children}`. Option 0 returns "Split approved; the bridge created child orders <ids>."; anything else an error result with the decision. A withdrawn hail (stop) is an error result, as for any tool. Command's system prompt gains one line on splitting.
- **Validation.** Both sides; the bridge's is the one that counts. `checkSplit` reads the hail's stored context, the one the captain sees, and refuses:
  - a crew member outside the `command` division;
  - options other than `[allow, deny]`;
  - a context that isn't one `OrderSplitContext` (unknown fields, trailing values), or names another parent;
  - no children, or more than 10 (`maxSplitChildren`);
  - a trimmed title empty, over 200 runes or holding a control character; a trimmed body empty or over 32 KiB;
  - a child with no division (its own, else the parent's), one `checkDivision` rejects, or a priority out of range;
  - parent repos `checkOrderRepos` rejects.

  Children take the parent's division and priority when they give none, and always the parent's repos; no assignee, model or budget.
- **The captain's question.** `record-hail` stores an `order_split` hail with a question the bridge builds from the context (`splitQuestion`), never the agent's: "Split ORD-0001 into 2 orders: 1) <title> [<division>, <priority>]; 2) ...", defaults resolved from the parent, control characters made spaces, titles cut to 800 bytes (any title `checkSplit` takes shows whole) and divisions to 40 (` [truncated]`), at most 10 children listed then "and N more". A context that doesn't decode or has no children reads "Split ORD-0001 into child orders: the request is malformed, and the bridge denies it". The hail's log entry carries the same text. `bmus hails list` and the console show it; a hail forged outside `split_order` gets it too.
- **Malformed splits.** `classify` runs `checkSplit` first, before the `regs.yaml` check. A malformed split is decided at once by step `deny-split`: `decided_by=policy`, route `auto`, decision "invalid order split: <why>", option `deny`, logged "hail H-0001 decided by policy: invalid order split: <why>". Nobody else sees it.
- **Routing.** A well-formed split comes from Command and goes to the captain ("from Command"), as `captain_only` in the default `regs.yaml` would send it anyway. `auto_allow` never takes it (tool_approval only). `on_timeout default_option` can decide one only under a `regs.yaml` that drops `order_split` from `captain_only`.
- **create-children.** In the mission's answer branch, after `decide-hail` and before `send-answer`, for an open hail whose recorded category is `order_split` answered with option 0, whoever decided it. Every decider queues one answer and the mission reads one answer per open hail: the step runs once. In order:
  - children this hail already created (`store.ChildBatch` by `created_by`) are the result, checked before anything else: a re-run after the insert committed returns them and never refuses;
  - a countermand that left the hail's row other than `allow` refuses ("the captain countermanded it: deny"). Without one the queued option 0 answer stands, free text included (`bmus hails answer --option 0 --decision "ok, go ahead"`);
  - `checkSplit` again (the captain may answer before `classify` denies, the roster may change across a restart);
  - `store.InsertChildOrders` in one transaction: each child in its division's pool with `parent_id` and `created_by` `hail:H-0001`, logged "order created: <title>" (payload `parent_id`), then the parent's "order split into 2 children: ORD-0002, ORD-0003" (payload `hail_id`, `children`). The parent must be away or hailing (`ErrParentEnded`). Then each child's division is woken.
- **One transaction, not one InsertOrder per child.** A re-run of the step after a crash finds the batch by `created_by` and returns its ids, writing nothing. Per-child transactions would duplicate the children made before the crash.
- **The answer.** The relayed answer's decision is the children's ids, comma separated; the hail row keeps "allow". A refusal at `create-children` creates nothing. `store.RefuseSplit` appends "split refused: <why>" to the hail's rationale (after "; " when it has one) and logs "order split refused: <why>" in one transaction, only while the rationale carries no such note: a retried step writes nothing twice. `hails list` and the console then show the allow with its refusal. The answer is that text with option 1.
- **Countermand.** One committed before `create-children` reads the hail refuses the split (above). One after it only sends the correction (#153): the children stay, for the captain to belay or withdraw. A split hail closed unanswered (park, recall, belay, mission end) creates nothing.
- **The split state.** `store.OrderSplit`. `order-done` reads `store.HasChildren` inside the step: an order with children settles `split`, its done entry (kind `done`, the report's payload, read by `bmus push` once the parent is done) reading "split, waiting on its child orders: <summary>...". The mission still ends `MissionDone`; its tour gets `mission_done` and Command goes back on deck.
- **Parent settle.** `store.settleFamily` runs in the transaction of every write that ends an order: `settleOrder` (done, failed, split; the tour's backstop included), `SettleOpenOrder` (a recalled mission's failure), `BelayOrder` and `BelaySplitOrder` (#146), `WithdrawOrder`. For an ended child it calls `settleSplit` on the parent; for an order just settled split, on itself (children that all ended before the report settle it then). `settleSplit` leaves anything not split, or with a child open, alone; otherwise:
  - every child done: done, "order done: its 2 child orders are done", kind `order` (the report's done entry stays the one push reads);
  - else failed, "order failed: child orders did not finish: ORD-0002 failed, ORD-0003 withdrawn", kind `fail`, payload `reason`, `code` `children_failed` (`store.CodeChildrenFailed`) and `not_done`.

  A settled parent checks its own parent in turn (a split child of a split order). Recall moves a child back to pool, still open, and settles nothing.
- **SettleOrder's result.** For the report's settle it returns `split`, what it wrote, even when the parent settled further in the same transaction. A re-run of `order-done` that finds the parent done or failed writes no late entry. That includes a parent the tour's backstop failed before a late report: its late work gets no entry.
- **A parent ended otherwise.** If Command's mission fails after `create-children` (`send-answer` out of retries, a crew error, the tour's backstop), the parent ends failed and its children run on; `settleSplit` leaves a parent that isn't split alone. `settleFamily` logs one entry on it in the ending transaction, "order ended with 1 open child orders, which run on: ORD-0003" (kind `order`, payload `open_children`). A belayed parent cascades instead (superseded by #146, "Cascade belay" below).
