- **Belay, recall, withdraw.** Recalling a split order is a bad request: "order ORD-0001 is split and its mission has ended; it waits on 2 open child orders: recall those instead, or belay it to end them all". Belaying one cascades (superseded by #146, "Cascade belay" below). Withdraw and route refuse it as "too late", as for any claimed order.
- **Counts and lists.** `Order.OpenChildren` is read with every order (a subquery in `orderCols` over a new index `orders (parent_id, state)`): children not done, failed, belayed or withdrawn. Status counts a split order neither open nor away, and never as a red alert. `OrderView` gains `open_children` (always present); `bmus order list` shows the state as "split (2 open)".
- **Console.** The snapshot carries split orders like open ones. `Views` adds the parent's view to a child's events: the count follows each child's change. The mission record lists a split order as "Split: waiting on 2 children" (blue chip), then its settled result; its children show in the open orders as any pool order.
- **Upgrade.** A new state value and one index (`CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS`, applied at `Open`); no column, `workflowVersion` unchanged. A mission still PENDING from before replays as it did: `create-children` runs only for `order_split` answers, which nothing raised before. An older binary doesn't know `split`; pre-release databases stay disposable.
- **Tests.** Store: the parent settles done in its last child's commit (the log hook's batch), failed naming failed, belayed and withdrawn children, a recalled child keeps it split, children ended before the report settle it at the report, `InsertChildOrders` re-runs write nothing. Bridge holodeck: the acceptance run under the default `regs.yaml` (captain approves, two children, Command on deck, status counts, recall refused, children run by `ops` and their own split hails denied, parent done); malformed splits denied with and without `regs.yaml`; a split decided by `default_option`, then countermanded, children kept, withdrawn, parent failed. `checkSplit` table test. A policy allow and a countermand to deny committed together before `create-children` create nothing and note the refusal. `create-children` run twice returns the same children after the roster lost their division, and a refusal run twice logs once. `splitQuestion` cases. Store: a parent failed with an open child logs it. The tour passes `--role command` (fake provider). combadge: `split_order` only for Command, its hail and results, zod refusals, `--role` checked at startup. Console: vitest for the record row and its count, Playwright approving a split from the page and withdrawing both children (`e2e/split.json`).

## Cascade belay (#146)

Dated 2026-10-30. Builds `phase-3-plan-2026-10-29.md` §8's cascade paragraph. Supersedes the belay half of #155's "A parent ended otherwise" and "Belay, recall, withdraw".

- **Where it runs.** In the transaction that settles the parent belayed. A split parent has no mission: `Daemon.BelayOrder` belays it at once (`belaySplit`, `store.BelaySplitOrder`: split to belayed, "order belayed[: <reason>]"). A parent with a mission (away, hailing, recalled and parked) is belayed as before: `BelayOrder` logs "belay requested" and queues the belay, and the mission's `order-belayed` step (`store.BelayOrder`) settles it and cascades. Any order with rows whose `parent_id` points at it cascades, split or not.
- **Not at request time for a parent with a mission.** The cascade runs with the parent's settle, not with its belay request. `create-children` may commit children after the request; the mission is sequential: by `order-belayed` they all exist, and none can be added after it. Children cascaded at request time and again at settle would get two belays.
- **Per child** (`store.cascadeBelay`, rowid order, all in the parent's transaction):
  - pool or directed, no parked mission: withdrawn, "order withdrawn: its parent ORD-0001 was belayed";
  - split (no mission): belayed, "order belayed: its parent ORD-0001 was belayed", then its own cascade. Nested splits recurse, each level logging its own entry;
  - away, hailing, or unclaimed with a parked mission: `store.BelayFunc` (`Daemon.belayChild`) sends its mission a synthetic belay with reason "its parent ORD-0001 was belayed" (`dbos.Send` with `WithSendTransaction`), logged on the child "belay requested: its parent ORD-0001 was belayed". The child's mission settles it, and cascades in turn;
  - no mission yet (`workflowLive` finds none: claimed, not started) is sent nothing: "not reached: its mission has not started". The cascade goes on, and the child runs on;
  - a mission that has ended (found, not live) would never read a belay: the cascade settles the child belayed itself, "order belayed: its parent ORD-0001 was belayed", and cascades to its children, as for a split child;
  - done, failed, belayed or withdrawn: left alone, "already done".
- **Log.** One entry on the parent, after the children's: "belay cascaded to 3 child orders: ORD-0002 already done, ORD-0003 belay requested, ORD-0004 withdrawn" (kind `order`, payload `children`: `[{id, state, action}]`, `store.ChildBelay`). An order with no children logs none.
- **Parent state.** Belayed, whatever its children end as. The parent leaves `split` before any child is touched, and `settleSplit` only moves an order still `split`: a child ending later (belayed, or done when its report beats the belay) never moves the parent to done or failed.
- **Idempotency.** `store.BelayOrder` cascades only when its call moved the parent: a re-run of `order-belayed` writes and sends nothing. A second belay of a belayed parent is "too late to belay", as for any belayed order. A child that settled on its own before the cascade is "already <state>"; one that settles after its belay was sent leaves its mission's belay unread or finds it done (`belayMission`'s existing path).
- **Orphan log.** `noteOrphans` now runs only for a parent that ends failed (mission failure, backstop): its children still run on. Never for a belayed one.
- **SettleOpenOrder.** Refuses `belayed` ("belay with BelayOrder"): every belay cascades.
- **Recall.** A split order still refuses recall: it has no mission or beam to recall. The captain recalls a child on its own.
- **Count.** `order.belay` and the console's `POST /api/orders/{id}/belay` answer `OrderBelayResult`: the `OrderView` fields plus `children`, the open children the belay reaches (as the request read them, or for a split parent those its cascade did not find already ended), and `belayed`, true when the call belayed the order itself.
- **CLI.** `bmus order belay` has no confirm prompt: the count is in its output, "order ORD-0001 belay requested, and its 2 open child orders", or "order ORD-0001 belayed, ..." for a split order the call belayed itself (`OrderBelayResult.belayed`; the state can't tell, as a mission may settle its order before the reply reads it).
- **Console.** The hail card's belay confirm reads "Belays ORD-0001 and its 2 open child orders. Belay them all?" when `open_children` is above 0 (`belayPrompt`). A split order's mission record row gets a belay control with the same confirm.
- **A belay during the report.** One queued while the mission pulls its report would be left unread once `order-done` settles the order split. `order-done` records `{split}` (an older `{}` reads not split). When split, `belayAfterSplit` drains `fromCrewTopic` with 1ms `Recv`s until one times out; DBOS records each, so a replay reads the same. A belay sent while the order was away committed before `order-done` (single writer): the drain sees it; a later one goes through `belaySplit`. With a belay, step `belay-split` runs `BelaySplitOrder` with its reason. Other drained messages are dropped. The mission still ends `MissionDone`: its report arrived.
- **Orders that already failed.** A parent that failed with open children (the orphan log above) isn't belayable afterwards ("too late to belay"), and nothing cascades from it. The captain belays or withdraws those children directly.
- **Tests.** Store (`cascade_test.go`): the acceptance (pool, away and done children; one commit; parent stays belayed after the away child settles; second belay writes nothing), a child's report beating its belay, a parent its last child settled first, an away parent's mission-path cascade with a child not reached, one whose mission ended and a re-run, nested splits. Bridge holodeck (`cascade_test.go`): the acceptance through real missions (the in-flight child belayed by its mission, its hail closed, the count 2), an away parent's cascade from `order-belayed`, and a belay sent while the parent's mission is held in `pull-work` (test hook `Config.pullWorkHook`), taken by `belay-split`. CLI: `belayLine`. Console: vitest for `belayPrompt` and the record row's control; Playwright belays a split order from its record row.

## Hail metrics (#156)

Dated 2026-10-30. Builds `phase-3-plan-2026-10-29.md` §9.

- **Store.** `store.HailMetrics(ctx, since)` reads `status, original_decided_by, created_at, decided_at, countermanded_at` from `hails WHERE created_at >= ?` in one query and counts in Go. `Total` is every hail created in the period, open and closed. A hail with a NULL `created_at` (from before #147) is left out.
- **Decided.** `status = decided` and `decided_at` set. Only `decideOpenHail` writes `decided_at`, in the update that sets `status = decided`, and nothing moves a hail out of `decided` (a countermand leaves both): the two agree, and the check takes both. Hails closed unanswered (`timed_out`: park, recall, belay, withdraw, order ended, the tour's backstop) and open ones count in `Total` only.
- **Deciders.** `original_decided_by` is one of `policy` (auto_allow, a malformed split's `deny-split`, the captain timeout's `default_option`), `command` and `captain`. `ByOriginalDecider` always has those three keys, 0 when none, plus any other value found. A timeout `hold` or `belay` decides nothing.
- **Auto-resolved share.** `(policy + command) / Decided`, as §9 has it. Malformed splits and timeout defaults are in it: the captain answered neither. `ByOriginalDecider` doesn't tell them from auto_allow; their `route` and `category` do.
- **Overrides.** `Countermanded / Decided`, `Countermanded` being decided hails with `countermanded_at` set. A hail countermanded twice counts once.
- **Time-to-answer.** `decided_at - created_at` over the decided hails (negative clamped to 0), the countermand's time not used. Median and p90 by nearest rank: the sorted values' `ceil(p/100 * n)`-th. The median of an even count is the lower middle value. No interpolation.
- **Time column.** `created_at` and `decided_at` are written in UTC as SQLite's text format (`2006-01-02 15:04:05.999999999+00:00`, trailing zeros trimmed). With one offset, `+` sorts below `.` and the digits: text order is time order; a bound in another zone isn't (a +01:00 bound at the same instant matched nothing in a probe). `since` is bound in UTC, and the comparison stays in SQL. No index on `created_at`: a week of hails is small.
- **No samples.** With `Decided` 0, `AutoResolvedShare` and `CaptainOverrides` report not ok and the percentiles are 0; the RPC writes `auto_resolved_share`, `captain_overrides`, `median_time_to_answer_ms` and `p90_time_to_answer_ms` as null.
- **Bridge.** `Daemon.HailMetrics(ctx, since)` returns `HailMetrics` (an alias of `store.HailMetrics`). RPC `hails.metrics`, args `{since}` (RFC 3339, default a week before the call: `DefaultMetricsPeriod`), result `HailsMetricsResult`: `since` (UTC), `total`, `decided`, `countermanded`, `by_original_decider`, the two rates as fractions and the two times in whole milliseconds.
- **CLI.** `bmus hails metrics [--since <d>] [--json]`, default `7d`. `--since` takes `time.ParseDuration`'s units plus a leading whole day count (`7d`, `1d12h`; a day is 24h), or an RFC 3339 time; zero and negative durations are refused. The CLI turns it into a time and sends that; `--json` prints the RPC's result unchanged. The table: since, hails, decided, one "first by" row per decider (policy, command, captain, then others by name), auto-resolved and captain overrides as "75.0% (6 of 8)", median and p90 as Go durations; `-` where null.
- **Console.** Nothing, as §9 says.
- **Tests.** Store: a week of hails by every decider path, two countermands, one timed out, one open, one with NULL `created_at`, one a nanosecond before `since`; the counts, share 0.75, override rate 0.25, median 1m and p90 20m by hand; the same result for a `since` in another zone; an empty period. `nearestRank` cases. RPC: nulls with nothing decided, the default week, a captain answer. CLI: `parseSince` cases, the table with and without samples, and `--json` equal to the RPC's reply on a countermanded timeout default.

## Hail parking (#154)

Dated 2026-11-02. Builds `phase-3-plan-2026-10-29.md` §7 in the sdk harness (`combadge/src/harness/sdk.ts`). Only what the implementation settled is recorded here.

- **Flag.** `serve --hail-park-after <duration>`, sdk only, into `HarnessOptions.hailParkAfterMs`; unset means 15m (`HAIL_PARK_AFTER_MS`). `parseDuration` takes Go-style `ms`, `s`, `m`, `h` units, decimals and sequences (`1h30m`); anything else, zero, or more than 2^31-1 ms (setTimeout's limit, about 596h) is refused at startup. The bridge passes it from `bmus bridge run --hail-park-after` (see "Hail parking flag (#180)").
- **Timer.** One per `tool_approval` or `clarifying_question` hail, started when it is emitted and cleared when it settles. The first to fire parks every hail held at that moment, younger ones included. In effect the threshold runs from the oldest held hail.
- **order_split.** Its hail starts no timer. A query that raised one never parks, before or after the split settles: an abort could cut off the split's result, and a resumed Command would ask to split again. The query's other hails hold until answered; a later query can park.
- **Running calls.** A call allowed through `canUseTool` (by a hail or an entry) counts as running from the allow to its PostToolUse or PostToolUseFailure hook; an allowed Agent/Task call counts for its whole subagent. A park that comes due meanwhile waits, then runs `RESULT_GRACE_MS` (5s) after the last such hook, with every condition checked again. Limit: 5s is a guess at the time the CLI needs to record the result, and with parallel calls the CLI may hold the result until its siblings finish; neither is tested. A call whose hook never fires holds off parking for the rest of that query.
- **The park.** Each query has its own `AbortController`, chained to the run's. The park records the held hails in `Park.refs`, closes the query's input, then aborts that controller. A parked query reporting no session id fails the run.
- **Per-query binding.** Each query gets its own `canUseTool` and combadge MCP server, bound to its controller. Once it is aborted, its calls take no entry and raise no hail, and `progress`, `report` and `request_handover` return an error and do nothing. Its messages are ignored from the abort on.
- **Wind-down.** The resume waits for the aborted query to end, at most `WIND_DOWN_MS` (30s) from the abort. Past that it logs "<order>: the parked query hasn't ended 30s after its abort; resuming without it" and resumes once the answers are in. The abandoned query's messages and calls are ignored as above; its hooks still run (transcript path, PostToolUse).
- **Resume.** Once every parked ref is answered, `drive()` builds the entries (`Grant`), then a new `Input` whose first message joins each answer's text with a blank line, then calls `query({resume})`. Corrections that arrive while parked follow that first message.
- **Wording.** A non-Bash call is named `<tool> <input as JSON>`. A deny with no text reads "no reason given" in the message; its entry denies with "denied by the captain", as an ordinary hail does. Trailing periods and spaces are dropped from a decision or answer the message quotes. A parked `AskUserQuestion` with several questions repeats the answers to its earlier questions ahead of the parked one's.
- **Entries.** `approvalKey` drops `description` for `Bash`, `Agent` and `Task`. Matching is `util.isDeepStrictEqual` on the key, plus the tool name. A `clarifying_question` leaves none. The expiry timer starts when the resumed query starts.
- **Stop while parked.** The resume's first message is the stop request, then one line per parked hail: its answer's usual text when answered, the "never answered" line for an unanswered `tool_approval`, nothing for an unanswered question. No entries are made, and the parked refs are dropped: a later answer logs as unknown. A stop arriving with the last answer, before the resume starts, takes this path too.
- **Logs.** Two lines on stderr: "parking <order>: N hail(s) held past Xs; the session resumes once all are answered" and "resuming <order> (session <id>) with the captain's answers" (or "the stop request").
- **Usage.** The aborted query sends no result. The report's usage is the resumed query's alone.
- **Tests.** `combadge/test/sdk.test.ts`, "sdk harness parking", on fake timers: one case per acceptance line of #154, plus a query with an `order_split` hail that never parks, a park waiting for a running call's PostToolUse, a refused hail from the aborted query's last turn, an abandoned query's late call, report and result, the aborted query's result during a stop while parked, an answer beating the timer, the wind-down backstop, a belay and a stop during the resume's startup, a failed resume, and a late answer after a stop. "serve --harness sdk" covers the flag's refusals and `parseDuration`.

## Hail parking flag (#180)

Dated 2026-11-04.

- **Flag.** `bmus bridge run --hail-park-after <duration>` sets `bridge.Config.HailParkAfter`, carried into every beam's `CombadgeSpec.HailParkAfter`. Unset (0) passes no flag, so serve's 15m default applies.
- **sdk only.** The transporter adds `--hail-park-after <d.String()>` after `--harness`/`--role` only when the harness serve ends up running is sdk: the last `--harness` among the spec's, the provider's configured flags (holodeck's `--harness holodeck --script`) and `CombadgeSpec.Args`, else sdk (`servedHarness`). serve refuses the flag with any other harness.
- **Limits.** `CheckHailParkAfter` refuses zero, negative, under 1ms and over `MaxHailParkAfter` (2^31-1 ms, printed as 596h31m23.647s), at flag parsing and in `bridge.Run`. The cap matches combadge's `MAX_TIMEOUT_MS`; under 1ms Go prints microseconds or nanoseconds, which `parseDuration` doesn't take.
- **Restarts.** A running serve keeps its value across a bridge restart, on both providers. On tsh, so does a serve `beamctl restart` revives, since it reruns serve's original command. On holodeck, a restarted serve gets the bridge's current value.
- **Tests.** `TestServeArgsHailParkAfter` and `TestHolodeckHailParkAfter` (a real sdk serve takes `2m0s`; echo gets none), `TestProvisionPassesHailParkAfter`, `TestRunRefusesBadHailParkAfter`, `TestBridgeRunRefusesBadHailParkAfter` (`600h` names the cap), and combadge's "parses Go's time.Duration.String() output, up to the cap".

## Hello before the first claim (#173)

Dated 2026-11-04.

- **Order.** The tour provisions, starts the pump, then waits for its beam's `hello` (`awaitHello`) before the first `claim-order`. serve sends `hello` after each attach's replay, so it is the first sign an order can reach a live combadge.
- **Signal.** `Sink.CrewHello`, called from the pump's `case TypeHello`. `pumpSink` sends `crew_hello` with the pump's beam, by `dbos.Send` in the inbox transaction, as `CrewError` does. The pump package still knows nothing of the bridge.
- **The wait.** A durable `Recv` loop after a recorded `hello-deadline` step. A `crew_hello` for the tour's beam ends it, and `hello-logged` writes "crew on deck: combadge said hello". One naming another beam is ignored. `heartbeat_missed` for the beam ends the tour as beam lost, `dismissed` dismisses it, and `crew_error` is logged. `order_assigned` is dropped: the first claim finds the order.
- **No status change.** The crew row stays `on_deck` with the `current_beam` `provisionBeam` set, which `ensurePumps` needs after a restart. The tour is the only caller of `claimOrder`, so the wait is the gate. `provisionBeam` now logs "beam <alias> up, combadge started".
- **Bound.** A serve that dies before its `hello` is caught by the pump's sickbay check (stale heartbeats, failed probe), which sends `heartbeat_missed`; the wait adds nothing for it. A serve that runs but never lets an attach through probes healthy, and the pump only recycles. For that the wait gives up after `defaultHelloTimeout`, 10m, and ends the tour in sickbay with code `no_hello` (`Config.helloTimeout` in tests). #172's exit, for a serve with no attach after a failed check, also comes after 10m.
- **Later hellos.** The main loop, `watchChange` and `settleWait` drop `crew_hello` without a log line. serve says hello on every attach, including the pump's reconnects.
- **Restart.** A bridge restart mid-wait resumes the tour in its `Recv` with the recorded deadline. `ensurePumps` re-attaches, and serve's hello on that attach ends the wait.
- **Downtime past the deadline.** A bridge down longer than the wait's remainder (a laptop asleep) resumes a `Recv` that times out at once. On a timeout the `hello-extend` step checks whether the daemon started less than `helloTimeout` ago. If so it records a fresh deadline and the wait goes on, so the re-attach's hello can still end it. A daemon grants this at most once per tour, because its next timeout comes at least `helloTimeout` after it started.
- **#172.** #172 landed second and added the `harness_check_failed` case to `awaitHello`: the crew error is logged, then ends the tour in sickbay (see "canUseTool startup check (#172)").
- **Consults.** The gate covers orders only. A consult to a Command whose serve hasn't said hello relies on the consult timeout.
- **Upgrade.** The tour's steps change after `start-pump`, and `workflowVersion` stays `"1"`. A tour still PENDING from before replays into `hello-deadline` where its history has `claim-order`, and DBOS v1.7.0 refuses that (`ErrorCodeUnexpectedStep`). The operator sees the tour end in ERROR, and the crew row is left `on_deck` or `away` with no tour behind it. To recover, dismiss and re-add the crew member, or start on a fresh database. Pre-release databases stay disposable, so the upgrade does nothing more.
- **Tests.** The fake transporter's sessions now send a `hello` (seq since+1) on every attach. `internal/bridge/hello_test.go`, on holodeck, with `attachGate` (a `tunePump` that refuses attaches until released): a late hello holds a directed order back; a serve killed before its hello ends in sickbay with no mission; a gate never opened ends in sickbay as `no_hello`; a bridge stopped past the deadline gets `hello-extend` and claims on the new bridge's hello; a reconnect's second hello logs no tour warning and the hailing mission runs on; a restart mid-wait claims on the new bridge's hello; after a watch change, tour 1 resumes the parked order only after its own beam's hello.

## Regulations obfuscations (#157)

Dated 2026-11-04. Extends "Regulations engine (#148)". The rule stays fail closed: a spelling the engine can't resolve goes to the captain, not to Command.

- **Existing files.** `bmus regs init` writes the default once. A `regs.yaml` written before #157 keeps #148's rules until it is removed and re-initialized, or merged by hand.
- **Subjects.** `classify` and `captain_only` regexes now read four forms of the text:
  - normalized;
  - de-quoted;
  - expansions stripped, then de-quoted. `$(...)`, `${...}` and `$[...]` are removed whole, nested ones included (`g$(echo $(true))it`). `$''` joins `$""`. An unclosed one stays.
  - resolved, then stripped and de-quoted. This form is new. Variables the text assigns literally are filled in (`x=sh; git pu$x`, `export`, `+=`, values naming other variables a few levels deep), and the last assignment wins. A `for` or `select` over literal words binds its variable to all of them, a space apart.
    - `${x:-w}` and `${x=w}` read as `w` unless `x` is assigned a non-empty value (any value, without the colon).
    - `${x:+w}` and `${x+w}` read as `w` when `x` is assigned a non-empty value (any value, without the colon), or not assigned at all, since the environment may set it. An `x` assigned empty reads as nothing.
    - Resolving past 1 MB is "too large to classify".
- **auto_allow reads the command as written.** Its `Tag` rules see only tags from `match` rules, run over the collapsed `Command` and the raw `Values`, with no folding, de-quoting, stripping or resolving. `command` and `opaque` rules never tag for auto_allow, as #148 intended. They read a parsed, de-quoted command that skips wrappers, paths and assignments, so `sudo git status`, `./git status` or `env LD_PRELOAD=x git status` would fit a `^git status` tag. A `Tag` auto_allow rule needs its tag first in both passes, the hail's and auto_allow's. Otherwise a `command` or `opaque` rule listed first, left out of the auto pass, would let a later `match` rule's tag allow `git push`. A view that can only add matches must not widen auto_allow: `git ${x:-status}` doesn't fit a `^git status$` tag, and `NOTES` spelled with a Cyrillic U+041E doesn't fit `NOTES`.
- **Command words.** `shellCommands` (`internal/regs/opaque.go`) splits a command into simple commands, those in `$(...)`, backticks and unquoted heredoc bodies included, with a small quote-aware scanner. It is not a shell parser. A simple command's command word is its first word once these are skipped:
  - assignments;
  - keywords (`if`, `then`, `do`, `!`, `{`, `function NAME`, ...);
  - wrappers, with their options, the options' values and fixed arguments: `env`, `command`, `builtin`, `exec`, `nohup`, `xargs`, `sudo`, `doas`, `nice`, `time`, `timeout`, `stdbuf`, `setsid`, `busybox`, `strace`, `ltrace`, `watch`, `flock`, `ionice`, `chroot`, `unshare`, `nsenter`, `chrt`, `taskset`.

  Each wrapper is also a command of its own, with the rest of its words, so `flock ... -c` and `watch` can be read. `env -S` and `--split-string` strings are split into commands. A `find`'s `-exec`, `-execdir`, `-ok` and `-okdir` start one more command, ending at `;`, `+` or the next `-exec`/`-ok`; a `find` inside one isn't split again, so a chain of them stays linear. Past 65536 commands the command is opaque. `for`, `select` and `case` lines run none. The scanner skips comments, quoted heredoc bodies and arithmetic (`((n=1<<3))`, `$((..))`), and reads `case` patterns, nested backticks (`` \` ``) and quotes inside `${..}`.
- **Command rules.** A classify rule's `command` regex reads each simple command as its command word's basename and then its arguments, de-quoted, a space apart. The default's shell, script, `source` and `go` rules use it, so `|sh>/dev/null`, `bash<x.sh`, `(./x.sh)`, `if ./x.sh`, `time . x` and `sudo -u me ./x.sh` are caught. `which sh`, `echo bash`, `grep sh file`, `man bash` and `git commit -m "Run tests in bash"` stay with Command.
- **Opaque commands.** A classify rule may set `opaque: true`, and with it `opaque_args`, a regex over a command word's basename. It fits when any of these holds:
  - a command word holds an expansion, an unquoted glob (`gi?`, `g[i]t`, extglob `@(..)`) or a brace expansion (`{gi,}t`). An expansion only in double-quoted directories (`"$GOROOT"/bin/go`, `"$(go env GOROOT)/bin/gofmt"`) doesn't count. Unquoted (`$GOROOT/bin/go`) it does, since word splitting could run anything.
  - an argument holds an unresolvable expansion, in a simple command whose basename `opaque_args` matches, or one run by `xargs` or `env -S`. An expansion is unresolvable when its variable is bound some other way than a literal assignment or a literal `for` list (`read`, `mapfile`, `printf -v`, `getopts`, `let`, a nameref, a `for` over a glob or expansion, `$(...)` or backticks, `set --`, arrays), or when it uses an operator resolve doesn't apply (`/`, `%`, `#`, `:N`, `^`, `,`, `!`, subscripts, `${#x}`). The binding is found anywhere in the command; the use counts only in those arguments. Substitutions themselves and variables bound nowhere in the command don't count.
  - `xargs` runs a command `opaque_args` matches with no visible subcommand (`xargs git`, `xargs rm`, `xargs sh`): its arguments come from stdin.

  - an argument holds an unquoted `$(...)` or backtick substitution, in a simple command whose basename `opaque_split_args` matches. Its output splits into words, options included (`rm $(printf -- -%s rf) x`).

  The default's `opaque_args` is `git`, `find`, `go`/`go*`, `rm`, the shells, `source` and `.`. Its `opaque_split_args` is `rm` and `find`. Leaving `go` out keeps `go test $(go list ./...)` with Command.
- **Rule scope.** `command` and `opaque` read only `Command`. `CheckRegs` rejects them on a tool with no command (`Write`, `Edit`, `Read`, ...). The default tags `opaque` on `Bash` and `Monitor`, and also a `git` whose subcommand is an expansion (`git $(printf '\x70ush')`). `captain_only` lists `tag: opaque` last, so earlier indexes keep their numbers.
- **Prose.** Questions and answers see every `match` rule, whatever its tool, as #148 has it, so an answer quoting `python -c` or `sh -c` still escalates. `command` and `opaque` rules skip them: "Should I rewrite this as a bash script?" stays with Command.
- **Aliases.** Defining an alias is tagged `opaque` on any tool: `git config ... alias.x` or `git -c alias.x=...`, `GIT_CONFIG_*=alias.x`, a `[alias]` section in a written file, `alias x=...`. Using an alias (`git p`) can't be read, so the definition goes to the captain.
- **Default tags.** `destructive`, on any tool:
  - `find` with `-delete`, or with `-exec`/`-ok` (and `dir` forms) running `rm`, `unlink` or `shred`, also behind `sudo`, `env` and the like;
  - `git clean` with any short option cluster holding `f` (`-fdx`, `-xdf`, `-f -d -x`) or `--f...`, and `clean.requireForce` anywhere, so `-c`, `GIT_CONFIG_KEY_n` and `GIT_CONFIG_PARAMETERS` count too;
  - `git reset` with `--ha...`.

  `external_effect`, on `Bash` and `Monitor`, as command rules:
  - a shell as the command (`sh`, `bash`, `dash`, `ksh`, `zsh`, `csh`, `tcsh`, `fish`, `ash`, `mksh`, `rbash`, `yash`, `posh`, `busybox ash`), whatever follows;
  - `source` and `.`;
  - `su`, `runuser`, `flock` and `script` with `-c` (or `--command`), and `watch`, which run a string through a shell; `sudo -s`/`-i` and `doas -s`;
  - a `*.sh` (or `.bash`, `.zsh`, ...) file as the command;
  - `go` or a versioned `go1.22.0` with `-exec`, `-toolexec` or `-vettool`, in `-`, `--` and `=` forms, including inside `go env -w GOFLAGS=`. A match rule also catches `GOFLAGS=` set with spaces, quoted or exported. A user's `go test .*` auto_allow can't run an arbitrary program through them.

  Scripts run by another interpreter (`python x.py`) still go to Command.
- **Confusables.** `fold` maps common Cyrillic, Greek and Latin lookalikes NFKC leaves alone (U+0435, U+0456, U+03BF, U+03C1, U+0131, U+0261, ...) to the ASCII letter they resemble. It runs after NFKC, in every subject but auto_allow's, so "delete" spelled with a Cyrillic U+0435 reads as `delete`. A table is simpler than treating any non-ASCII letter as captain-only, which would send every non-English answer to the captain.
- **Route changes.** Commands that now go to the captain instead of Command:
  - an unquoted variable or substitution in the command word (`$GOROOT/bin/go vet`);
  - an unresolvable expansion in an argument of git, find, go, rm, a shell or `source`: `n=$(nproc); go test -p $n ./...`, `for p in $(go list ./...); do go vet $p; done`, `rm ${x%/}`;
  - an unquoted substitution in an argument of rm or find (`rm $(ls *.tmp)`; quoted, `rm "$(ls *.tmp)"` keeps its route);
  - an expansion as git's subcommand (`git $x`, `git "$sub"`);
  - `xargs` running git, find, go, rm or a shell without a visible subcommand (`git ls-files | xargs rm`);
  - `./build.sh`, `source .venv/bin/activate` and `watch ...`;
  - any shell run as a command, `-c` or not.

  `TestClassifyDefault` pins the other side, and these keep their routes: `go test ./...`, `ls *.go`, `git status`, `go test -run 'A|B.*'`, `find ... -exec gofmt`, `git clean -n`, `git reset --soft`, heredoc commit messages, `"$(go env GOROOT)/bin/gofmt"` and the prose above. So do `x=$(date); echo $x`, `for f in *.go; do gofmt -l "$f"; done`, `${f%.go}`, `${#arr[@]}`, `while read -r line; do echo "$line"; done`, `git commit -m "$msg"` and `xargs -0 git add`.
- **Known gaps.** These still go to Command. "Regulations gaps (#188)" closed the rest of #157's list.
  - a glob in an argument (`touch push; git pu?h`), or a substitution in an argument of anything but `rm`, `find`, `go` and git's subcommand (`git -C . commit $(printf -- --amend)`). Counting a substitution in git's arguments would send `git commit -m "$(cat msg)"` to the captain;
  - a variable bound nowhere in the command, in an argument of anything but `rm`, `find` and `go` (`git -C . commit $x`), or inside a quoted argument it doesn't start (`rm -f "/tmp/$x"`). Counting it would send `git commit -m "$msg"` to the captain;
  - scripts run by an interpreter or a build tool (`python3 x.sh`, `make`, `npm run`, `go generate`);
  - git settings written to a config file, or a hook written under `.git/hooks`, then used by a plain git command;
  - `git checkout x.go`: a lone path without `--` reads as a branch;
  - on a tool other than `Bash` and `Monitor`, or in a question or answer, an ANSI-C string after a quote in a comment or heredoc body that a later `$` then quote pairs with (`# don't 'a$'`, then `git $'\x70ush'`): both decodings lose step there. Finding the strings with `shellCommands` would need offsets into the text its nested scanners don't keep. On `Bash` and `Monitor`, an `ansi_c` rule tags any `$'` bash decodes, wherever the shell parse finds it (#200);
  - other git commands that drop history or refs (`git reflog expire`, `git update-ref -d`, `git worktree remove --force`);
  - a variable bound by `printf -v`, `read` or a bare `export x` (no `=`), outside the arguments of the commands `opaque_args` lists: its value isn't seen, and `opaque_split_args` reads it as unbound;
  - a wrapper option with a value the tables don't list;
  - aliases and variables set outside the command (in `~/.gitconfig` before bmus, or in the environment);
  - lookalikes outside the table (Armenian, Cherokee, ...).

## canUseTool startup check (#172)

Dated 2026-11-04, revised 2026-11-05 after review. Checks at serve startup what `spike-hail-parking-2026-11-02.md` Q0 found on claude 2.1.258: a PreToolUse `ask` reaches `canUseTool` under bypassPermissions.

- **The check.** `checkAsk(query, claudePath, model?, {timeoutMs, cwd})` in `combadge/src/harness/sdk.ts`, run as the sdk harness's `selfCheck`. One query, prompted to ask one question with AskUserQuestion, then to run `true` with Bash.
- **Options.** The order query's where safe: bypassPermissions, `settingSources` unset (the CLI then loads every source, local included), the in-process tools' names in `allowedTools`, every built-in tool, `gitPushPolicy` and `askPolicy` on AskUserQuestion, cwd the work dir, the SDK's default model unless `--check-model`. It differs in: no system prompt preset, no MCP servers (`strictMcpConfig`), `maxTurns` 2, and a catch-all hook (`checkProbes`) that denies every call but the two probes and ToolSearch (a CLI may defer AskUserQuestion behind it).
- **Session.** `persistSession` is unset, as for an order: each check leaves a session under `~/.claude/projects` on the beam.
- **Decisive probe.** AskUserQuestion through the production `askPolicy`, the path every live hail takes today (`clarifying_question`). Pass: `canUseTool` saw an AskUserQuestion call, hooked or not. Fail: `askPolicy` fired, and a PostToolUse (or PostToolUseFailure) with the hooked call's tool_use_id fired, or the query ended, without `canUseTool`. Inconclusive: no claude, `askPolicy` never fired, or the query threw. A pass or a run tool decides even if the query then throws. The reason names the init message's `claude_code_version`.
- **Secondary probe.** A hook `ask` on Bash `true`, the path `tool_approval` hails will take, graded the same way into `SelfCheck.notes`. Its `canUseTool` deny interrupts only once the question was seen, so an agent that runs Bash first still gets to the question. serve logs it ("harness check: Bash hook ask: ..."); it never fails the check.
- **Covers.** Whether this CLI, with the beam's user settings, routes askPolicy's ask on AskUserQuestion to `canUseTool` under bypassPermissions, on the check's model.
- **Doesn't cover.** An order repo's project settings (cwd is the work dir), the `claude_code` preset, MCP and in-process tools, another order's model, parked hails' resume path, and a CLI changed after startup.
- **Timeout.** `CHECK_TIMEOUT_MS`, 2m like a consult: the query is aborted and the check is inconclusive.
- **Serve.** `Harness.selfCheck?()` runs once in `start()`. Each attach's handshake, and commands after it, wait until the check settles; on a fail the error is in the outbox before any replay. The socket opens at once, because holodeck's `StartCombadge` waits only 10s for it.
- **On fail.** serve emits `error {code: "harness_check_failed", reason}` with no order, sends no `hello`, drops every command, and resolves `Server.ended` once an attach has drained its replay, or `CHECK_FAILED_EXIT_MS` (10m) after the check. `main` then closes and exits 1. Pass and inconclusive are logged ("harness check passed", "harness check inconclusive; going on: ...").
- **Exit after the drain.** If the pump recycled that attach before reading the error, the tour sees the exit as beam lost. The real reason is then only in serve's stderr.
- **Exit after 10m.** A bridge down past the 10m sees the same: beam lost, the reason only in serve's stderr.
- **Flag.** `--check-model`, sdk only, into `HarnessOptions.checkModel`. The bridge passes none; `--combadge-arg` can.
- **Bridge.** `CodeHarnessCheckFailed`. `pumpSink.CrewError` now sets `CrewSignal.Beam`. A crew error with that code from the tour's beam is logged by `logCrewError`, then `harnessCheckFailed` ends the tour: in the main loop, in `watchChange`'s stand-down wait and in `awaitHello`. One from another beam is logged and ignored, as `heartbeat_missed` is (`checkFailedOn`). It shares `lostToSickbay` with `beamLost`: a running mission gets `beam_lost` with "harness check failed: <reason>" and parks; the crew member goes to sickbay with the error's reason and code `harness_check_failed`.
- **Tests.** `combadge/test/sdk.test.ts`: "sdk startup check" (options, the catch-all leaving AskUserQuestion and ToolSearch alone, Bash before the question, an unhooked AskUserQuestion call and another call's PostToolUse, `--check-model`, both fails, a fail despite a later error, AskUserQuestion deciding alone with the Bash note in every state, the inconclusive cases, the timeout) and "serve's startup check (#172)" in-process with a fake query (hello after a pass and after an inconclusive check, an attach held while the check runs and its queued order run after the pass, the fail's outbox and replay with no hello and `ended`, dropped commands, the 10m exit on fake timers). The bundle: `--check-model` refused with holodeck, and passed to a fake claude as `--model=`. Go: `internal/bridge/checkfail_test.go`, on holodeck, with `checkFail` (a `tunePump` turning each hello into the error once armed): before the hello, idle in the main loop (with a stale beam's error ignored first), and mid-mission (the order parks). `TestHolodeckHailParkAfter` and `TestBridgeRunPassesHailParkAfter` now give their sdk serves a claude that exits 1, so the check makes no model call.
- **Not tested.** The `watchChange` case. The real CLI: on a beam with claude 2.1.258 the check should pass.

## Corrections for parked orders (#167)

Dated 2026-11-05. Follows "Countermand (#153)".

- **Held.** A correction that reaches the mission while parked, stopping (a stand_down or recall went out) or off the beam (ship-repos found the tour ended) is held in `heldCorrections`, the latest per hail. Stopping now holds rather than drops: what follows it is a parked order. Only recorded `Recv` results fill it, so a replay rebuilds it.
- **Resume.** Right after a successful `send-order`, `deliverHeld` sends each held correction with `send-correction` (`sendRetries`), in hail order (by number). Each leaves the list as it is taken, so a step failing partway leaves only the rest to log as not delivered. serve queues them behind the order until the run registers.
- **Other crew member.** A held correction whose hailer isn't the crew member the order resumes on (a recalled order claimed elsewhere) is logged "not delivered: the order resumed on <crew>, not <hailer>". The rule matches the running case: a correction goes to the crew member that hailed.
- **Never resumed.** When the mission ends with corrections still held (belayed, failed, or done by a report that beat the stop), `missionWorkflow` logs each "not delivered: the order was <status> before it resumed" before telling the tour. Withdraw doesn't arise: an order with a parked mission is belayed, not withdrawn.
- **Views.** `OrderView.parked_at` (UTC, omitted when unset), also in `web/src/api.ts`.
- **Notes.** The RPC note, and the console card and its confirm prompt, say "order ORD-0001 is parked: the agent is told on resume" for an away or hailing order with `parked_at` whose assignee hailed. Parked on another crew member, it says "is parked on <assignee>: the agent is told only if <hailer> resumes it"; recalled (pool or directed, parked), "is recalled: the agent is told only if <hailer> resumes it". An ended mission keeps the "no live run" note.
- **Upgrade.** A mission still pending from before this change that dropped a correction while parked, stopping or off the beam replays into different steps (it now holds where `log-correction-dropped` is recorded), and DBOS refuses it. Pre-release databases stay disposable, as in #173.
- **Tests.** `parked_correction_test.go`, on holodeck with the order parked by beam loss (sickbay): two countermands while parked deliver only the last, as the re-energized serve's first delivery, right after the resume's `send-order`; a variant restarts the bridge once both are held, and the replay rebuilds them; a belay while parked logs the held correction as not delivered; a recall resumed on tuvok logs sorell's as not delivered and sends nothing. `TestHailCountermandWhileStopping` now finds the correction after the tour resumes the order it parked. Vitest cases cover the parked, parked-elsewhere and recalled notes on the card.

## Finished destinations (#160)

Dated 2026-11-05. Supersedes the "Finished hail workflows" line of "Consult reply routing (#151)".

- **Sentinels.** `comms.ErrUndeliverable` now means a destination that doesn't exist yet, and keeps its retry for `UndeliverableAfter`. `comms.ErrGone` means one that has finished. The pump logs it `undeliverable` (the message as payload), records the inbox row and acks at once, since a finished workflow never becomes live again.
- **Check.** `pumpSink` sends through `sendLive`: `workflowLive`, then `dbos.Send` on the inbox transaction. The check runs while that transaction holds SQLite's write lock (`_txlock=immediate`), so a workflow can't start or finish between the check and the commit.
- **Live.** `workflowLive` treats PENDING, ENQUEUED and DELAYED as live, a mission in recovery included. SUCCESS, ERROR, CANCELLED and MAX_RECOVERY_ATTEMPTS_EXCEEDED are finished. Missions, tours and hails register with `WithMaxRecoveryAttempts(-1)`, so they never reach the last.
- **Window.** A mission that timed out and is still running `failMission`'s steps is PENDING, so a late report in that window is accepted and never read. The window is small, and a belay is covered by `awaitBelayed`.
- **Missions and hails.** `ToMission` and `ToHail` return `ErrGone` for a finished mission or hail workflow.
- **Crew errors.** `CrewError` returns `ErrGone` for an ended tour, so a crew-level error is logged rather than lost. It keeps #172's `Beam`.
- **Hellos.** `CrewHello` to an ended tour is dropped with no log entry, since serve says hello on every attach.
- **Heartbeats.** `HeartbeatMissed` is unchanged, since it has no inbound seq to record and an ended tour has no one to put in sickbay.
- **Captain's log.** The pump's `undeliverable` entry, with the order id and the full message, is the only record. The order keeps the status its mission left.
- **Errors.** The sentinel is wrapped with the bare workflow id: "comms: message destination has finished: order/ORD-0001".
- **Tests.** `TestPumpSkipsGoneAtOnce` (comms) and `TestPumpSkipsFinishedMission` (bridge, a real pump on DBOS) set `UndeliverableAfter` to 1h and check that the next message is delivered without a reconnect. `TestPumpSkipsUndeliverableAfterTimeout` still covers the retry. `TestPumpSinkSendIsAtomicWithInbox` checks `CrewError` (`ErrGone`) and `CrewHello` (nil, its inbox row recorded) on a dismissed tour.

## Tour ending before send-order (#195)

Dated 2026-11-07. Follows "Watch change, park and resume (#100)" and "Corrections for parked orders (#167)".

- **Problem.** A tour that ended between `ship-repos` and `send-order` left `send-order` retrying with no pump for about 2m45s, then failing the order `send_failed`, though the tour's `beam_lost` was already queued.
- **Check in the step.** `send-order` (`sendOrderStep`) checks the tour's end mark (`tourEnded`) and the crew row (`tourOver`) before every attempt, as `ship-repos` does. A retry's wait also wakes on the mark. Either way the step records `orderNotSent` (0) and sends nothing. `endTour` marks the tour after queueing the mission's message and before `StopPump`, so the mark is there by the time the pump is gone.
- **Crew row.** The mark lives in memory, so a bridge restart after the tour finished loses it. `tourOver` then reads the tour as ended when the crew member is in sickbay, dismissed, or on a later `tour_no` than the tour behind `TourID`. Both `send-order` and `ship-repos` use it. The later-tour case was missing before #195 too: a crew member re-energized before the step re-ran got the order on its new tour's beam. An empty `TourID` (a mission run outside a tour) goes by the status only.
- **Retries.** The step keeps `sendRetries`' schedule (16 attempts, 500ms doubling to 15s) but loops itself instead of using DBOS step retries, so the wait can wake on the mark. A pump that is only reconnecting is still retried, and each retry logs a warning. Out of attempts, the order fails `send_failed` as before, with the last attempt's error in the reason rather than DBOS's joined list.
- **After it.** With 0 recorded the mission treats the order as off the beam, as after `ship-repos`' `TourEnded`. No held correction is sent. The main loop then reads the tour's queued message and handles it as usual: `beam_lost` parks, `dismissed` fails. A correction read meanwhile is held (#167). The new path reads no message itself, so none can be lost.
- **Why not a Recv.** A short `Recv` after a failed attempt would consume the tour's message and have to repeat the main loop's handling. The mark is the signal `ship-repos` already trusts, and the step's recorded result decides on replay.
- **Replay.** The step name and its `uint64` result are unchanged. Command ids are microsecond clock values, never 0, so a mission recorded before this change replays as it ran. A step cut off by a restart re-runs, since nothing was recorded. Nothing breaks.
- **Hook.** `Config.sendOrderHook`, called before the step's first attempt, for tests.
- **Tests.** `TestTourEndsBeforeSendOrder` (`sendorder_test.go`, holodeck): `kill` kills serve from the hook, so `send-order` retries until the tour ends in sickbay; `check_failed` has serve fail its harness check (#172) and starts `send-order` once the tour is marked. Both park ("order parked: ...") within 5s of the tour's mark, a third of the retry wait's cap. Both also record 0 for `send-order`, have no `order-failed` or backstop, and run to done after re-energize. `TestSendOrderTourOverWithoutMark` calls `sendOrderStep` and `shipRepos` on a daemon that never saw the mark, which is the state after a restart. With the crew member in sickbay, dismissed or on a later tour, both read the tour as ended. On its own tour `send-order` keeps retrying. No test restarts a real bridge for this. `TestHarnessCheckFailedMidMission` no longer waits for `send-order` before arming.

## Corrections carry the hail's question (#190)

Dated 2026-11-07. Follows "Countermand (#153)" and "Corrections for parked orders (#167)".

- **Problem.** The sdk harness names a correction's question from the answer the run received for that hail. A held correction reaches a resumed run that never got that answer, so it read "overrides the earlier answer to hail H-0001" with no question. An answer read while parked is dropped, yet its countermand is held and delivered.
- **Protocol.** `CorrectionBody` gains an optional `question` (`omitempty` in Go, optional in `protocol.ts`, `minLength` 1 in the schema). The golden `correction.json` carries one. A body without it still validates, and combadge doesn't validate inbound bodies at runtime, so an older combadge ignores the field.
- **Where it is filled.** `send-correction` (`sendCorrection`) reads the hail row and sets `question` before it sends. Every correction goes through that step, live ones and those `deliverHeld` sends on resume, so all carry it. `CountermandHail` still queues the body without a question. The question is optional: a failed read of the row is logged as a warning and the correction goes without one, so only the send spends `sendRetries`.
- **Replay.** The row is read inside the step, and the step name and its `uint64` result are unchanged. A recorded send replays without reading the row again, so missions from before this change replay as they ran.
- **Text.** A run that received the answer keeps "The captain overrides the earlier answer to '<its question>': <note>.", even when the correction carries a `question`. A run without one uses the correction's `question`: "The captain's answer to '<question>' is now: <note>. It replaces any earlier answer to it.". That run may never have read the first answer (an answer read while parked is dropped), so the text doesn't say it did. The agent's prompt says nothing of corrections, so the second sentence tells it this replaces an answer. With neither, the hail id is named as before.
- **Tests.** `TestCountermandParkedDeliveredOnResume` checks that the resumed run's correction names H-0001's question; the countermand tests in `consult_test.go` check it on live corrections. Vitest cases check the text for a correction with a `question` for a hail the run never answered, and for one the run did answer (its own question wins). The golden test validates the correction golden without `question` and rejects it with an empty one.

## Regulations gaps (#188)

Dated 2026-11-07. Extends "Regulations obfuscations (#157)" and decides each gap it left. The rule stays fail closed; a gap is kept only where covering it would hail the captain on common work.

- **Unbound variables.** `opaque_split_args` now also counts a variable the command doesn't assign a literal value (or loop over a literal list), and a substitution, when it is unquoted or starts the argument: `rm $X /tmp/x`, `find . "$X"`, `go test $x ./...`, `go test ./... -run $T`. The positional parameters and `$"..."` count; `$$`, `$?`, `$!`, `$#`, `${#x}`, arithmetic and ANSI-C strings don't. So don't `HOME`, `TMPDIR`, `PWD`, `GOPATH`, `GOBIN`, `GOROOT`, `GOCACHE`, `GOMODCACHE` and `USER` unless the command assigns them, nor a `for` variable whose list is a substitution that only lists Go packages (`for p in $(go list ./...)`). A quoted variable inside a word (`rm -f "/tmp/$name"`, `-ldflags "-X v=$V"`) keeps its route. Either form may split into words or start with `-`, and go reads flags after the packages too, so only quoting can narrow it.
- **Substitutions in go.** The default's `opaque_split_args` is now `rm`, `find` and `go`/`go*`, so `go test $(cat flags)` and `go test "$(printf -- -toolexec=x)"` go to the captain. A substitution that only lists Go packages doesn't count: `go list` with package patterns and only `-e`, `-deps`, `-find`, `-tags=` or `-mod=`, piped through greps that only filter lines (`-v`, `-E`, `-F`, `-i`, `-w`, `-x`, `-e`, one pattern, no file, no `-o`), in `$(...)` or backticks. `go test $(go list ./...)` and `go test $(go list ./... | grep -v /e2e)` keep #148's route. Import paths never start with `-`.
- **Resolved command rules.** `command` rules also read the command with its own literal variables filled in (resolve), so `o=-toolexec=./e; go test $o` and `x=.; git checkout $x` read as written out. A literal `for` list reads as all its words, as in the match view: `for b in a b; do git checkout $b; done` reads as `git checkout a b` and goes to the captain.
- **git settings that run programs.** Two `match` rules on any tool tag `external_effect`. Keys: `core.pager`, `core.editor`, `core.fsmonitor`, `core.hooksPath`, `core.sshCommand`, `core.askPass`, `core.gitProxy`, `sequence.editor`, `diff.external`, `diff.*.command`/`textconv`, `filter.*.clean`/`smudge`/`process`, `merge.*.driver`, `difftool`/`mergetool`/`man`/`browser` `.*.cmd`/`path`, `pager.*`, `credential.helper`, `gpg.program`, `uploadpack.packObjectsHook`, `remote.*.uploadpack`/`receivepack`, `include.path`, `includeIf.*.path`, `protocol.*allow`. Variables: `GIT_PAGER`, `GIT_EDITOR`, `GIT_SEQUENCE_EDITOR`, `GIT_EXTERNAL_DIFF`, `GIT_SSH`, `GIT_SSH_COMMAND`, `GIT_ASKPASS`, `GIT_PROXY_COMMAND`, `PAGER`, `EDITOR`, `VISUAL`, `SSH_ASKPASS`.
  - A key set with `-c`, or a variable assigned with `=`, counts unless its value is `cat`, `true`, `false`, `:` or empty, unquoted. `GIT_EDITOR=true git rebase --continue` and `git -c core.pager=cat log` keep their routes. A quoted or escaped value counts, `"cat"` and `""` included, since the pager and editor run through `sh`.
  - A key named in a quoted `-c`, `--config-env`, `git config` or `GIT_CONFIG_*` counts whatever the value, and so does a variable appended to with `+=`. So do `GIT_CONFIG`, `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL`, `GIT_CONFIG_SYSTEM`, `GIT_CONFIG_PARAMETERS`, `GIT_EXEC_PATH` and `GIT_TEMPLATE_DIR`, which point git at other config or programs.
  - A `$` or backtick in a `-c` or `--config-env` setting, a `git config` key, or a `GIT_CONFIG_*` key or value counts, since it may splice in any key: `git -c core$N.pager=./x log`. In a `git config` command, any `$` or backtick up to the next `;`, `&`, `|` or newline counts, past options with values (`--file`, `-f`, `--type`).

  Match regexes read assignments and `-c` values, which command words skip. Covered spellings include `-ccore.hookspath=x`, `git config set core.pager x`, `GIT_CONFIG_KEY_0=core.pager`, a multi-entry `GIT_CONFIG_PARAMETERS`, `GIT_PAGER+=./x`, `GIT_CONFIG_KEY_0+=core.pager` and `core$N.pager`.
- **parallel and sem.** They join `watch`: any `parallel` or `sem` is `external_effect`, since they run their arguments through a shell.
- **Interpreters and build tools.** `python3 x.sh`, `make`, `npm run` and `go generate` stay with Command: covering a file run by an interpreter would hail the captain on most builds.
- **Destructive git commands.** `command` rules on `Bash` and `Monitor` tag `destructive`, past git's global options (`-C`, `-c`, `--git-dir`, ...), so a commit message naming them is no match. Each rule has one leading anchor, with its forms as alternatives after the subcommand, so a regex never rescans the whole render:
  - `git checkout` with `--`, a word starting with `.` or holding `:`, `*`, `?` or `[` (no branch can), two words besides options (`git checkout HEAD x.go`), or `-f`, `--force`, `-B`;
  - `git switch` with `-f`, `--force`, `-C` or `--discard-changes`;
  - `git restore` of the worktree: `--worktree`/`-W`, or anything without `--staged`/`-S`. `git restore --staged x` only unstages and keeps its route;
  - `git branch` with `-D`, `-f`, `--force`, `-M` or `-C`;
  - `git stash drop` and `git stash clear`.

  `git checkout main`, `git checkout -b x origin/main`, `git switch -c x`, `git branch -d x`, `git branch -m x` and `git stash pop` keep their routes. `git push` with `--force`, `-f` or `--force-with-lease` was already `external_effect`, as is any push.
- **ANSI-C bodies.** `subjects` adds the de-quoted, stripped and resolved views of the text with each `$'...'` decoded, once for each of two decodings, (`\xHH`, `\NNN`, `\uHHHH`, `\UHHHHHHHH`, `\cX`, the letter escapes, `\'`, `\\`), folded again, and read as a single-quoted string. `git $'\x70ush'` and `x=$'\x70ush'; git $x` read as `git push` on any tool. The first copies every `'...'` and `"..."` span and escaped character as it is, so a `$'` inside them opens nothing (`grep 'a$' x; git $'\x70ush'`). Bash differs in comments and heredoc bodies, where a quote is no quote, and inside `"$(...)"`, `` "`...`" `` and `"${x:-...}"`, where it decodes `$'`. The second decodes every `$'` wherever it is, and reads those. In both, `\c` before the closing quote is literal and `\c\\` is one escape; in the first, a decoded NUL ends the body (`git $'pu\x00'sh` reads as `git push`). The undecoded views stay beside the decoded ones, since a view only adds matches. The `$'` rule on `Bash` and `Monitor` stays.
- **Route changes.** Commands that now go to the captain instead of Command:
  - `rm`, `find` or `go` with a variable the command doesn't assign, unquoted or starting an argument: `rm -f "$f"` with `f` from the environment, `find "$dir" -name x`, `find . -name "$pat"`, `go test -run "$T"`, `go build -o "$OUT"`. `go build -o "$HOME/bin/x" .`, `rm -f $TMPDIR/x` and `find $HOME/.cache -name x` keep their routes;
  - a substitution starting an argument of `rm`, `find` or `go`: `rm "$(ls *.tmp)"`, pinned to Command by #157, and `find "$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel)" -name x`; or unquoted in one of `go`'s, unless it lists packages;
  - program-running git settings with any other value: `GIT_SEQUENCE_EDITOR="sed -i ..."`, `git config core.hooksPath .githooks`, `git -c core.hooksPath=/dev/null commit`, `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL=/dev/null`;
  - a `$` or backtick in a `git config` command or a `-c` setting: `git config user.email "$EMAIL"`, `git -c user.name="$NAME" commit`;
  - any `parallel` or `sem`;
  - the git commands above, and a literal `for` list fed to `git checkout`;
  - a Write or Edit holding `$'...'` that decodes to a captain-only command.

  The other way, `for p in $(go list ./...); do go vet $p; done`, which #157 sent to the captain, goes to Command again.
- **Tests.** `TestNeverAutoAnswered` has a case for each covered spelling, under the default and both fixtures (their classify rules copy the default's). `TestClassifyDefault` pins the ordinary forms above to Command and the route changes to the captain. `TestOpaqueCommand` covers unbound variables and `goList`; `TestDecodeANSIC` the decoder. `FuzzClassify` has seeds for ANSI-C escapes, `go list` substitutions, unbound variables and resolved loops.

## Tracker interface (#54)

Dated 2026-11-07, revised 2026-11-08 after two reviews, and on 2026-11-08 again for the idempotency markers (#206). Builds `campaigns-plan-2026-11-03.md` section 4.1 and the tracker half of "Repos in and out of beams (#51, #53, #54)". The findings flow (section 4.6: `file_finding`, `findings/<order>`, `tracker_review`) is stage 2.

- **Package.** `internal/tracker` holds `Tracker` (`Name`, `Canon`, `Get`, `List`, `Create`, `Comment`, `Close`), `Item`, `Query`, `Mark`, the errors `ErrNotFound`, `ErrAuth`, `ErrRateLimited` and `ErrBadRef`, and the adapter registry (Unlabel and ErrLabels: #154). Adapters register by name from `init`, and the core packages import none. `cmd/bmus` imports `internal/adapters/github` for that side effect. `trackertest.Fake` is an in-memory tracker for tests.
- **Items.** `Item{Ref, Title, Body, Labels, Milestone, Parent, State, BlockedBy}`. `BlockedBy` lists open blockers only, as a closed blocker no longer blocks. It is nil without dependency data and empty when nothing blocks. `List` sets `Parent` only when the query names one, and matches labels case-insensitively, as GitHub does. `Create` takes the same struct and ignores `Ref` and `State`.
- **Canon.** `Canon(ref)` is a ref in the adapter's canonical form, read without calling the tracker, or `ErrBadRef`.
- **Close reason.** `Close(ref, reason)` takes `completed` (the default) or `not_planned`, for stage 2's discard.
- **Config.** `<home>/tracker.yaml`, or `bmus bridge run --tracker <path>`, read like `regs.yaml`: a missing default is no tracker, and a missing named file is an error. The file holds one document with `adapter` and a section named after it. Unknown keys, an unknown adapter and a bad section fail startup.
- **GitHub adapter.** `internal/adapters/github`, on the standard library's `net/http`, with no new dependency. `repo: owner/name` is required, and `api_url` defaults to `https://api.github.com`. An `http` `api_url` is taken only for a loopback host, as the token would travel in the clear.
  - Auth: the value of the variable `token_env` names, when that is set and non-empty, else the output of `gh auth token --hostname <host>`, read once at startup. The host is `github.com` for `api.github.com` and `api_url`'s host otherwise, so a GitHub Enterprise server gets its own token. No token fails startup. The token is the bridge's own and never goes to a beam (D6).
  - Refs: `owner/name#N`, `#N` or `N`. A ref to another repository is `ErrBadRef`, so a beam's request can't reach past the configured repo. Items of the configured repo come back as `owner/name#N` in the config's spelling.
  - Other repositories: a parent's sub-issue or a blocker can live elsewhere. The adapter compares an issue's `repository_url` with the configured repo, ignoring case. A foreign issue keeps its own ref, its `BlockedBy` is nil, and the adapter calls none of its endpoints.
  - List: `GET /issues` with `state`, `labels` and `milestone`, the milestone's number found by its title. An unknown title is `ErrNotFound`. Pages are followed through `Link`, only under `api_url`, as the token goes with them. With a parent, `GET .../sub_issues`, filtered on the bridge. Pull requests are dropped.
  - Get, Comment and Close read the issue first and refuse a pull request as not found. An issue that answers under another number or repository (a transfer followed by a redirect) is not found either.
  - Redirects: GitHub answers 301 for a transferred issue or a renamed repository, and Go would turn a 301 on POST or PATCH into a GET, or replay a 307 or 308 one with its body and token. The client's `CheckRedirect` follows only a GET, and only to a URL under `api_url`. Any other 3xx comes back as an error, so a close can neither land on another issue nor report success having done nothing.
  - BlockedBy: `GET .../dependencies/blocked_by`, keeping open blockers. The call is skipped when `issue_dependencies_summary.blocked_by` is 0. A 404 there reads as no dependency data (nil).
  - Create: reads the parent, each blocker and the milestone first, so a missing one fails before anything is created. Then `POST /issues`, `POST .../sub_issues` with the new issue's id for a parent, and `POST .../dependencies/blocked_by` with each blocker's id. When a link fails the issue already exists: its ref comes back with the error, for stage 2's recovery.
  - Markers (#206): `Comment` and `Create` take a `tracker.Mark{Key, Since}`. A comment or issue written under a key ends with a blank line and `<!-- bmus <key> -->`. GitHub keeps that line in the raw markdown, where the API and the edit box show it, and hides it when it renders the text. A key is words of letters, digits and `._:/-` separated by single spaces, at most 200 bytes, so it can't close the HTML comment or break the line. A key needs a `Since` (`Mark.Check`). An empty key writes no marker and looks nothing up.
    - Author: only a marker the token's own account wrote counts, compared by the comment's or issue's `user.id`. Hail ids are sequential and readable from earlier markers, and GitHub's `since` filters on `updated_at`, so an outsider could plant the next hail's marker and keep it fresh by editing it. The adapter reads the account once with `GET /user`, at startup and again on a keyed write until it succeeds. A failure there refuses the write as a failed lookup does. A GitHub App installation token gets a 403, as it has no user; markers are then taken from any author, and the bridge logs one warning saying so.
    - Comment lists the issue's comments with `since` set to `Since` less an hour of slack for clock skew, every page under `api_url`, and posts nothing when one ends with the marker. Only the end of a comment counts. The text before the marker is the agent's, and a marker quoted there could pass a later hail's comment as posted. The window bounds the lookup's cost.
    - Create lists `GET /issues?state=all&since=...` the same way, skipping pull requests and other repositories' issues. The search API has its own rate limit and an indexing lag that can hide an issue filed seconds before the retry, and listing reads what GitHub has committed. An issue found keeps its number, and only its missing parent and blocker links are added, read from `.../sub_issues` and `.../dependencies/blocked_by`. Nothing calls Create in production yet. Section 4.6's finding marker is this format with the key `finding <order>/<key>`, looked up by time instead of the `bmus:unreviewed` label, which `keep` removes.
    - A write whose outcome is unknown (a transport error such as a timeout, an answer that doesn't decode, or a 5xx) is looked up again, and once more 2s later if that finds nothing, as GitHub may list a write a moment after taking it. The wait ends with the context. A marker found counts as done. Otherwise the write's error comes back with "(the comment may have been posted: GitHub lists none under its key yet)", or "the issue may have been created", so the captain and the agent know it may be there. A 4xx was refused and is not looked up.
    - A lookup that fails refuses the write: "nothing posted: could not look for a comment already posted on o/r#1 under H-0001: <error>", or "nothing created: ...". One that fails after a lost answer returns the write's error with "(the comment may have been posted: looking for it afterwards failed: <error>)".
    - `Get`, `List` and `Create` return bodies without a trailing marker, so an order sourced from a filed finding doesn't quote it. The bridge's answers, questions and log entries carry no comment text read back from the tracker, and the agent and the captain see the comment as the agent wrote it.
  - Errors: 404 and 410 are `ErrNotFound`, 401 is `ErrAuth`. A 429 is `ErrRateLimited`, as is a 403 with `X-RateLimit-Remaining` 0 (the primary limit) or with `Retry-After` or a message naming a rate limit (a secondary one). Any other 403 is `ErrAuth`. GitHub's message is kept.
- **Order source.** Orders gain `source`, free-form: a store column (added at `Open`), the order file, `order.create`, `OrderView` and the console's types. Only `tracker:<ref>` is read.
  - When: in `CreateOrder`, once every other check has passed and before the insert, with a 20s deadline on the tracker's read (the console's POST has none). The item's text is in the row before any workflow reads the order, and a replay reads the row. A recorded step would add a second read for no gain, since no workflow runs an order before it exists.
  - The body is the order's own body, a blank line, a line saying the text below is tracker item <ref> quoted for reference and written by its author, then the item's title and body in a backtick fence. The fence is one backtick longer than the longest run in the text (at least three), so the text can't close it and pose as the captain. The order's own text comes first, as it is the captain's instruction about the item. An order without a title takes the item's as "Tracker <ref>: <title>": the title sits outside the fence, and the prefix says whose text it is. The source is stored with the canonical ref, and the "order created" entry carries it as its payload.
  - The item is read once. Editing the issue afterwards doesn't change the order.
  - Refusals create nothing. No tracker is a bad request, "order source tracker:#1: no tracker configured (tracker.yaml)", and so is a ref the adapter can't read or an empty one. An item the tracker can't find is `not_found`. Any other tracker error is returned as it is.
- **Requests from beams.** `tracker_comment` (`{ref, body}`) and `tracker_close` (`{ref, reason?}`) are hails with options `[allow, deny]`, as `order_split` is. The schema checks both contexts.
  - `record-hail` stores the context with its ref made canonical (`canonTrackerContext`, re-encoded without HTML escapes, as combadge wrote it), and the bridge's own question (`trackerQuestion`): "Comment on tracker item o/r#1: <the comment on one line, cut to 2 KiB>" or "Close tracker item o/r#1 as not planned". `oneLine`, shared with `splitQuestion`, makes control and format characters (`unicode.Cf`, bidi overrides included) spaces.
  - The Regulations engine reads the ref and the whole comment as the hail's `Values` (`regsHail`), beside the question, so `classify` and `captain_only` rules see text past the question's 2 KiB.
  - `classify` runs `checkTrackerRequest` before the `regs.yaml` check. It refuses no tracker, options other than `[allow, deny]`, a stored context over 32 KiB (`maxConsultContext`, so a consult carries all of it), a context with unknown fields or trailing values, an empty ref, one `Canon` refuses or one that isn't canonical (record-hail stored it canonical, so the tracker's configuration changed since), an empty comment, and an unknown reason. Step `deny-request` then decides the hail at once by policy, route `auto`, "tracker request refused: <why>". Nobody else sees it.
  - Otherwise Regulations route it like any hail. An answer with option 0 runs three mission steps after `decide-hail` and before `send-answer`, as `create-children` does.
  - `tracker-window` reads the hail's `countermand_until`. A decision the captain may still countermand (Command's, or the captain timeout's `default_option`) waits it out in a durable `dbos.Sleep`, per the campaigns plan section 4.7, at most the tour's settle wait (`missionSettleTimeout`, 5m). A captain's decision has no window. Belays and recalls queued meanwhile wait for the sleep, and a belay doesn't cancel an allowed request: the mission reads it after the answer.
  - `tracker-request`, never retried by DBOS (`WithStepMaxRetries(0)`), reads the hail again and calls the tracker once. It refuses when the order's tour has ended (its end mark, or `tourOver` on the crew row, as `send-order` does, #195). It reads the hail in a write transaction (`store.GetHailLocked`, `_txlock=immediate`) with now taken inside it, so a countermand in flight commits first and a later one is too late. A window still open then (one longer than the capped wait) refuses the request. A countermand to any option but 0 refuses it. The option is stored (`hails.countermand_option`): a countermand to option 0 with the captain's own text allows, and a countermand from before it counts as allowing only with the decision "allow". `checkTrackerRequest` runs again. A store error here is a refusal, so the step never fails.
  - `tracker-record`, with store retries, logs "tracker: commented on o/r#1" (`hail_id`, `ref`), or notes "tracker request refused: <why>" once on the hail's rationale (`store.NoteRefusal`, which `RefuseSplit` now calls) and logs it.
  - The answer's decision is "commented on o/r#1" or "closed o/r#1 as completed", or the refusal with option 1. A tracker error's text goes in as it is (the GitHub adapter's starts "github: ").
  - Replays (#206): `tracker-request` posts the comment under the key "<hail id> <nonce>", with the hail's `decided_at` as `Since`. The nonce is 16 random hex digits the store makes when it first opens a database and keeps in its `settings` table (`store.Nonce`). It survives restarts, and a fresh database gets a new one, so its H-0001 never matches an older database's marker. A crash during the tracker's HTTP call leaves the step unrecorded. Its re-run finds a comment GitHub took and posts nothing, or posts one GitHub never got. A client timeout after GitHub took the comment is looked up within the same call. Either way the step returns "commented on o/r#1", as the first run did. Closing twice is harmless and takes no key.
- **Regulations defaults.** `tracker_comment` and `tracker_close` are both captain-only. A comment publishes text outside the ship, as `git push` does. `tracker_comment` is listed last in `captain_only`, after `tag: opaque`, so earlier indexes keep their numbers; the test fixtures list it last too. Dropping it lets Command answer comments, and the bridge then waits out the countermand window before posting. The default notes that `on_timeout: default_option` with option 0 would then allow comments on a timeout. `tracker_create` stays listed until stage 2.
- **Combadge.** Every role gets `tracker_comment` and `tracker_close`, checked by zod and pre-allowed. `tracker_comment` refuses, before any hail, a context whose JSON (`{ref, body}`) is over the bridge's 32 KiB in bytes, so CJK or escape-heavy text gets a clear message. Option 0 returns "Done: the bridge <decision>.", and anything else an error result. Like `order_split`, their hails start no park timer and keep the query from parking, so the bridge's result reaches the agent. The order's system prompt gains one line on them.
- **No tracker.** Sources and requests are refused as above. Orders without a `tracker:` source and every other hail are unchanged, and `bmus status` says nothing about it.
- **Upgrade.** Two columns, `orders.source` and `hails.countermand_option`, which `Open` adds. Missions and hail workflows from before replay unchanged: the new steps run only for the new categories.
- **Tests.** `internal/tracker`: the registry, `Load`, `Canon`, and the fake's `List` with `BlockedBy` (open blockers, none, nil without dependency data) and every filter, labels in any case. `internal/adapters/github` against an `httptest` fake of the REST API, paged two at a time: list with blockers (summary, dependencies call, nil on 404, pull requests dropped), sub-issues, foreign sub-issues and blockers, the config's spelling of the repo, create with parent, milestone and blockers, a missing blocker or parent failing before the create, a failed link returning the ref, comment and close (a pull request refused), 404, 401, 429 and both 403 kinds, a `Link` to another host not followed, bad refs, `GHHost`, token resolution and config errors, 301 and 307 on PATCH and POST never followed, a GET redirect off `api_url` not followed and one to another issue not found. Bridge: a `source` order on the fake gets the fenced text, title and canonical ref, the fence outlasts backtick runs, refusals create nothing, and without a tracker the source is refused. A `captain_only` rule matches a comment past 2 KiB. Holodeck: without a tracker both requests are refused by `deny-request`, with and without `regs.yaml`. With the fake and the default `regs.yaml` the captain allows a comment (`captain_only[7]`), a close (`captain_only[4]`) and a comment on a missing item; the fake records the first two and the third is answered deny. With comments handed to Command and an 8s window, a comment countermanded to deny is never posted and one countermanded to option 0 with the captain's text is. `tracker-request` refuses an open window and an ended tour (end mark and crew row), and a non-canonical ref is refused. Store: `countermand_option`, `CountermandedAway` and `GetHailLocked`. Markers (#206): a first `POST .../comments` taken by GitHub whose answer times out, then a retry, leave exactly one comment; a marker on the third page of comments is found; a marker quoted mid-comment, or older than the window, doesn't count; a failed lookup posts and creates nothing; a lost answer followed by a failed lookup says the comment may be there; Create after a lost answer takes the issue found and links it once, and a half-linked one gets its missing link; bodies come back without the marker; `Mark.Check` and the fake's keys; a planted marker from another author is ignored and the comment posted, the bridge's own marker is found, and another nonce doesn't match; `GET /user` read once, retried after a failure, and a 403 taking any author; a second lookup after the settle wait finds a comment the first missed, and the wait ends with the context; a key without `Since` refused; the store's nonce kept across opens and new for a new database; a re-run of the `tracker-request` step (`trackerRequestCall` called twice, as DBOS re-runs a step a crash left unrecorded) posts nothing new and returns the same result, with "<hail id> <nonce>" and `decided_at` as its mark. `checkTrackerRequest` and `trackerQuestion` tables, the schema test for both contexts, combadge's two tools, their refusals and that their hails never park, and the CLI's `--tracker` and the order file's `source`.

## Regex `$'` noise (#200)

Dated 2026-11-08, revised after review. Narrows the `$'` rule that "Regulations gaps (#188)" kept.

- **Rule.** A classify rule may set `ansi_c: true`. It fits a command holding an ANSI-C string where bash decodes one, as the shell parse (`parseShell`) finds it: outside single and double quotes, comments and heredoc bodies, in any word (assignments, `for` and `case` words, redirection targets and heredoc delimiters included), and inside `${...}`, arithmetic (`(( ))`, `$(( ))`) and substitutions, quoted or not. Bash decodes the string in arithmetic and then runs any `$(...)` it holds in a subscript. It also fits when a `${...}` in an unquoted heredoc body holds one, where bash leaves it as written; that errs toward the captain. It also fits when the parse gives up. Like `command` and `opaque` rules, it reads only the hail's command, never prose or the auto_allow pass. The default's `external_effect` rule for `$'` on `Bash` and `Monitor` uses it, with no regex. A `$` ending a quoted regex is no ANSI-C string, so `grep 'foo$' x.go` is no fit.
- **Parse fixes.** Inside `"..."` and unquoted heredoc bodies, `$'` and `$"` are now a literal `$`, as in bash: `grep "foo$" x` no longer reads the rest of the command as one string. A heredoc delimiter has its quotes removed, `$'...'` decoded and `$"..."` read as `"..."`, and any quote makes it quoted: `cat <<$'EOF'` ends at a line `EOF`, and the commands after it are read. Before, its body never ended and hid them, `<<$"EOF"` included.
- **Residual.** A comment or heredoc body holding a quote and `$'` ahead of an ANSI-C string (`# 'a$'`, then `x=$'-\x65'; perl $x ...`) throws both decodings out of step. The parse skips the comment and finds the string, so the rule still tags it on `Bash` and `Monitor`. Dropping the rule would send it to Command there.
- **Prose.** Like other parse-based rules, `ansi_c` rules skip prose, so a question or answer holding `$'` now relies on the decoded views. Before, any `$'` in prose went to the captain; the residual in prose joins the gap other tools have. Prose doesn't run.
