- **Route changes.** To Command on `Bash` and `Monitor`, and in a question or answer: a `$` ending a quoted argument, as in `go test -run '^TestFoo$' ./...`, `grep -n 'foo$' x.go`, `rg 'TODO$'`, `grep -E 'a|b$' f`, `jq '.a$' f.json`, `grep "foo$" x.go` and `echo "$'"`. To the captain on `Bash` and `Monitor`: commands after a `<<$'EOF'` or `<<$"EOF"` heredoc that the rules above tag. `echo $'\x70ush'` and `echo hi > $'\x2egit/hooks/pre-commit'` still go to the captain.
- **Tests.** `TestClassifyDefault` pins the regex forms above, `sed -n '/^x$/p' f`, `awk '/foo$/' f` and `sed -e 's/x$//' f` to Command. `TestNeverAutoAnswered` also runs #188's ANSI-C cases on `Bash` and `Monitor`, and adds ANSI-C strings in assignments, `for` lists, redirection targets, `${:+}` and arithmetic, the residual through comments and a heredoc body, and the heredoc delimiter cases, on both. `TestParseShellANSIC` covers where the parse finds ANSI-C strings and `heredocDelim`. `FuzzClassify` has seeds for both.

## SQLITE_BUSY under load (#204)

Recorded 2026-11-08.

- **Symptom.** Under the #193 load harness (4 CPU burners, 2 `dd conv=fsync` writers, the bridge package looping), a store write sometimes failed with `database is locked (5) (SQLITE_BUSY)`, as `hd.createOrder` (`Store.InsertOrder`) did in #188's and #197's runs.
- **Connections.** The store and DBOS open `bridge.db` the same way: WAL, `_txlock=immediate` and `busy_timeout(5000)`, which DBOS's `OpenSQLitePool` adds to its 8-connection pool. No connection begins a deferred transaction that later writes: `ReadStatus` is the one deferred BEGIN, and it only reads. DBOS also sets `synchronous(NORMAL)`. The store keeps SQLite's default, `FULL`, and fsyncs the WAL on every commit while it holds the write lock.
- **Cause.** No single writer holds the lock past the timeout. A driver build that logged every write transaction on the shared file showed short holders back to back: DBOS's queue runner (`DequeueWorkflows`, `TransitionDelayedWorkflows`, `CancelTimedOutWorkflows`), workflow steps, the pumps' `RecordInbox` and other store writes. Under the `dd` load, store commits that held the lock over 500ms spent a median 630ms (up to 1.8s) in the commit's fsync, against 0.2ms for DBOS's commits. SQLite's busy handler polls the lock, with sleeps growing to 100ms, and keeps no queue. It counts busy_timeout as the sum of those nominal sleeps: BEGINs that succeeded waited up to 13s of wall time. A writer that keeps finding the lock taken gives up after its 5s of polls, while the others take the lock between them. A probe of 4 or 16 goroutines inserting orders through one store under the same load got 24 and 147 SQLITE_BUSY errors a minute.
- **Fix.** The store queues its own writers. Every store write goes through `withTx`, `AppendLog` and `InsertInboxSeq` included, which used to write outside a transaction. `withTx` holds a one-slot semaphore (`Store.writeSem`) from BEGIN to COMMIT, so store writes wait in Go, in turn, and at most one store connection polls SQLite against DBOS's writers. A writer whose context ends while it queues returns `ctx.Err()` at once. A BEGIN that still gets SQLITE_BUSY is retried with backoff (10ms, doubling to 500ms) for up to 30s (`busyRetryFor`) or until the context ends, when the error wraps both `ctx.Err()` and the last SQLITE_BUSY. BEGIN IMMEDIATE takes the lock or fails having done nothing, so a retry never applies a write twice. With both, the probe got no errors and its slowest insert took 0.5s. With the retry alone, 16 goroutines still got 5 errors a minute, after 30s waits.
- **Unchanged.** busy_timeout stays 5000: a longer one polls the same way. The store keeps `synchronous=FULL`, as a pump acks a beam's message once its inbox row commits and `NORMAL` could lose that commit on power loss. DBOS retries its own SQLITE_BUSY errors without a limit. A callback that runs inside a store write transaction must not start another store write: it now waits on the write slot for good, where before it failed with SQLITE_BUSY. The methods that take one, and `BelayFunc`, say so.
- **Tests.** `TestInsertOrderRetriesBusy` holds the write lock from another connection past a 1ms busy_timeout and checks that the insert lands once the lock is released. `TestInsertOrderBusyGivesUp` checks the deadline and the context. `TestQueuedWriteHonoursContext` cancels a writer queued behind a held slot. `TestConcurrentWritesQueue` runs 8 writers with a 1ms busy_timeout and no retry, and fails with SQLITE_BUSY without the write slot. Under the harness, the issue's acceptance run passed 40 of 40 with no SQLITE_BUSY. The three runs before the fix (101 results) had none either: there the error is rare, and the probe is what reproduces it.

## Console push (#4)

Recorded 2026-11-12.

- **API.** `POST /api/orders/{id}/push` takes `{target_branch?, force?}` behind the console's session, Origin and CSRF checks, and calls `Daemon.PushOrder`. It returns `[{name, url, branch, commit}]`: `PushedRepo` gained the remote URL and the target branch, which `order push --json` now prints too. Errors map as over RPC: 400 for an order that isn't done or wasn't fetched, 409 for `ErrPushRejected` with git's output kept to the bridge's log.
- **Timeout.** The console's push runs under 60s, against `order.push`'s 10 minutes: the page waits on the request. An action that runs past its time now fails with the console's own code `timeout` (504) and a message saying it may have taken effect in part. Before, it was an `internal` 500.
- **URL redaction.** A registered URL may carry credentials, and a token may stand in the user part. `redactURL` drops the userinfo, query and fragment of any URL with a scheme, `ssh://git@...` included. It cuts at the last "@" after the scheme, so a password with an unencoded "/", "?" or "#" doesn't survive a parse that ends the authority early. An "@" in a path then hides part of the URL. An scp-like address (`git@example.com:o/r.git`) and a local path are kept, since their user part holds no password. `TestRedactURL` lists the forms it is known to handle. `PushedRepo.URL` and the snapshot's new `repo_urls` are redacted; git itself still gets the registered URL.
- **Base commit.** The diff command's base is the commit of the order's latest ship-repos log entry whose `from` is its `base_ref`. A checkpoint's commit would leave the earlier work out of the diff. If upstream moved between missions, the later base is still right: `git diff <base>...bmus/<order>` diffs from the merge base. The console's views of done orders carry it as `base_commits` (`consoleSource.orderView`, from the new `Store.ListOrderLogKind`), so it doesn't depend on the snapshot's log window. The bridge's home comes in the snapshot's new `home` field: the status had none. The command quotes each clone's path in single quotes.
- **Force.** No force control shows upfront. A 409 shows the rejection and a "Force push" button, behind its own confirm naming each remote and saying the remote branch's commits are overwritten, as the CLI's `--force` is an explicit opt-in. Force is plain `--force`, as in the CLI.
- **Page.** Push, the pushed marker and "Copy diff command" sit on a done order's mission record row, the console's one view of a finished order. The pushed marker reads the recent log's "repo <name> pushed to <branch>" entries. The pull request link is built in the page, for github.com remotes only (`githubCompareURL`): the host must be exactly github.com, so look-alikes such as `github.com.example.com` get none.

## Console mission report (#104)

Recorded 2026-11-12. Replaces the copied diff command of "Console push (#4)".

- **API.** `GET /api/orders/{id}/report` sits behind the session check like the other reads and calls `Source.OrderReport`. It returns `{order, crew, beams, report, pushes, cap, repos: [{name, base, diff, truncated}]}`. `order` is the order's view with `created_at` and `done_at`, the times of its "order created" and done entries. `crew` is the assignee's view. `report` is the done entry's summary and artifacts, and `pushes` are its "repo <name> pushed to <branch>" entries. `cap` is the diff's cap in bytes, for the page's note. Missing data is `null` or `[]`: an order from before a field was recorded, or a crew member whose row is gone. Errors map as an action's: 400 for an order that isn't done, a repo with no full commit id as its base or no `bmus/<order>` in its clone, 404 for an unknown order, 504 `timeout` past 30s. The read runs on the request's context and takes no part in the action gate, so a client that goes away ends it.
- **Beams.** The beams are the aliases the order's entries carry, oldest first. Ship-repos and done entries now record the beam (`beam` in their payloads), as checkpoint entries already did. A re-energize or watch change moves an order to another beam, and its next ship-repos names it. Orders from before carry only their checkpoints' beams, if any.
- **Git.** `Daemon.OrderReport` reads the diff as `orderDiff`, which runs `git diff --no-ext-diff --no-textconv --no-color --src-prefix=a/ --dst-prefix=b/ --end-of-options <base>...refs/heads/bmus/<order> --` through `runGit`'s path, with `-c core.attributesFile=/dev/null -c diff.external= -c core.bigFileThreshold=<cap>`. An agent can commit a `.gitattributes` naming a diff driver, and the captain's config may define one by that name: no diff or textconv program runs. The fixed prefixes keep the `diff --git a/x b/x` headers whatever `diff.noprefix` says. The base is the one #4 recorded, now read by `baseCommits` when the diff is asked for. It takes no repo lock: it only reads, and a fetch may hold the lock for minutes.
- **Cap.** Each repo's diff is capped at 2 MiB while git writes it (`runGitCapped`). Past the cap the bridge kills git's process group and cuts the diff back to its last whole line, with `truncated: true`. Nothing past the cap is held. The bridge reads git's stdout pipe before `Wait`, and kills only before `Wait` starts: git can't be reaped while it is read, so its pid can't be reused. git reads a blob whole before diffing it. A file over the cap is "Binary files ... differ" through `core.bigFileThreshold`, which halves git's peak (about 122 MB to 64 MB for a 60 MB file): git still reads the blob once to print that line.
- **Page.** `/report/<order>` serves `index.html`, for an order id of `[A-Za-z0-9-]` only; no other path under `/report/` serves anything, and `/diff/` is gone. From the top, the page shows "MISSION REPORT" with the order's id and title, then panels for the order (state, priority, division, repos with their base ref, branch, times and body), the crew member, the beams, the report and any pushes, then the diff with Copy. Missing data reads "unknown". The body, summary and artifacts render as escaped text, as the diff does. `index.html` loads `/app.js` and `/app.css` by absolute path. The CSP is unchanged. The page goes through the same session exchange as the console and shows its "No session" message without a cookie. Each line renders as plain text in its own element, coloured by its first character after a file's first `@@`. A repo's section renders at most 50,000 lines, with a note that Copy has the rest. Copy writes the raw diff of every repo, and a refused clipboard leaves a note to select the text.
- **Mission record.** "Mission report" replaces "Copy diff command" on a done order with repos, as a small link opening the page in a new tab (`rel="noopener noreferrer"`). Each row puts the order id and text on a full-width line, then a footer: the crew member and status chip on the left, the actions on the right. Push takes the gold action style at the same small size as "Mission report", and its confirm opens under the two. The snapshot's `home` and the order views' `base_commits` are gone: nothing else read them.

## Beaming in (#106)

Recorded 2026-11-12. Revises "No status change" in "Hello before the first claim (#173)".

- **Status.** `store.CrewBeamingIn` (`beaming_in`): the crew member's tour is provisioning a beam or waiting for its combadge's hello. Before, the row stayed `on_deck` through both, with no beam while provisioning, so a restart past the beams' TTL with the login expired showed "on deck, beam: none" for as long as the login stayed expired.
- **Transitions.** `beaming_in` sits exactly between a tour starting to provision and its hello:
  - crew add: `AddCrew` inserts the crew member `beaming_in` (a status other than empty or `beaming_in` is a bad request). `store.InsertCrew` still defaults to `on_deck` for callers that set nothing; only the bridge adds crew.
  - watch change and re-energize: `store.NextTour` sets `beaming_in` with `tour_no+1` and the cleared `current_beam`, in its one conditional update, so a dismissed crew member is still left alone.
  - provisioning: `provision` sets `beaming_in` with the new beam (`SetCrewStatusUnlessDismissed`), where it set `on_deck`. A crew member re-energized by a tour started before this change goes from `sickbay` to `beaming_in` here.
  - the hello: `hello-logged` (`Daemon.helloOnDeck`) sets `on_deck` from `beaming_in` only (`store.UpdateCrewStatusFrom`), with "crew on deck: combadge said hello" in the same transaction. A crew member in any other status is left alone and nothing is logged: a dismissal that landed during the wait stands, and the tour then claims nothing. A re-run of the step logs nothing more.
  - sickbay and dismissal override `beaming_in` as any other status: `toSickbay` and `DismissCrew` don't look at it.
  - a bridge restart mid-provisioning or mid-hello-wait changes nothing: the row is `beaming_in` in the store, and the resumed tour moves it on. `ensurePumps` now attaches a `beaming_in` crew member that has a `current_beam` (serve is up, the hello may be pending), as `pumpCrewStatuses` includes it.
- **#173's design.** The hello gate stays the tour: it alone calls `claimOrder`, after `awaitHello`. #173 left the row `on_deck` only because nothing showed the wait. The new status records what the gate already meant, and the store backs it: `ClaimOldestDirectedOrder` and `ClaimOldestPoolOrder` claim nothing for a crew member `beaming_in`, an order already away (a parked one after a watch change) included, and write nothing. They didn't require `on_deck` before: they only refused `dismissed`.
- **Re-energize.** A crew member `beaming_in` is handled as one in sickbay. With its tour live the call does nothing (`under_way`). With no tour, after a crash between an earlier call's `NextTour` and its tour's start, it starts tour `tour_no` without another `NextTour`; `attachCrewTours` also starts that tour on the next bridge start. With its tour ended without a hello (ERROR or CANCELLED), it runs `NextTour` and starts the next tour.
- **Recall.** A parked order whose crew member is `beaming_in` can be recalled, as from sickbay. Its new tour claims nothing until the hello, which can be a whole tsh login wait away, and "being resumed; try again" would hold for all of it.
- **Command.** `commandCrew` and `commandDown` count a `beaming_in` crew member of the command division as on deck only with a `current_beam` (`commandOnDeck`): serve is up and the hello pending. A consult sent then waits on the hello or the consult timeout, as #173 noted. With no beam yet (provisioning, perhaps waiting on a tsh login, after a watch change or a re-energize), hails go to the captain, logged "Command not on deck", and the status lists that reason.
- **Status reason.** `transporterWorkflow` keeps an in-memory set on the Daemon (`setBeamLoginWait`, under `loginMu`): a crew member is in it from a provision attempt that found the login expired until an attempt that didn't, or the workflow's end (a deferred clear), keyed by crew member with its tour so a later tour's wait isn't cleared by an earlier one. It is a display hint, never workflow state: the workflow doesn't branch on it, and a replay that sets it for a moment before its next live attempt is harmless. After a restart it fills again as the resumed workflows reach their next attempt. `statusOf` counts only crew members both `beaming_in` in the store and in the set, and adds "N crew waiting for a beam (tsh login)". It sets yellow and, like Command's and the login's reasons, is listed under red too, after Command's and before the login's. No column: the store would have to be written from a workflow body or a step for a hint.
- **Counters.** `CrewCounts.BeamingIn` (`beaming_in` in JSON), in `bridge.status`, the console's status event and `bmus status` ("crew 0 beaming in, 1 on deck, ..."). `BeamsLive` counts a `beaming_in` crew member with a `current_beam`.
- **Display.** Console: a "Beaming in" chip in cream, the one tone no other crew status uses, with a blinking light; a crew card's beam reads "provisioning" while there is none, and its task line "Beaming in: no orders until its combadge says hello" when it has no order. The Transporter Room pad has its own `beaming` mode (a dashed cream disc and a faint beam) labelled "provisioning", beam or not. The stats row gains "Beaming in", and "Crew aboard" counts it. `bmus crew list` prints the status as is (`beaming_in`) and the beam as `provisioning` while there is none.
- **Migration.** None. An existing database has no `beaming_in` rows. An `on_deck` row with no `current_beam` (a tour provisioning under the old code) is left as is: its next transition (provision's, `NextTour`'s) sets it right. A tour PENDING in its hello wait across the upgrade has an `on_deck` row, which `hello-logged` leaves alone, without its log line; its claims work as before.
- **Tests.** Store: `TestBeamingInClaimsNothing` (directed, pool and an order already away, nothing written; claims once on deck; a dismissal during the wait stands), and `NextTour` sets `beaming_in`. Bridge: `TestBeamingInWaitsForLogin` on holodeck with a login-gated provider (`loginGateProvider`): the bridge stops with the login expired before the first beam's watch change (its lifetime is the 2s lead plus 15s), stays down past that lifetime, and on restart the crew member is `beaming_in` with no beam on tour 1, with `bridge.status` listing "1 crew waiting for a beam (tsh login)", then `on_deck` on a new beam once the login is back, its hello before its first claim. The hello tests now expect `beaming_in` during the wait and across a restart in it; re-energize expects `beaming_in` on tour 1 before the beam is up, a crashed re-energize is started from `beaming_in`, and a `beaming_in` crew member whose tour was cancelled gets a next tour (`TestReenergizeBeamingInEndedTour`). `TestCommandBeamingIn` routes a hail to the captain while Command is beaming in with no beam, and to Command once its beam is up. `TestRecallRefusals` accepts a recall of an order parked on a crew member beaming in. `statusOf` cases for the counter and the reason. The fake tsh of `TestLoginExpiry` now sends a hello. Web: vitest for the chip, pad, crew card and counter.

## Phase 4 schema (#111)

Recorded 2026-11-13. Implements `spec/phase-4-plan-2026-11-12.md` section 1.

- **Migration.** Follows the schema convention (#58). `beams.removed_at`, `beams.labels`, `orders.budget_turns` and `orders.budget_extended` are `addedColumns` entries. `order_usage`, `order_egress` and `backups` are new tables, which an older database gains through `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS`. Nothing goes in `requiredColumns`, so every existing database opens. `workflowVersion` stays `"1"`.
- **Types.** `order_usage` counts default to 0 and its `order_id` references `orders`. `order_egress.hail_id` references `hails`, as a grant always comes from a hail. `backups.id` is an autoincrement integer, `finished_at` is NULL while a backup runs or after a crash in one, and `error` is empty for a backup that passed its check.
- **removed_at.** `store.SetBeamRemoved(crew, alias, at)` marks every unmarked row of that crew member with that alias. Only one beam holds an alias at a time, so older rows with it are gone too. `removeBeam` calls it when the beam is removed or found gone, and so does a provision re-run that finds its tour's recorded beam gone, before it creates another. A removal that fails leaves the row NULL, which reconcile (`spec/phase-4-plan-2026-11-12.md` section 5) reads as a beam that may still exist. `DismissCrew` sets it through `removeBeam`, directly or through the dismissed tour's `remove-beam` step. `BeamRecord.RemovedAt` carries it.
- **Accessors.** Only `SetBeamRemoved` and `RemovedAt`. Each feature issue adds the accessors its tables need.
- **Tests.** `TestOpenAddsPhase4Schema` opens a database made with `testdata/schema-before-111.sql`, a copy of `schema.sql` before this change, twice. Its rows survive, and each changed or new table's columns match a new database's. `TestSetBeamRemoved` covers the store method. The bridge tests for remove-previous, a dismissal that removes the beam itself and a dismissal through the tour check `removed_at`, and a failed removal leaves it NULL.

## Usage reporting (#117)

Dated 2026-11-13. Builds the measuring part of section 6 of `phase-4-plan-2026-11-12.md`. Revises "Usage" in "Hail parking (#154)" and the report's `usage` in "SDK harness (#11)".

- **Message.** `usage`, beam to bridge, order-bound: `{input_tokens, output_tokens, turns}`, cumulative for the order on this beam. Input counts cache reads and writes. Counts never decrease. The latest report for (order, beam) is the one with the highest envelope seq, which is monotonic across combadge restarts; the body has no seq of its own. It is in the schema, `protocol.ts` (`UsageBody`, `USAGE_EVERY_TURNS`) and `protocol.go` (`UsageBody`), with a golden file.
- **Counting.** `Tally` in `combadge/src/harness/sdk.ts`. A main-loop `assistant` message adds its `message.usage` and one turn; a subagent's (`parent_tool_use_id` set) adds tokens only. A synthetic API-error message (`error` set) counts for nothing. The CLI sends one message per content block, several sharing a message id and none with final usage, so a message id counts once, at the highest usage its messages carried. A `result` replaces the running query's count with `usageOf(result)`, field by field never below the count it replaces: a crash or error result can carry zeroed usage.
- **Precision.** Between results, a running report's input is exact and its output is a lower bound, since streamed output isn't final; the query's result settles it. Messages that arrive once a query is aborted (a park, a belay, a stop's end) count for nothing, though their tokens were spent. A refusal fallback's `supersedes` is not evicted: the refused leg was spent too, and its turn stays counted, an overcount of one turn per fallback.
- **Resumed sessions.** The SDK documents that a resumed session's results may carry the totals its transcript saved. A resumed query's result that covers the session's earlier queries (E) plus what this query streamed is taken to include E, and E is taken off it. Otherwise it counts as the query's own. Any unstreamed part of the resumed query (compaction, subagent text the stream didn't forward and, in practice, most of its output) can push a result of its own past that bar; the count is then low by at most E, at most half the true total. A transcript that saved only part of E can err either way.
- **Lifetime.** serve keeps each unfinished order's latest usage report in `state.json`, written before the outbox line, and hands it to the order's next run as `Order.usage`. Counts carry on across a park, a resume, a stopped order sent back and a combadge restart. A completed order's entry is dropped. The report's `usage` is the same cumulative token count, per beam: an order re-run on another beam reports that beam's tokens only.
- **When.** Every 10 main-loop turns, and before every `checkpoint_ready` (the stopping one included), before the report and when a run ends any other way (a belay, a failure), each of these only when the counts moved after the last report.
- **Holodeck.** A `usage` step, `{"usage": {"input_tokens", "output_tokens", "turns"}}`, emits those counts as given. The report's usage is the run's latest usage step's tokens, else `Order.usage`'s, else zero. Echo reports nothing.
- **Bridge.** The pump records and acks `usage` like any message and logs it at debug; a body that fails to decode is logged as a warning and dropped, as `progress` is. Storing it per (order, beam) is #118.
- **Tests.** `combadge/test/sdk.test.ts`: "sdk harness usage (#117)" (turns, subagents, split and API-error messages, every 10 turns, the result's totals, a run carrying on from `Order.usage`, counts that never drop, a checkpoint, a failed and a belayed run) and parking cases (a resume with and without the earlier totals, the misread case, a stop while parked). Holodeck: the step and its validation, and a stopped order's usage kept in `state.json` across a serve restart and reported by its next run. Go: `TestPumpHolodeckUsage` runs `fixtures/usage.json` through a real serve and checks the debug log, the inbox and that nothing reaches a mission or the log; schema cases in `TestSchemaRejects`.

## Jettison (#108)

Recorded 2026-11-13. Revises the missing-branch error of "Mission report (#104)" and the push's missing-branch refusal.

- **Command.** `bmus jettison`, alias `bmus purge`, calls `bridge.jettison` with `{older_than, yes}`. It deletes the `bmus/<order>` branches in the bridge's clones (`<home>/repos/<name>`) of orders that ended (done, failed, belayed or withdrawn) at least `--older-than` ago. Without `--yes` it is a dry run. `--older-than` defaults to `7d` and takes a Go duration (`36h`) or whole days (`7d`); the CLI and the bridge share the parser (`bridge.ParseAge`) and refuse a negative age.
- **Scope.** Only `refs/heads/bmus/<order>` of an order the store has, in a directory under `repos/` with a `HEAD`. A branch whose order the store doesn't have is left alone and not listed. Other branches, remote-tracking refs, tags and remotes are never touched. An order with several repos has a branch in each, and each is listed.
- **Base refs.** A branch an open order's `base_ref` names stays: a re-run of that order's ship-repos resolves it again. Each open order's `base_ref` for the repo has its `~`, `^` and `@{...}` suffixes stripped and is resolved in the clone with `git rev-parse --symbolic-full-name`; any `refs/heads/bmus/*` it names is kept. This is read at listing and again under the repo's lock before deleting.
- **Ended at.** The orders table has no end time. The bridge uses the time of the order's latest captain's log entry (`store.LatestOrderLogTS`). Every move to an ended state logs an entry in the same transaction, so this time is never before the order ended, and it works for orders that ended before this change. A later entry (a push after done) only keeps the branch longer.
- **Delete.** Per repo, under its lock, waiting at most 2 minutes for it (past that the repo's branches are skipped as busy). Under the lock the bridge re-reads the base refs, drops a branch no longer at its listed commit (skipped as moved), and deletes the rest in one `git update-ref --stdin` transaction of `delete <ref> <old>`. If the transaction fails, each branch is deleted on its own, and one whose old-value check fails is skipped as moved. Each deletion is logged on its order as "branch bmus/<order> jettisoned from repo <name>" with the repo, branch and commit. A clone `for-each-ref` fails on is skipped and reported. Any other error stops the run with an error naming how many branches were deleted before it. The RPC's timeout is 10 minutes.
- **Output.** The dry run prints a table (REPO, ORDER, BRANCH, ENDED), a "skipped" line per skipped branch or repo with its reason, and a summary line with the branch and order counts, the age, how many branches were kept (open, ended too recently, or named by a `base_ref`) and how many skipped. `--yes` prints the same for what it deleted, with "jettisoned ..." as the summary. `--json` prints the result.
- **Jettisoned.** A repo's branch counts as jettisoned when the clone is readable, has no `bmus/<order>` (`rev-parse --verify --quiet` exits 1), and the order's log has its jettison entry for that repo (`branchJettisoned`). A branch missing without the entry, or a clone git can't read, keeps the old errors.
- **Report.** A jettisoned repo is `{"jettisoned": true}` in the report's `repos`, with an empty diff and no error; its base commit is not required. A diff that fails is checked again, for a jettison that lands between the check and the diff. The page shows "branch jettisoned" in the repo's header and a note that `bmus jettison` deleted the branch, in place of the diff.
- **Push.** Pushing a jettisoned order's work is refused with "bmus/<order> was jettisoned from repo <name> on <date>; its work is no longer on the bridge".
- **Tests.** Bridge: `TestJettison` with old done (two repos), old failed, recently belayed and open orders, branches named by open orders' base refs in five spellings, an unknown order's branch, other branches, remote-tracking refs, tags and an unreadable clone. The dry run lists only the old ended orders' branches and changes no ref; `yes` deletes exactly those; a shorter age takes the recent one. The report marks both repos jettisoned, the push says so, and a branch missing without a jettison or a missing clone is still a 400. `TestJettisonRepoRechecks` moves one branch and names another as a base ref after the listing: the first is skipped, the second kept, the third deleted. `TestJettisonRepoBusy` skips a repo whose lock stays busy. CLI: `TestJettisonCLI` (`purge` prints the same as `jettison`, `--older-than`, `--yes`) and the skipped lines. Web: vitest for the marker, and the console e2e jettisons the done order's branch and reloads its report.

## Budget ledger (#118)

Dated 2026-11-13. Builds the configuration part of section 6 of `phase-4-plan-2026-11-12.md` and the bridge side of its measuring part, on top of "Usage reporting (#117)". Revises that section's "both fields carry what remains". Enforcement is #119, the views #120.

- **Roster.** A division gains `budget: {tokens, turns}` (`DivisionConfig.Budget`, type `Budget`). A negative value fails the roster's check, and an unknown key under it is an error, as anywhere in the roster. Zero or missing is unlimited.
- **Order.** The order file and `order.create` gain `budget_turns` beside `budget_tokens`, refused when negative. `store.Order` carries `BudgetTurns` and `BudgetExtended`, the #111 columns.
- **Resolving.** `resolveBudget(order, crew)` in `roster.go` resolves each field on its own: the order's value when above zero, else its division's, else 0. It returns a `BudgetSource` per field (`{tokens, turns}`, each `order`, `division` or `default` for none), since an order may set its tokens and take its division's turns. The division is the order's; a directed order with none takes its crew member's. A `split_order` child is created with no budget and takes its division's. Campaign orders don't exist yet; created without a budget, they will too.
- **Limit and used.** The order command carries the budget as a limit plus what other beams used. `sendOrder` resolves the budget on every send, reads `store.OrderUsage` and calls `budgetFor`. `budget_tokens` and `budget_turns` are the current limit, each field times `1 + budget_extended`, 0 still meaning unlimited. `used_tokens` and `used_turns` are the usage summed over the order's rows whose beam is not the one this send goes to (the crew member's current beam); tokens are input plus output. The harness is over budget when used plus its own count passes the limit (#119). Its own count already holds this beam's earlier runs on the order (#117), and the bridge leaves this beam's row out of used: nothing is counted twice and the harness needs no baseline. A spent budget is sent as it is, and an extension only raises the limit.
- **Wire.** `OrderBody` gains `budget_turns`, `used_tokens` and `used_turns`, optional with absent as 0, in the schema, `protocol.ts` and `protocol.go`; the order golden file carries all three. No harness reads the budget fields yet. The holodeck's step log records the four budget fields when nonzero, so tests can see what reached the harness.
- **Recording.** The pump's `usage` case replaces #117's debug-only one. It upserts `order_usage` for (order, pump's beam alias) inside the inbox transaction (`InboxTx.RecordUsage`), straight to the store like `progress`. Only a higher envelope seq replaces a row; an equal or lower one, late or replayed, changes nothing, and the inbox already drops an exact replay. An unknown order writes nothing, and a report without an order or with a bad body is dropped with a log line. The order's state and assignee don't matter: what a beam spent was spent.
- **Same beam twice.** An order recalled and directed back to the same crew member's beam reports under the same alias. serve keeps the order's counts in `state.json` across runs (#117), so the second run's reports continue the first's and the row keeps growing. Seqs are monotonic across combadge restarts on one beam. A new beam that reuses a removed beam's alias starts its seqs again. The holodeck mints fresh aliases; tsh's are the provider's, which may be reused. If an order lands on a reused alias that already has a row for it, the new beam's reports stay below that row's seq and are dropped, and the order's usage is undercounted. The keying stays (order, alias).
- **Tests.** `TestResolveBudget` (order, division, mixed, the crew member's division, none, a split child) and `TestBudgetFor` (other beams, a new beam, an extension, unlimited, overspent, only this beam's row). `TestLoadRoster` reads a budget and refuses a negative or unknown one. `TestRecordUsage` in the store: a higher, lower and equal seq, another beam, an unknown order. `TestPumpUsageRecorded`: the row, a late lower seq and a replay that change nothing, an unknown order, none and a bad body. `TestPumpHolodeckUsage` now checks the row from `fixtures/usage.json`. `TestBudgetLedger` runs a holodeck order with its division's budget through a watch change, the new beam running its own script: the first send carries the limit with nothing used, the first beam's report becomes a row, the second send carries the same limit and the first beam's usage, and the new beam adds a second row. The CLI, RPC and holodeck step-log tests cover the new fields.

## Budget enforcement (#119)

Dated 2026-11-13. Builds the enforcing part of section 6 of `phase-4-plan-2026-11-12.md`, on top of "Usage reporting (#117)" and "Budget ledger (#118)".

- **Over budget.** A harness is over budget when the order's `used_tokens` plus its own token count passes `budget_tokens`, or `used_turns` plus its own turns passes `budget_turns`; a limit of 0 is unlimited. Its count already holds this beam's earlier runs on the order (#117). The shared logic is `Budget` in `combadge/src/harness/common.ts`.
- **Wire.** `comms.CategoryBudget` (`budget`), options `[extend, stop]` (`OptionExtend`, `OptionStop`), context `BudgetContext{used, limit, turns_used, turns_limit}`, where used counts the other beams plus the harness's own. `AnswerBody` gains `budget {tokens, turns}`, the order's new limit, set only on an extension. An extend without `budget`, from an older bridge, leaves the harness's limit as it was, and its next call hails again. The schema checks the options and context of a budget hail and the answer's `budget`. Golden files may now have variants named `<type>.<variant>.json`: `hail.budget.json` and `answer.budget.json`.
- **sdk harness.** `budgetPolicy` is the first PreToolUse hook. With no limit it does nothing, nor for a call `gitPushPolicy` denies, which never reaches `canUseTool`. Otherwise it yields once (`setImmediate`) to let messages already read reach the tally, then asks for the call while over budget and records its tool_use_id. If the CLI sends the hook request before the assistant line that starts the turn, the check lags one turn. `canUseTool` runs a budget gate first. Over budget, it waits until no other hail is outstanding (the harness's answer entries are empty), checks again, and raises one budget hail, which concurrent calls share. The hail waits only on the withdraw and the query's abort, and each call waits on it under its own signal. A budget hail never meets another hail of the order. `extend` takes the answer's limit and lets the call through: a call the budget alone asked about is allowed, an `AskUserQuestion` goes on to its own hail. `stop` denies the call with the stop request and takes the stand-down path: every hail is withdrawn, the request goes in as a user message, the stop timeout's fallback applies, and once the checkpoint is out the run rejects with `BudgetExceeded`. That checkpoint is an ordinary one, without `stopping`. While any stop is under way the hook asks nothing: the agent must be able to hand over. A budget hail is never parked itself, and the run doesn't park while one is pending; once it is answered, later hails park as usual.
- **serve.** A run rejected with `BudgetExceeded` ends with `error{code: budget_exceeded}`. Such an order is recorded completed even when a stand_down was relayed meanwhile.
- **Holodeck.** Before every step but `usage` and `crash`, the holodeck checks the budget against its latest usage step (else `Order.usage`). Over it, it raises the budget hail with ref `holodeck-<order>-<index>-budget` and logs a `budget` step-log entry at the step's index. `extend` takes the answer's limit and runs the step. `stop` writes a placeholder checkpoint into `<work-dir>/<order>/<script length>/`, emits it and ends the run `budget_exceeded`. A run under budget reaches its first await where it did before.
- **Routing.** `regs.CaptainFixed` names the categories fixed to the captain whatever `regs.yaml` says, with why; today only `budget` ("category budget is fixed to the captain"). The campaigns plan's `campaign_land` will join it. `Classify` returns `RouteCaptain` for them before any rule, checked config or not, and `CheckAnswer` rejects every answer to them. The hail workflow then marks the hail captain-only, so `on_timeout: default_option` holds. `auto_allow` can't name `budget` anyway (only `tool_approval`), and listing it in `captain_only`, or dropping it, changes nothing. The default `regs.yaml` says so in a comment and lists no rule for it.
- **Question.** `recordHail` stores a budget hail with the bridge's question (`budgetQuestion`): "ORD-0001 has used 1.2M of 1M tokens and 140 of 150 turns. Extend by its budget again, or stop?". A field without a limit shows its usage alone ("420k tokens"). It also stores the context's four counts re-encoded and the options `[extend, stop]`, whatever the harness sent.
- **One open hail.** The store keeps one open hail per order (`InsertHail` returns the open one's id). A hail with another ref that reaches the mission while one is open is queued and recorded once the open one's answer has gone out. The open hail keeps its category and ref, so answering a question never runs `budget-extend`. A park or a recall drops the queue.
- **Answering.** `AnswerHail` needs an option for a budget hail. A decision naming one, in any case, picks it; other text is a bad request.
- **Extend.** On option 0, the mission runs the step `budget-extend` (`extendBudget`) before send-answer. `store.ExtendBudget` inserts the hail into a new table, `budget_extensions(hail_id PRIMARY KEY, order_id, granted_at)`, with INSERT OR IGNORE. Only when the row is new does it increment `budget_extended` and log "budget extended for hail H-0001: limit now ...", in the same transaction. The extension is keyed by its hail and a re-run adds nothing. The table is created with `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS`, and older databases gain it on open. The answer carries the new limit: the budget resolved for the mission's crew member times `1 + budget_extended`.
- **Stop.** Any other option goes to the beam as is. The harness checkpoints and ends `budget_exceeded`. The mission pulls the checkpoint as usual and fails the order with that code, even with a stand_down or recall pending; it then logs "recall superseded by the budget stop" (or `stand_down`) first. A captain who wants the work continued issues a new order from the checkpoint.
- **Backstop.** `comms.Sink` gains `UsageRecorded`, which the pump calls in the inbox transaction of a usage report that changed the order's row. The bridge's `budgetCeiling` resolves the budget for the order's assignee, else the reporting crew member, and sums every beam's row. When usage passes 125% of the current limit in tokens or turns, it sends the mission a belay with reason "budget ceiling passed". It uses BelayOrder's check (`belayCheck`), message and log entry (`orderRequest`, shared with `requestOrder`), in the same transaction. Only an order whose mission can take a belay is belayed: away, hailing, or parked unclaimed. A late report for an ended order, or for a split one, belays nothing. The request's log entry carries the payload mark `backstop: budget_ceiling`, and an order whose log holds it is not belayed again: reports keep coming until the belay lands. A captain's belay with the same reason has no mark. The run's report may sit right behind the usage report that passed the ceiling. The mission therefore drains what is queued behind the backstop's belay (`drainReport`, waiting up to 2s for each message): a report from the beam completes the order and logs "budget ceiling belay set aside: the order's report came in first". Anything else is dropped and the belay goes on.
- **Output lag.** Streamed assistant messages carry no final output usage (#117). Between results a run's output count is a lower bound, and an order usually runs as one query. A check against tokens can fire late by the running query's output; turns and input are exact. `includePartialMessages` would give each message's final output in its `message_delta`. It was left out. It sends every streamed delta through the SDK's pipe, and a `message_delta` names no message id, so tying it to one means tracking `message_start` per parent tool use id, an ordering the SDK doesn't document. Cache reads dominate an order's tokens, and the 125% backstop has room for the lag. The lag is documented in the sdk harness and the README.
- **Already over.** An order is sent whatever its usage, on a recall or a watch change, and hails at its first tool call.
- **Tests.** Store: `TestExtendBudget`, and `TestOpenAddsPhase4Schema` gains `budget_extensions`. Regs: `TestNeverAutoAnswered` gains `budget` (default and both fixtures), and `TestBudgetFixedToCaptain` checks it with a `captain_only` rule for it, without one, under an auto_allow that fits any command, unchecked, and an auto_allow rule naming it refused. `FuzzClassify` is unchanged: it fuzzes Bash commands alone and enumerates no categories. Comms: `TestSchemaBudget`, and `TestPumpUsageRecorded` checks `UsageRecorded` runs for a report that changed the row only. Bridge, on holodeck: `TestBudgetHailExtend` (a 10-turn budget hails at turn 11 under a regs.yaml that drops budget from captain_only, auto-allows any command and times out to default_option 0; the timeout holds, a free-text answer is refused, "Extend" extends, `budget_extended` is 1 and the order finishes), `TestBudgetHailStop` (a checkpoint in the cargo bay, the order failed `budget_exceeded`), `TestBudgetCeilingBelays` (usage past 125% belays once across two reports, its entry marked, and a late report for the ended order belays nothing); on a fake provider, `TestMissionQueuesHailBehindOpenOne` (a budget hail behind an open question keeps out of its answer and extends once answered itself) and `TestBudgetStopSupersedesRecall`; `TestBudgetQuestion` and `TestCeilingPassed`. The drain has no test of its own. combadge: "sdk harness budget (#119)" (a 10-turn budget, tokens with other beams' usage, an order sent over budget, concurrent calls sharing one hail and a question after the extension, a hook request ahead of its message, a budget hail held until an open question is answered, a park after an extension, stop with the agent's checkpoint, stop with the fallback's), and the holodeck through serve (extend, an order sent over budget, stop with its checkpoint and `error{code: budget_exceeded}`).

## Budget views (#120)

Dated 2026-11-13. Builds the surfaces part of section 6 of `phase-4-plan-2026-11-12.md`, on top of "Budget ledger (#118)" and "Budget enforcement (#119)".

- **OrderView.** `budget_tokens` and `budget_turns` stay the order's own budget, 0 for none, as `order.create` and `order.budget` take it. `limit_tokens` and `limit_turns` are the current limits: the budget resolved for the assignee (`resolveBudget`) times 1 + `budget_extended`, 0 for unlimited. `budget_source` names each resolved field's source (`order`, `division` or `default`). The view also gains `budget_extended` and `usage {input_tokens, output_tokens, turns}`, summed over the order's beams. `orderView` is now a `Daemon` method, as it needs the roster.
- **Reading.** `store.Order` gains `Used` (a `UsageTotal`) and `AssigneeDivision`, read with the order by subqueries in `orderCols`, the way `OpenChildren` is. Every view, the console's snapshot included, takes no extra query per order. A directed order without a division resolves its budget for its assignee's division, as `sendOrder` does.
- **order list.** A USAGE column after PROGRESS: `420k/1M 42/150t`, tokens (input and output) then turns against the limits, each part only when that field has a limit, and `-` with neither. Usage without a budget is left to `--json` and the console, which keeps the column about budgets. Counts use the bridge's `ShortCount`, now exported, which gains a `B` step for billions (as does the console's `shortCount`). There is no `order show`; turns sit in the list with a `t` suffix.
- **order budget.** `bmus order budget <id> [--tokens N] [--turns N]`, RPC `order.budget` (`OrderBudgetArgs`, pointer fields), returns an `OrderBudgetResult`: the `OrderView` and the order's open budget hail, if any. An absent field keeps the order's value, and 0 hands the field back to the division's budget, as at creation. At least one is required. `store.SetOrderBudget` reads the order, runs the bridge's check and writes the change and its log entry in one transaction; a change that changes nothing writes nothing. An ended order is refused ("order ORD-0001 is done, too late to change its budget"), and so is a split one ("has split into child orders"), whose own mission has ended.
- **Cap.** Each budget field is at most `MaxBudget`, 1e15, in `order.create`, the order file, `order.budget` and the roster (`CheckBudgetField`), and a negative one is refused as before. The limit, times 1 + the extensions, then stays far inside an int64. bmus has no sentinel for an order that is unlimited in a division with a budget: the captain gives it a budget too large to reach, such as 1e15, which the README says.
- **Extensions.** Setting a budget keeps `budget_extended`. The new limit is the new budget times 1 + the extensions, and the change says so: the log entry is "budget set: limit now 10k tokens, 40 turns, with 1 extension", and the CLI prints "order ORD-0001 limit now 10k tokens (order), 40 turns (order), with 1 extension". Resetting the count would quietly undo answers the captain gave, and a budget hail's extend keys on its hail, so the count stays the record of those answers. A captain who wants an exact limit after extensions divides by 1 + the count.
- **Open hail.** When the order has an open budget hail, the result carries it with the limit its extend would set (the budget times 2 + the extensions), and the CLI prints a second line: "H-0007 is open: extend makes the limit 15M tokens, 300 turns". The extend's answer carries that limit.
- **Running orders.** A change reaches a running beam only with the order's next send, a recall or a watch change, since the order command alone carries the limit. A lower limit would stay unknown to the beam, which would never hail, and the 125% backstop would belay the order without a checkpoint at its next usage report. An order that is away or hailing may therefore only have its limits raised: lowering either one is refused with "order ORD-0001 is away: recall it before lowering its budget". A limit set on a field that had none counts as lowering. Raising is safe: the backstop reads the higher limit at the next report.
- **Refresh.** A usage report writes no log entry, and the console's stream sends views behind log entries. `InboxTx.RecordUsage` notes the order when it writes its row, and `RecordInbox` hands the log hook a refresh once the transaction commits: a `LogEntry` with ID 0 and kind `refresh` (`store.RefreshKind`) that is in no log. The stream sends that order's view with no event id, as it sends status events, and moves no log position. A stream that misses one, dropped or reconnecting, gets the usage with its snapshot or the order's next entry.
- **Console.** The away-missions card gains a usage line under the progress note. With a budget it reads "Budget", then each field with a limit as "420k / 1M tokens" over a 4px meter (`role="meter"`), green, gold from 80% and red from 100%, the text marked " (over)" and the meter capped at full from 100%; a field without a limit shows its count in grey. Without a budget it reads "No budget" and the counts in grey. The card reads the limit fields.
- **Mission report.** The report page (`/report/<order>`) gains a Budget panel before the Report panel, from the report's `order`, which is the same `OrderView` and already carries the limits, the sources, the extensions and the usage. Each field reads like the card's ("820k / 1M tokens", " (over)" from 100%), then its source ("order budget", "division budget", or "no limit" for a field without one) and the card's meter (`UsageMeter`, now shared). An Extensions row says "none" or "1: each limit is 2 times its budget". Without a budget it reads "No budget." and the usage. A usage report that lands after the order is done still counts, as the ledger keeps every report.
- **Tests.** Store: `TestSetOrderBudget` (a kept field, the check's values and refusal, no change, a missing order, the summed usage and the assignee's division) and `TestUsageRefresh` (a written row hands over a refresh, a lower seq and a rollback none). Bridge: `TestRPCOrderBudget` (own budget, limits and sources for a directed order taking its crew member's division; usage over two beams; on the away order, raises that keep the extension and log once, 0 back to the division, lowered tokens and turns refused; an open budget hail and its extend limit; the cap; the bad requests and not found; a pool order lowered; a withdrawn and a split order refused), `TestConsoleUsageRefresh` (a usage report reaches a console stream as an order event with no id and no log entry), `TestShortCount`, a roster over the cap in `TestLoadRoster`, and `TestRPCCommands` and `TestConsoleActions` check the new fields. CLI: `TestOrderBudget` (the USAGE cell before and after a change, `--json`, the bad requests and the cap, an order file over the cap, a withdrawn order refused), `TestCLIEndToEnd` refuses a change on its done order, `TestUsageCell` and `TestBudgetLine` (with the open hail's line). Console: vitest for `shortCount`, `orderUsage`, the card and the report's `BudgetFacts`, each with a budget, without one and overspent. The e2e shows the card's usage with no budget and refuses setting one while the order is away, shows a pool order's usage against its 1M budget and a raise to 2M, and checks the report's Budget panel of a done order with a 1M budget; `holodeck.json` and `work.json` gain a usage step. `TestConsoleOrderReport` checks the report's order carries a late usage report.

## Pushed record row (#139)

Dated 2026-11-14. Amends the mission record row of "Console push (#4)" and "Console mission report (#104)".

- **Footer.** The "pushed to <branch>" marker sits on the footer's left side, after the status chip. Push, "Force push" and "Mission report" stay on the chip's line while the left side has 14rem, and wrap to the right below it on narrow widths. "Force push" takes the small size of the other two.
- **Messages.** The push confirm, the "Pushed." result and the rejection or error alert take full lines under the footer. The result and the alerts are left-aligned, and the result's list has no marker. Each repo's line reads `app: <branch> at <commit>`, then the redacted remote, which wraps to its own line when the row is narrow.
- **Clearing.** The "Pushed." result clears itself 15 seconds after it shows (`PUSH_RESULT_MS`), and Push takes the focus if the result had it. A new push restarts the timer, and unmounting the row stops it. The "pushed to <branch>" marker stays, so the row still says where its work went. The rejection and error alerts stay until the next action.
- **Tests.** The e2e push test runs at 1440px and checks, after a push and after a force push, that the buttons sit right of the chip on its line and the result list's `list-style-type` is none. Vitest checks the result's timer with fake timers: still set at 14.9s, run at 15s, restarted by a new push, and none for a rejection.

## Order dependencies (#142)

Dated 2026-11-14. Builds `campaigns-plan-2026-11-03.md` section 4.2, the first half of Campaigns stage 1 (#141).

- **Table.** `order_after (order_id, after_id)`, both referencing `orders`, primary key on the pair, and an index on `after_id` for the cascade. `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` and `CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS` add both to an older database. No column and no new workflow step: `workflowVersion` stays as it is.
- **Reads.** `store.Order` gains `After` (every order it waits on) and `WaitingOn` (the afters not done, whatever this order's state is), both read with the order through `orderCols`, in the afters' creation order. A claimed or ended order keeps its `waiting_on` as read; the CLI and the console show the wait only for a pool or directed order. `OrderView` carries them as `after` and `waiting_on`, always arrays. `waiting_on` is an addition to the issue: the console's snapshot leaves out most ended orders, and neither client should work out what is done from a partial list.
- **Insert.** `InsertOrder` refuses, in its transaction and before writing anything, an `after` naming no order, the order itself (its id is the next one), or an order that ended failed, belayed or withdrawn (`store.ErrAfter`, wrapped). `CreateOrder` turns that into a bad request with the store's text, e.g. "after ORD-0001: it ended withdrawn and will never be done". An order already done is accepted and satisfied. A split order is accepted and waits. A repeated id counts once.
- **Claims.** Both claim queries skip an unclaimed order with a row in `order_after` whose order isn't `done` (`waitingSQL`). A split order counts as not done until `settleSplit` settles it done. The directed claim's already-away branch is unchanged: an order away passed the check when it was claimed, and done is final.
- **Cascade.** `settleFamily`, which runs in every transaction that ends an order, calls `cascadeAfter` for an order ending failed, belayed or withdrawn. It withdraws each order waiting on it that is still pool or directed with no parked mission, in rowid order, logged on the dependent as "order withdrawn: it waits on ORD-0001, which was belayed" ("which failed", "which was withdrawn"; kind `order`, payload `reason` and `after`). Each withdraw runs `settleFamily` in turn, which carries the cascade down the chain in the same transaction. There is no summary entry on the order that ended: each dependent's entry names it.
- **Cascade inside a belayed family.** `cascadeAfter` skips a dependent whose parent is belayed. In the same transaction that parent's `cascadeBelay` withdraws it with its own cause, "order withdrawn: its parent ORD-0001 was belayed", and reports it withdrawn in the parent's summary. Without the skip, a child waiting on a sibling the cascade had just withdrawn would carry the after's cause and read "already withdrawn" in the summary. A dependent outside the family still gets the after's cause.
- **Claimed dependents.** A dependent that is away, hailing, split, or recalled with its mission parked is left to run, and nothing is logged for it. Every claim found its afters done, so only a row written another way (the store test inserts one away) reaches this case.
- **Wake.** The mission's `order-done` step settles through `settleReport`, which then wakes every crew member on deck (`wakeOnDeck`) when an unclaimed order waiting on the settled order now has all its afters done (`store.HasReadyDependents`, driven from `order_after`). The check walks up through each parent that the settle made done, which covers a split parent that its last child's report settles done. It is best effort, as `wakeDivision` is, and the tours' idle re-check claims a waiting order whose wake was lost. The crew member whose mission ended claims again on its own after the mission.
- **Recall and route.** Route takes a waiting order as any unclaimed one, and it keeps waiting. Recall refuses it with the existing pointer to route, and belay with the pointer to withdraw. Withdrawing it cascades to the orders waiting on it. An `after` recalled back to the pool is open again, and its dependents keep waiting.
- **Results.** `order.withdraw` answers `OrderWithdrawResult`: the `OrderView` and `waiting`, the orders its transaction withdrew down the chain (`store.WithAfterCascade` collects them). `OrderBelayResult` gains `waiting` as well. For a belay queued for a mission it lists the orders waiting on the order, down the chain, as the request read them (`store.WaitingChain`); the mission's settle withdraws them later. For a split order it lists what its cascade withdrew, its children's dependents included. Both lists are always present. The CLI appends ", and 2 orders waiting on it (ORD-0002, ORD-0003)" to the withdraw and belay lines. The console's API returns the same results, and its withdraw and belay controls show no result text.
- **Status.** A waiting order counts as open. A waiting red order still puts the bridge on red alert: it is an open red order the captain may want to unblock. Its reason names the wait, "ORD-0002 red alert (waiting on ORD-0001)" (`StatusSnapshot.RedWaiting`).
- **Splits.** `OrderSplitChild` gains `after`, earlier children of the same split by 0-based index. `checkSplit` refuses a child's own index, a later one or a negative one ("child 2's after names child 2: a child waits on earlier children only"), and drops repeats. The children carry the indexes as `store.Order.AfterSiblings`, which `InsertChildOrders` turns into the earlier children's ids in its one transaction. It refuses a bad index with `ErrAfter` as well, which `create-children` treats as a refused split. `splitQuestion` shows the wait with the question's own numbering, each child once as `checkSplit` takes it: "2) child two [ops, red, after 1]", "after 1 and 2" for two.
- **Combadge and schema.** `split_order`'s zod schema takes `after` (integers from 0) and refuses an index that isn't below the child's own before any hail. The agent's question line gains ", after 1". The tool description and Command's system prompt line say that children run side by side unless `after` names earlier children. The protocol schema takes `after` as unique integers 0 to 8, at most 9 of them. A new golden variant, `hail.order_split.json`, carries a child with `after: [0]` through the Go and TypeScript golden tests.
- **CLI.** The order file takes `after: [ORD-0001, ...]` and `bmus order create` takes `--after` (repeatable), merged with the file's without repeats. `bmus order list`'s PROGRESS cell reads "waiting on ORD-0001, ORD-0003" for a pool or directed order with orders still in `waiting_on`, and the progress otherwise.
- **Console.** The open-orders card's state reads "In the pool, waiting on ORD-0001" or "Queued for WORF, waiting on ORD-0001". The feed's `Views` adds the views of the unclaimed orders waiting on an order to every entry naming that order, so the wait clears on the card when the order it waits on is done.
- **Tests.** Store (`after_test.go`): a red order after an open one isn't claimed by either claim, nor while its after is recalled to the pool, and is claimed once the after is done; an order after a split order waits until the split settles done; insert refuses an unknown order, the order itself and failed, belayed and withdrawn orders, writing nothing, and accepts a done one; belaying the first of a chain withdraws the second and the third in the belay's commit and leaves a claimed dependent away, a re-run writes nothing, and failed and withdrawn cascade too; `InsertChildOrders` resolves sibling indexes and refuses a later one; `HasReadyDependents` looks only at the settled order's dependents; a belayed split parent withdraws a child waiting on a sibling with the parent's cause; `WaitingChain` and the withdraw's collector name the chain. Bridge: `TestSettleReportWakesReady` (a stubbed `wakeSend` sees the crew on deck woken only by the settle that readies a dependent), `TestOrderBelayWaiting`, the status reason with and without a store, `TestSplitAfterRunsInOrder` on holodeck (a red second child with `after: [0]` stays in the pool while a second ops crew member is idle, and is claimed after the first child's done entry), `TestOrderCreateAfter` (RPC refusals, the view, `Views`, withdraw cascading), `checkSplit` and `splitQuestion` cases. Schema cases for `after`. CLI: `TestOrderAfter` (`--after`, the PROGRESS cell, a refused after) and the cell's cases. combadge: the split hail with `after` and its zod refusals. Console: vitest for the card, a waiting order next to a normal one, and the wait clearing on a feed update.

## Order bases (#143)

Dated 2026-11-14. Builds `campaigns-plan-2026-11-03.md` section 4.3, the second half of Campaigns stage 1 (#141), and completes the stage.

- **Form.** A repo's `base_ref` may be `order:<id>`, where `<id>` is an order id as the store makes them (`ORD-` and four or more digits). Anything else after `order:` is a bad request, suffixes such as `order:ORD-0001~1` included (`checkOrderRepos`). A git ref can't hold ':', so the form takes no ref away from the captain.
- **Insert.** `CreateOrder` checks the bases after the repos (`checkOrderBases`). The named order must exist. One that ended failed, belayed or withdrawn is refused as #142 refuses such an `after`, in the repo's words: "repo app: base_ref order:ORD-0001: it ended withdrawn and will never be done". An order with child orders is refused with them named, "ORD-0001 was split into ORD-0003, ORD-0004: base it on one of them" (`splitWhy`), whether it is still away or has settled. A done order's done entry must name the repo with its work fetched into the clone, with no fetch or reject error and a full commit id (`orderWork`). Its branch must not have been jettisoned (`branchJettisoned`: "ORD-0001's branch was jettisoned on 2026-11-14; its work may be gone"), and the commit must resolve in the clone. An open order must list the repo in its own repos. Its work can't be checked yet, and `ship-repos` checks it when the dependent starts. Each refusal is a bad request and writes nothing.
- **Insert window.** The checks read the store outside the insert's transaction. For an order that ends failed, belayed or withdrawn in that window, `insertAfter` refuses it inside the transaction. One that is split, or ends done without the repo's work, in that window is accepted: the split's cascade or the dependent's `ship-repos` then catches it, and the latter fails with `repo_failed`.
- **After.** The base's id is added to the order's `after` when absent, once, after the afters the captain gave. The wait, its display and #142's cascade then apply unchanged: a base order that ends failed, belayed or withdrawn withdraws its unclaimed dependents.
- **Ship.** `bundleRepoFrom` resolves the base only when no checkpoint ships (`orderBaseCommit`). The named order must have no child orders, must be done, and its done entry must name the repo as at insert. The commit must be in the bridge's clone (`git rev-parse --verify --quiet <commit>^{commit}`), with every object it reaches (`git rev-list --objects --quiet <commit>`), so a clone missing part of the work fails once instead of retrying the bundle. Each failure is `errRepoPermanent`, and the mission fails with `repo_failed`. There is no fetch from origin: the work is already in the clone, and an upstream that can't be reached doesn't hold the order back. `bmus/<order>` is set to the commit and bundled with its history, as for any base. The shipped entry reads "repo app shipped to the beam at <commit> (order:ORD-0001)", with `base_ref` and `from` both `order:ORD-0001` in its payload. The beam gets the base_ref as given, and combadge only quotes it to the agent.
- **Checkpoints.** A dependent that resumes from its own checkpoint ships the checkpoint as before and never reads the base again. Only the first ship, or a re-ship with no usable checkpoint, starts from the named order's commit.
- **Splits.** A split child takes its parent's repos, a base included, with no `after` of its own for it. The parent was claimed only once the named order was done, and done is final.
- **Split base.** A split order brings back no work of its own. `InsertChildOrders` withdraws, in the split's transaction, the unclaimed orders (pool or directed, no parked mission) with a repo based on `order:<parent>` (`cascadeBase`). Each is logged "order withdrawn: ORD-0001 was split into ORD-0003, ORD-0004: base it on one of them" (kind order, payload `reason` and `base`), and its withdraw cascades to the orders waiting on it through #142's `settleFamily`. A dependent already claimed is left to run, and its `ship-repos` fails with `repo_failed` and the same text.
- **Jettison.** `protectedBranches` keeps `refs/heads/bmus/<id>` for each open order with a base of `order:<id>` on that repo, without resolving it through git. `ship-repos` needs only the commit. The branch keeps that commit reachable in the clone and in reach of the captain until every dependent has ended.
- **Jettison race.** An order can be created between jettison's re-read of the bases under the repo's lock and its delete: create takes no repo lock. After the delete, `restoreBases` reads the open orders' `order:` bases again and creates each deleted branch one of them names at its old commit (`update-ref --stdin` with `create`), logged on the named order as "branch bmus/ORD-0001 restored in repo app: an open order names it as its base". It counts as kept, and gets no jettison entry. A create that lands after this second read still ships: the delete leaves the objects in the clone until a gc, and `ship-repos` needs only the commit. That window is left open as benign.
- **Report.** `baseCommits` is unchanged: the shipped entry's `from` equals its `base_ref`, so the base is the named order's commit and the diff holds only the dependent's own work. The page's order panel shows such a repo as "app @ order:ORD-0001 (<first 12 hex of the commit>)", from the repo's diff base, and as the plain base_ref when the report has no base. Other base refs show as before.
- **CLI.** The order file takes the form in `base_ref`. `bmus order create`'s help and the README describe it.
- **Tests.** Bridge: `TestOrderCreateBase` (an unknown order, malformed ids, a repo the named order didn't use, done or open, withdrawn, split with and without children, done without a done entry, done with unfetched work, a jettisoned branch and a commit missing from the clone are bad requests that insert nothing; done and open bases are taken, each once in `after`), `TestBundleRepoFromOrderBase` (the done entry's commit with no fetch, the refusals as `errRepoPermanent`, and a checkpoint shipped while the named order is still open), `TestMissionFailsOnOrderBaseMissing` (an open base that ends done without the repo's work, or with a commit the clone lacks, fails the dependent with `repo_failed` and nothing sent), `TestSplitOrderBase` (the split withdraws the dependent with the children named and its own dependent down the chain, a new order naming the split order is refused, and a split child of an order based on `order:<id>` ships that order's commit), `TestJettisonKeepsOrderBase` (`older_than` 0s with `yes` keeps the named order's branch while the dependent is open, then while only a child order of an ended parent names it, and takes it once both are withdrawn), `TestJettisonRestoresBase` and `TestOrderBaseRunsOnWork` on holodeck (the dependent is claimed after the base's done entry, ships its commit, and its first commit's parent is that commit; the report's base is that commit and its diff holds only the dependent's lines). CLI: `TestOrderBaseCLI`. Console: vitest for the base line and `baseText`.

## Order rebase (#148)

Dated 2026-11-14. Lets the captain, and Command with the captain's approval, change the base and after of orders nobody has started, on top of "Order dependencies (#142)" and "Order bases (#143)".

- **Unstarted.** An order can be changed while it is pool or directed, has no parked mission (`parked_at` is NULL) and has no checkpoint. Every claim leaves one of those behind: a claimed order is away or hailing, a recalled one keeps its parked mission, and settling clears `parked_at` only as the order ends. Anything else is refused with what it is: "order ORD-0003 is away: a crew member has started it on its current base", "... was recalled and keeps its parked mission, whose work is built on its current base", "... has a checkpoint, ...", "order ORD-0003 ended done", "order ORD-0003 is split: its mission has ended, and its work is in its child orders". There is no option to discard started work: belay the order and create a new one.
- **Store.** `store.ReorderOrder(id, OrderChange{Repo, Base, AfterAdd, AfterRemove}, entry)` reads the order with its repos, checks it is unstarted, applies the change, checks it and writes it, with one log entry, in one transaction. `OrderChange.Apply` is the pure part, shared with the hail's question. A new base of `order:<id>` replaces the `after` entry the repo's old `order:` base implied, and a plain ref drops it, unless another repo's base still names that order. Then `AfterRemove` drops ids, refusing one the order doesn't wait on, one in `AfterAdd` too, and one a new base implies ("after ORD-0001: order ORD-0003 is based on order:ORD-0001, so it waits on it; rebase ORD-0003 to drop the wait"). Then `AfterAdd` adds ids, and the new base's order comes last. An explicit `after` entry naming the old base order is dropped with it: the store can't tell the two apart.
- **Checks in the transaction.** Each order added to `after` is checked as `insertAfter` checks one (exists, isn't the order, didn't end failed, belayed or withdrawn). Each new base order gets the same check and must have no child orders, also when a plain `after` entry already named it. A cycle is refused when the added order waits on this one through a recursive walk of `order_after` and of a split order's children, which it waits on to settle done: "after ORD-0003: ORD-0003 waits on ORD-0002, directly or down its chain, so the wait would be a cycle". Only the order's own edges change: checking each added order against the current graph finds every cycle. Refusals are `store.ErrReorder` or `ErrAfter`, wrapped, and write nothing. A change that leaves the order as it was writes and logs nothing.
- **Bridge.** `Daemon.reorderOrder` runs `checkOrderRepos` on the repos the change would give and `checkOrderBases` (#143) on the repo whose base changed, before the store call, exactly as `CreateOrder` does: a done base needs its work in the clone and an unjettisoned branch. The checks read outside the transaction, with the same window #143 describes; the transaction catches an order that ended or was split meanwhile. Refusals are bad requests, a missing order `not_found`. An order left waiting on nothing wakes its assignee's tour or its division, best effort.
- **Log.** One entry on the order, kind order: "order rebased onto order:ORD-0005 (was order:ORD-0001)" (" on repo app" for an order with several repos), "order after: ORD-0001, ORD-0005 (was ORD-0001)" ("none" for empty), joined by "; " when a change asks for both. The payload has `after` and `was_after`, and for a rebase `repo`, `base_ref` and `was_base_ref`. The console's feed refreshes the order's view on the entry, and the open-orders card shows the new wait.
- **RPC and CLI.** `order.rebase {id, onto, repo?}` and `order.after {id, add, remove}` return the `OrderView`; `order.after` drops repeated ids. `bmus order rebase <id> --onto <base> [--repo <name>]` prints "order ORD-0003 rebased onto order:ORD-0005, waiting on ORD-0005"; `bmus order after <id> [--add <id>]... [--remove <id>]...` prints the order's after. Without `--repo`, an order with several repos is refused.
- **Protocol.** `comms.CategoryOrderReorder` (`order_reorder`), options `[allow, deny]`, context `OrderReorderContext{changes, reason}` with `OrderReorderChange{order, onto, after_add, after_remove}`. The schema takes 1 to 10 changes, each with an order id (`^ORD-[0-9]{4,}$`) and at least one of the three fields (`minProperties: 2`), an `onto` of 1 to 256 bytes, after lists of 1 to 10 unique ids, and a reason of 1 to 1000 characters. Golden variant `hail.order_reorder.json`. The `onto` cap counts bytes of UTF-8 in the bridge (`len`) and in zod (`Buffer.byteLength`). JSON Schema's `maxLength` counts characters, so the schema keeps 256 there as a looser bound and says so in its description.
- **Tool.** combadge's sdk harness registers `reorder_orders({changes, reason})` and pre-allows it only for `--role command`, beside `split_order`. zod checks the shape before any hail, and the hail's own question is the agent's summary, which the bridge replaces. Option 0 returns "Reorder approved; the bridge applied ...", anything else an error result. Like a split, a query that raised one never parks. Command's system prompt gains one sentence.
- **Validation.** `checkReorder` refuses a hail from outside the command division, other options, a context with unknown fields or trailing values, no changes or more than 10, a blank or too long reason, a reason that isn't valid UTF-8 (its own message; `encoding/json` turns invalid bytes into U+FFFD, which makes this a guard for other callers), an order id that isn't one or is named by two changes, a change asking for nothing, an `onto` with surrounding space, a leading '-', a control character, more than 256 bytes or `order:` without an id, and after lists that are too long, hold a non-id or a repeat, or share an id. `classify` runs it first, and a malformed hail is decided at once by step `deny-reorder` ("invalid order reorder: <why>", by policy, route auto).
- **Question.** `recordHail` stores the bridge's question (`reorderQuestion`): "Reorder 2 orders: 1) ORD-0003 "<title>" (pool): base main -> order:ORD-0005; after none -> ORD-0005; 2) .... Command's reason: "<reason>"". Each change shows the order's state and current base and after, and the ones it would have, or "no such order", or `Apply`'s refusal. A line for an order a crew member has started ends "(will be skipped: <why>)", read through `store.StartedWhy`, the read-only form of the transaction's check. The reason comes last and quoted with `%q`: Command's text can't pass for part of the change list. Text is one line and clipped. A context that doesn't decode reads as malformed.
- **Routing.** A hail from Command goes to the captain whatever `regs.yaml` says (#149). The default `regs.yaml` also lists `order_reorder` in `captain_only`, as `captain_only[8]`, after the existing entries, so rule indexes in logs stay put. That makes it captain-only, and `on_timeout: default_option` holds. It is not in `regs.CaptainFixed`: a captain may drop the rule and let a default option decide one.
- **Countermand window.** A reorder decided with a countermand window (a captain timeout's `default_option`, once `order_reorder` is out of `captain_only`) waits it out before anything is applied. Step `reorder-window` and a durable sleep do this through `awaitCountermandWindow`, the wait `tracker-window` uses (#54), now shared with it. The sleep is capped at the tour's settle wait. `apply-reorder` reads the hail under the store's write lock and refuses a window still open. A countermand inside the window therefore applies nothing.
- **apply-reorder.** In the mission's answer branch, for an `order_reorder` hail answered with option 0, before `send-answer`. A hail no longer decided, a window still open, a countermand away from option 0, or a context `checkReorder` now refuses refuses it whole: "reorder refused: <why>" is noted once on the hail's rationale (`NoteRefusal`) and logged on Command's order, and the answer takes option deny. Otherwise each change goes through `reorderOrder` in turn, as the captain's would, rechecked in its own transaction. A bad request or a missing order skips the change; any other error fails the step for a retry. The decision reads "applied ORD-0003; skipped ORD-0004: order ORD-0004 is hailing: a crew member has started it on its current base", and with nothing applied "applied nothing; ..." with option deny. Skips are noted once as "reorder skipped: ORD-0004: <why>" and logged on Command's order. Each applied entry carries `hail_id` and `reordered_by`, and Command's crew id. A re-run counts a change whose order already has an entry `reordered_by` the hail as applied without running it again.
- **Upgrade.** No schema change and no new workflow version: `apply-reorder` and `deny-reorder` run only for `order_reorder` hails, which nothing raised before.
- **Tests.** Bridge: `TestOrderRebaseAndAfter` (rebase onto an open order, onto a ref, after add and remove, the same change twice logs nothing, the implied after and an absent one refused), `TestOrderReorderRefusals` (claimed, parked, a checkpoint, done, withdrawn, split, cycles direct, down the chain and through a split order's children, the order itself, bad bases, a repo the order doesn't use, bad afters; nothing written or logged), `TestApplyReorder` and `TestApplyReorderRefused` (a stale change skipped and named, notes logged once over two runs, nothing applied refused), `TestCheckReorder`, `TestReorderQuestion` (with a started order's skip note), `TestReorderViews`, `TestReorderCountermandWindow` on holodeck (a policy allow with an 8s window, countermanded to deny inside it, applies nothing), and on holodeck `TestOrderRebaseRunsOnWork` (rebased onto a newer order, claimed after its done entry, ships its commit) and `TestReorderOrdersHail` (under the default `regs.yaml`, `captain_only[8]`, the bridge's question, an order claimed while the hail waits is skipped and named in the answer, the same hail from a crew member outside Command denied by policy). Store: `TestReorderOntoPlainAfter` (a split order already in plain `after` is refused as a new base, an open one is taken once). Regs: `TestNeverAutoAnswered` covers `order_reorder` in the default and both fixtures, which list it too. Comms: `TestSchemaOrderReorder`. CLI: `TestOrderRebaseCLI`. combadge: `reorder_orders` for Command only, its hail and results, zod refusals.

## Stage 2 schema (#151)

Dated 2026-11-14. The schema of `campaigns-stage-2-plan-2026-11-14.md` section 1, the first ticket of Campaigns stage 2 (#150).
