- **Hails.** `hails.order_id` and `crew_id` are nullable, and `hails` gains `deliver_to TEXT`: the id of the bridge workflow that raised the hail, NULL for a hail from a beam. A partial unique index, `hails_deliver_to ON hails (deliver_to, category) WHERE deliver_to IS NOT NULL`, allows one hail per raiser and category. `store.Hail` gains `DeliverTo`, and `hailCols` reads the three nullable columns through `COALESCE(..., '')`, so every reader gets empty strings.
- **No migration.** SQLite can't drop a `NOT NULL` in place. `hails.deliver_to` is a `requiredColumns` entry, and Open refuses an older database: "bridge.db predates the bridge-raised hails (#151); delete <path> and start the bridge again, which makes a fresh database (pre-release databases are disposable, and this one can't be upgraded in place)". Every earlier refusal now ends the same way.
- **Check order.** Open checks `requiredColumns` before it runs `schema.sql`, on the tables that already exist. The schema's new index names `deliver_to`: run first, it would fail an older database with "no such column", and checking first gives the refusal. A table that doesn't exist yet is created whole by the schema. Any database with an older `hails` table is refused, which in practice is every database that needs an `addedColumns` entry; the list stays for later columns. `TestOpenAddsModelColumns` and `TestOpenAddsPhase4Schema` give their old databases `deliver_to` to keep covering it.
- **Findings.** The `findings` table as the plan defines it, keyed by `(order_id, key)`, with `order_id` a foreign key to `orders`. Nothing writes it yet (#153 does).
- **InsertBridgeHail.** `store.InsertBridgeHail(ctx, h, entry)` records an open hail with no order and no crew member. `DeliverTo` and `Category` are required, and a hail naming an order or crew member is refused, all as `ErrBridgeHail`. A hail already recorded for the same `deliver_to` and category, open or closed, returns its id and writes nothing, so a re-run of the raising step raises nothing new. The log entry gets `hail_id` in its payload and may name the order the raiser works for (`entry.OrderID`).
- **What it leaves alone.** Unlike `InsertHail`, it sets no crew member or order hailing: no beam waits on the hail. `hailClosedAway` matches nothing for a NULL order and crew member, so deciding, timing out or countermanding the hail changes no status either. `InsertHail` ignores `DeliverTo`.
- **Audit.** Every hail query is in `internal/store/store.go`. `InsertHail`'s and `OpenHailForOrder`'s `order_id = ?` never match NULL, so an order's open hail is still its beam's. `hailClosedAway`'s subqueries yield NULL and update nothing. `ListHails`, `ListCountermandable`, `GetHail`, `GetHailLocked` and `HailMetrics` read through `hailCols` or don't read the columns. `ReadStatus` counts a bridge hail in `hails_open`, as the plan wants. In the bridge, `hails.list`, the console's snapshot and feed, `bmus status`, `hails.metrics` and `openBudgetHail` take the empty strings. Left for #152: `classifyHail`, `consultBody`, `routeCaptain`, the answer and timeout paths (`AnswerHail`, `decideOpen`, the captain timeout's belay, `notifyHailClosed`), the countermand RPC's order note (`countermandHail`) and the hail log entries all read `h.OrderID` and `h.CrewID`. `HailView` has no `deliver_to` yet and `bmus hails list` prints empty cells (#152). The console's hail card and notification show an empty order and crew member (#157). Nothing raises a bridge hail before #152.
- **Tests.** Store: `TestOpenRejectsHailsWithoutDeliverTo` opens a database made from `testdata/schema-before-151.sql`, a copy of `schema.sql` before this change, and gets the refusal, with no findings table or index added. `TestStage2Schema` checks the columns, the findings key and its foreign key. `TestInsertBridgeHail` inserts twice and gets one id and one log entry, again after the hail is decided, a new hail for another category or raiser, and the index refusing a direct duplicate. `TestInsertBridgeHailRefusals` covers the refusals. `TestBridgeHailBesideBeamHail` has a beam hail and a bridge hail on one order: the beam's dedupe, `OpenHailForOrder` and its hailing states are unaffected, closing the bridge hail leaves them hailing, and lists, the status count, the countermandable list and metrics read both. Bridge: `TestBridgeHailReaders` reads one through `hails.list`, the console's snapshot and feed, `bmus status` and `hails.metrics`.

## Bridge-raised hails (#152)

Dated 2026-11-14. Section 2 of `campaigns-stage-2-plan-2026-11-14.md` and the regulations of its section 7, the second ticket of Campaigns stage 2 (#150).

- **Raising.** `raiseBridgeHail` runs a `raise-hail` step (`store.InsertBridgeHail`, `deliver_to` the calling workflow's id) and starts `hail/<id>` as a child it never awaits. `awaitBridgeHail` reads the row (`check-raised-hail`) and waits on `hail-closed` until it is no longer open, at least every 24h like `awaitCaptain`. #155's findings workflow is the first production caller; here a test-only workflow (`testRaiser`, added through `Config.registerExtra`) uses them.
- **Classify.** A hail with `deliver_to` reads no order or crew member (`hailOrder` returns the zero order). Regulations see priority 0 and the "from Command" branch never applies. With `on_timeout: belay` the timeout holds and logs "holding for the captain (no order to belay)".
- **Advisory categories.** `advisoryCategories` holds `findings_triage`. Classify records `Advisory` and a countermand window of 0 for such a hail. Wherever the workflow would run `route-captain` (no `regs.yaml`, a `captain_only` rule, Command not on deck, a consult timeout, a rejected or escalated reply), `skip-advisory` runs instead: it closes the hail `timed_out` with the reason as its rationale (`store.TimeOutOpenHailWhy`), logs "hail H-0001 skipped, Command's alone to answer: <why>" and wakes the raiser, in one transaction. A reply that decides the hail decides it as for any hail.
- **Consult.** `consultBody` leaves out `crew`, `order`, `order_spec` and `recent_log` for a bridge hail. `ConsultBody.Crew` and `Order` are omitempty, the schema no longer requires them, `protocol.ts` makes them optional and `consult.orderless.json` is the golden variant. The sdk harness's prompt drops absent fields, and its system prompt says the bridge raised the hail when there is no order.
- **Waking the raiser.** `notifyRaiser` sends the hail's id on `hail-closed` to its `deliver_to` when that workflow is live, in the transaction that decides or closes the hail. `notifyHailClosed` calls it first, so `AnswerHail` and every `TimeOutOpenHailThen` path wake the raiser; `decideOpen` (policy, Command, the timeout's `default_option`) and `skip-advisory` call it directly, since the hail workflow is their caller. `AnswerHail` and `decideOpen` send no answer for a bridge hail: there is no mission.
- **Countermand.** `Daemon.CountermandHail` writes the row and its log entry for a bridge hail and sends nothing: no correction, no wake. The RPC's order note is skipped, so the reply has none. A raiser that acts on a countermandable decision must sleep past `countermand_until` and re-read the row before acting (plan section 2). `awaitBridgeHail` returns at the first decision and does not wait out the window.
- **Log entries.** Entries the hail workflow writes for a bridge hail name no order or crew member. The raise's entry names whatever order the raiser passes.
- **Views.** `HailView.deliver_to` (omitempty), also in the console's `api.ts`. `bmus hails list` prints `-` for an empty order or crew member (`printHails`). `bmus status` already counted the hail in `hails_open` (#151).
- **Regulations.** `regs.CategoryTrackerReview` joins `CaptainFixed`: "category tracker_review is fixed to the captain", so classify routes it to the captain under any `regs.yaml` or none and `default_option` holds. The default `regs.yaml` drops `tracker_create`, which nothing raised: `tracker_close` is now `captain_only[3]`, the option rule 4, `tag: opaque` 5, `tracker_comment` 6 and `order_reorder` 7. Old hail rows keep the rationale they were decided with. Comments in the default say `tracker_review` is always the captain's and that listing `findings_triage` under `captain_only` turns triage off. The two `internal/regs/testdata` fixtures drop `tracker_create` too.
- **Not here.** `tracker_review` still records reminders and a captain timeout until #156 drops them. `AnswerHail` refusing a `findings_triage` hail and the console's card for a bridge hail are #157.
- **Tests.** Regs: `TestFixedToCaptain` (budget and tracker_review under a `captain_only` rule, a dropped one, none, a permissive `auto_allow`, and unchecked), `TestNeverAutoAnswered` with `tracker_review` in place of `tracker_create`, `TestDefaultCaptainOnly` without it. Bridge: `TestClassifyBridgeHail` on a store holding no order or crew member; `TestApplyTimeoutBelayBridgeHail`; `TestConsultOrderless` (the golden's fields, both valid against the schema); `TestSkipAdvisory` for each of the six reasons on holodeck; `TestBridgeHailDecided` (Command decides a triage hail with no window, the captain answers a `tracker_review` hail, each raiser wakes, no mission ran); `TestCountermandBridgeHail` (the RPC succeeds with no note, and the raiser's first message is one the test sent after). CLI: `TestPrintHailsBridgeHail`. combadge: an orderless consult through the sdk harness.

## Findings from the crew (#153)

Dated 2026-11-15. Section 3 of `campaigns-stage-2-plan-2026-11-14.md`, the third ticket of Campaigns stage 2 (#150). Filing the findings as issues is #155.

- **Message.** `finding`, beam to bridge, order-bound: `{key, title, body, labels?, severity?, blocked_by?}`, in the schema, `protocol.ts` (`FindingBody`) and `protocol.go` (`FindingBody`), with `finding.json` as its golden file. `key` is `^[a-z0-9][a-z0-9-]{0,39}$`. `title` is one non-blank line of at most 200 characters, without control characters (C0 and DEL, as combadge's `ONE_LINE`). `body` is required, may be empty, and holds at most 32 KiB: the schema's `maxLength` counts characters, and the bridge and combadge count bytes of UTF-8, as for a reorder's `onto`. `labels` has at most 5 entries of 1 to 50 characters, without commas, control characters or leading and trailing spaces. A label starting with `bmus:` in any letter case is refused everywhere (`comms.ReservedLabel`, the schema's `not`, zod): the bridge sets those itself. `severity` is `low`, `medium` or `high`.
- **Blockers.** Each `blocked_by` entry, at most 10, is a finding key or a tracker ref of the form `#12` or `owner/name#12`, at most 200 characters. That is GitHub's shape, the only adapter there is. It stays loose on purpose: #155 makes a ref canonical with the tracker's `Canon` and drops one it refuses, with a note. A bare number matches the key pattern and is read as a key.
- **Check.** Nothing validates a message against the schema at runtime, so `FindingBody.Check` holds the same limits for the pump. `TestSchemaFinding` runs every case through both and expects them to agree.
- **combadge.** `file_finding` is registered and pre-allowed (`ALLOWED_TOOLS`) for every role. zod checks the limits before anything is sent, trimming the title and labels, which the bridge then never sees with surrounding spaces. The tool emits the message and returns "Finding <key> recorded: the bridge files it as an issue when the order ends." It raises no hail and touches no parking state. The run keeps the keys it sent (`RunState.findings`, kept across a park and a resume). A key it already sent replaces that finding and doesn't count again; a 21st distinct key is refused with an error result that says to revise an existing key instead. The keys live in the run only. A combadge restart or an order's next run starts the count again, and the bridge's own cap covers that. The system prompt gains one line: file what deserves its own issue outside the order's scope and carry on.
- **Holodeck.** A `finding` step emits its body as given; validation only checks it is an object, so a script can send a body the bridge must drop. The finding step checks the budget first, as every step but `usage` and `crash` does. Echo files none.
- **Pump.** Like `usage`, a finding goes straight to the store in the inbox transaction and never into a workflow. `InboxTx.RecordFinding(order, finding)` in `internal/store/findings.go` keeps it only while the order exists, is in no ended state (done, failed, belayed, withdrawn) and isn't `split`, whose mission is over too, and has the pump's crew member as assignee. A recalled order clears its assignee, so a finding the recalled run sends afterwards is dropped. A key already recorded replaces every field of its row, `crew_id` and `received_at` included, while the row is `new`; a row the findings workflow moved on keeps its content. A new key is dropped once the order holds `store.MaxFindings` (20) keys. A drop returns `store.ErrFindingDropped` wrapped with the reason, having written nothing. `RecordFinding` also writes the log entry, so the row and the entry that orders it stay together.
- **Logging.** A kept finding logs "finding <key>: <title>" with kind `finding` (`store.FindingLogKind`) on its order at the message's time, payload `{tour, seq, key}`, in the same transaction. A replacement logs again. A body that fails to decode or fails `Check`, a finding without an order and one the store drops are recorded in the inbox and dropped with a warning, like a bad `progress` body. The console tags the kind FIND.
- **Ordering.** The pump can record a beam's messages out of seq order after a reconnect. Before replacing a row, `RecordFinding` looks in the same transaction for a `finding` entry of the order, the key and the same crew member with a later (tour, seq), read with `json_extract` as `OrderLogMarked` does. One found drops the older finding with a warning. Entries of other crew members don't count: tour numbers are per crew member, and a reassigned order's runs follow each other in time.
- **CLI.** `oneLine`, which prints every log text and hail field in `bmus log` and `bmus hails list`, turns control characters (C0, DEL, C1) into spaces before collapsing whitespace. A title the bridge took can't move the terminal, and older entries are covered too.
- **Notes for #155.** A blocker ref outside the configured tracker is dropped with a note, as `Canon` refuses it. When filing, `@` mentions and cross-repo `#` mentions in the body need neutralising, or the issue pings people and links issues the crew never meant to.
- **Reading.** `store.OrderFindings(order)` lists an order's findings by key, with labels and blockers decoded (empty lists, never nil). #155 reads it.
- **Tests.** Store: `TestRecordFinding` (kept with its fields, replaced while new, the 21st key dropped while a known key still replaces, a filed row kept, another crew member's and an unknown order, each ended state and `split`, away and hailing kept). Store also: seq 10 then 9 of a key keeps 10, a later tour beats a higher seq, and a reassigned order takes the next crew member's finding. Comms: `TestPumpFindingRecorded` (kept, replaced and logged; seq 13 before 12 drops 12; bad bodies, no order and store drops warned and acked, none reaching a mission) and `TestSchemaFinding` (control characters, label spaces and reserved labels). CLI: `TestOneLine`. Bridge: `TestHolodeckFindingsRecorded` runs `fixtures/findings.json` on holodeck and finds both rows and the three log entries before the order's done entry. combadge: `file_finding` for every role (its zod refusals, the limits accepted, a replaced key, the 21st refused, no hail, the prompt line), the holodeck step through serve and in process, and its validation; the schema's title and label patterns under ajv.

## Tracker additions (#154)

Dated 2026-11-15. Section 6 of `campaigns-stage-2-plan-2026-11-14.md`, for Campaigns stage 2 (#150). It extends "Tracker interface (#54)" and its markers (#206). Nothing calls `Unlabel` or `Limit` yet: #155 and the review use them.

- **Unlabel.** `Tracker.Unlabel(ctx, ref, label)` removes one label. An empty label is refused.
  - The GitHub adapter reads the issue first, as `Close` does, and refuses a pull request or a missing issue as `ErrNotFound`. An issue without the label, compared in any case, is done with no DELETE.
  - Otherwise it sends `DELETE /repos/{o}/{r}/issues/{n}/labels/{name}` with the name as the issue spells it, escaped as one path segment (`url.PathEscape`). A `/`, `%`, `#`, `?` or a non-ASCII letter stays in the name.
  - A 404 from the DELETE reads the issue again. The label gone (someone removed it meanwhile) is done, and the label still there returns the 404.
  - The names `.` and `..` are refused. Escaping leaves dots as they are, and a segment of its own named that way would name a directory to any server or proxy that cleans paths.
- **Limit.** `Query.Limit` is the most items `List` returns. 0 is no limit, and a negative one is refused (`tracker.CheckLimit`).
  - Order: `List` returns items newest first. The GitHub adapter asks `GET /issues` for `sort=created&direction=desc`, so a limit keeps the newest issues. A parent's sub-issues come in the parent's order, which the sub-issues API sets.
  - The limit counts items that fit the query: pull requests and items filtered on the bridge (a parent's sub-issues) don't use it up.
  - The adapter reads pages through `eachPage`, which `getAll` now wraps, and stops once enough issues fit. It reads blockers afterwards, for the kept issues alone.
  - Paging is by offset. An issue filed while `List` pages shifts the next page by one, which repeats the last issue read. The adapter drops an issue it has seen by its ref, so it is listed and counted once. The new issue is missed, as it is newer than the first page.
- **The fake.** `trackertest.Fake` already kept a created item's labels and each closed item's reason (`CloseReason`).
  - It gains `Unlabel`, matching labels in any case as GitHub does.
  - `List` goes newest first, or in creation order under a `Parent`, as the adapter does, and honours `Limit`. Only `internal/tracker`'s own tests read its order.
  - `DropLabels` makes `Create` store an item without its labels and return it with `ErrLabels`. A retry under the same `Mark` returns the same item and error. `Add` ignores that error.
- **Labels on Create.** GitHub drops labels silently for a token without push access. After its links, `Create` compares the issue's labels with the ones asked for, in any case (`tracker.MissingLabels`). GitHub takes a requested label that differs from a repository label only in case as the repository spells it, and that passes; the item carries GitHub's spelling. Any missing come back as "o/r#12 created without the labels bmus:unreviewed (GitHub drops labels for a token without push access, or one was removed)", wrapping the new sentinel `tracker.ErrLabels`, with the item and its ref beside the error.
  - This follows the link-error pattern: the issue exists, so the caller gets its ref and stores it. Links come first, so a link failure is reported first and a retry adds the missing link before it checks labels.
  - An issue found under its marker is checked the same way. A retry after a labels error finds the issue by marker, creates no twin, adds no link twice, and returns the same error with the same ref. Retrying can't add the labels: a token GitHub dropped them for can't set them either. `ErrLabels` lets #155 tell this apart from a link error and record the issue as filed with a note instead of retrying it.
  - A label someone removed between the first attempt and a retry also reads as missing. Retries come within one filing step, before the review that removes `bmus:unreviewed`, so the case needs a person to edit the issue in that window.
- **Tests.** `internal/adapters/github` against the `httptest` fake:
  - `TestUnlabel`: present in another case (the DELETE uses the issue's spelling); names with `/`, a space, `%`, `#`, `?` and non-ASCII letters sent as one segment; absent with no DELETE; a 404 with the label gone (done) and with it still there (the error); a pull request, a missing issue, a bad ref, an empty name, `.` and `..` refused with nothing deleted.
  - `TestListLimit`: newest first, two pages read of four, blockers read for the three kept issues alone, pull requests not counted, sub-issues stopping at the limit in the parent's order, a negative limit refused. `TestListShiftedPage`: an issue filed between pages, the repeated issue listed once. `TestListWithBlockers` now expects newest first.
  - `TestCreateMissingLabels`: a fake that drops labels; the error names both, the ref and the blocker link come back, and a retry under the key creates nothing and links nothing again. A label the repository spells in another case passes, with GitHub's spelling.
  - `internal/tracker`: `TestFakeLabelsCloseAndLimit` (newest first), `TestFakeDropLabels` and `TestMissingLabels`.

## Findings workflow (#155)

Dated 2026-11-15. Section 4 of `campaigns-stage-2-plan-2026-11-14.md`, the fourth ticket of Campaigns stage 2 (#150). It follows the notes for #155 in "Findings from the crew (#153)". The `tracker_review` hail is #156.

- **Start.** `missionWorkflow` is now `endMission` (the mission as it was, up to telling the tour) followed by `startFindings`: a `count-findings` step (`store.CountFindings`) and, when the order has any findings, `findings/<order>` started as a child the mission never awaits. Every ending reaches it: done, split, failed, belayed, the recalled path that tells no tour, and a mission whose bridge-side step failed for good. Two cases skip it: DBOS shutting down, which leaves the mission pending, and a `count-findings` step or child start that fails for good, which ends the mission in ERROR. The new steps follow the mission's last earlier step, so a mission recorded before this change replays.
- **Workflow.** `internal/bridge/findings.go`, registered as `findings` with no recovery limit. Its steps: `begin`, `list-backlog`, the triage hail's own steps, `apply-triage`, `plan-filing`, one `file-finding` per finding to file (`finding-failed` after one out of retries) and `findings-summary`. It returns `FindingsResult{Filed, Dropped, Failed, NotFiled}`, and the rows hold the outcome for #156, which raises its review after filing. Note for #156: rows can stay `new` (no tracker, or a findings workflow that ended in ERROR), and the review should list only rows the workflow moved on.
- **Begin.** It records `Since` (the time it ran, every marker's `Since`), the database's nonce, the order's title and the findings still `new`. With no tracker it logs "findings of ORD-0001 not filed: no tracker configured" and the workflow ends with the rows `new`.
- **Backlog.** `list-backlog` calls `List` with `Limit` 200, open issues newest first. It retries like filing. A failure skips triage with "the backlog could not be read: <error>". It also cuts filing's retries to 2 (`retriesBacklogDown`): the tracker is likely down, and 5 slow retries per finding would hold filing up for long.
- **Triage hail.** A `findings_triage` hail with no options, raised with `raiseBridgeHail` and awaited with `awaitBridgeHail`. The bridge writes the question of the plan. The context is `{order, title, findings, backlog, backlog_omitted}`:
  - findings are numbered from 1 in key order, each with key, title, body cut to 2 KiB, labels, severity and blockers;
  - backlog entries (ref, title, labels, blockers) are added while the encoded context stays within the consult's 32 KiB, and `backlog_omitted` counts the rest;
  - twenty findings with 2 KiB bodies outgrow 32 KiB on their own, so the body cap halves until the findings fit or the bodies are empty;
  - findings still too big with empty bodies keep titles of 100 bytes and their first 2 labels and blockers;
  - findings that still don't fit skip triage with "the findings don't fit the triage context's 32 KiB (<n> bytes without the backlog)".
- **Command's answer.** `parseTriage` is strict. It takes one JSON object, with whitespace and a ```` ```json ```` fence around it removed, and refuses unknown fields and trailing values. Numbers run from 1 to the batch's size. `duplicates` maps a number to a ref, and `blocked_by` maps a number to refs or `f<number>`. A ref must be one of the offered backlog's once `Canon` makes it canonical. A finding can't be both dropped and a duplicate, or block itself. Anything else files everything, noted as "Command's answer was not used: <why>".
- **Applying it.** A duplicate is dropped with `duplicate_of` set, as the review lists it with the dropped ones. Dropped rows get `why`, Command's rationale cut to 2 KiB. Added blockers join the row's `blocked_by`: refs as canonical refs, `f<number>` as that finding's key. One entry sums it: "findings of ORD-0001 triaged by Command: dropped 2 (1 as duplicates), 2 blockers added". `store.TriageFindings` writes the rows and the entry in one transaction and leaves rows that are no longer `new` alone.
- **Skipped triage.** A hail `skip-advisory` closed (no `regs.yaml`, a `captain_only` rule, Command not on deck, a consult timeout, a rejected or escalated reply), a backlog that couldn't be read, findings too big for the context, or an answer that doesn't parse files everything. The workflow logs "findings of ORD-0001 filed without triage: <why>", with the hail's rationale as the reason. Nobody asks the captain (plan decision 4).
- **No countermand wait.** Classify gives advisory hails a window of 0, so triage has no `countermand_until` and acts on the decision as soon as the hail closes.
- **Plan.** `plan-filing` reads each finding to file's blockers. A key is one of the order's findings. A key the order lacks, or the finding's own, is dropped. A ref is made canonical with `Canon`, and one it refuses is dropped: another repository's ref, per #153's notes. A depth-first topological sort in key order puts blockers first and drops the edge that would close a cycle. Every dropped blocker is logged as "finding <key>: blocker <b> dropped: <why>". Canon runs inside the step, and a replay reads the recorded plan.
- **Links.** At filing time `resolveBlockers` maps a blocker that is a filed finding to its issue. A duplicate links the issue it repeats. One dropped or failed links nothing. Each blocker is linked once.
- **Filing.** `file-finding` reads its row and returns it as it is when it is no longer `new`, so a re-run after the step settled files nothing. It calls `Create` with:
  - the title, with mentions and foreign refs broken (below);
  - the agent's body as a text code block, a blank line, then "Filed by <crew> from ORD-0001 (severity high)." (no parenthesis without a severity; an empty body leaves the line alone);
  - the finding's labels plus `bmus:unreviewed`;
  - the blockers;
  - `Mark{Key: "finding <nonce> ORD-0001/<key>", Since}`.

  A re-run after a crash between the tracker's answer and the store finds the issue under its marker and files no second one.
- **Outcomes.** Filed: the row gets the ref and state `filed`, and the log "finding <key> filed as o/r#12". `ErrLabels` with a ref: filed, with the error as the row's `why` and in parentheses after the entry's text, and no retry, since a retry can't add the labels. Any other error with a ref (a link that failed): the ref is stored (`store.SetFindingRef`, state still `new`) and the step fails, and the retry's `Create` finds the issue by its marker and adds only the missing links. An error without a ref fails the step, except a blocker that no longer exists: the adapter reads every blocker before it creates anything and fails with `ErrNotFound`. Then `file-finding` reads each blocker with `Get`, drops those not found with a note ("finding <key>: blocker <b> dropped: <error>", written with the row's settle), and calls `Create` again in the same attempt.
- **Retries.** `file-finding` and `list-backlog` retry 5 times with backoff from 2s up to 2 minutes (`trackerRetries`; `Config.trackerRetries` in tests). DBOS joins every attempt's error, and `stepError` keeps the last attempt's. Out of retries, `finding-failed` sets the row `failed` with that error and logs "finding <key> not filed: <error>", and filing goes on with the next finding. A row with a ref stored already has its issue, short of a link: it is set `filed`, with the link error as `why` and in the entry, and its dependents link to it. Each tracker call has a 2-minute deadline.
- **The body.** The agent's body is filed inside a fenced code block with the info string `text`. The fence is a backtick run longer than any run in the body, and at least 3. No line of the body can close it, whatever fences, tildes, HTML blocks or entities it holds. GitHub renders nothing in a code block as a mention, a reference, a link or HTML, which covers emphasis around a mention, `&#64;` entities, issue URLs, markdown links and `<a href>`. The "Filed by" line and the adapter's marker follow the block as the bridge's own text. A markdown-aware rewrite came first, and a review bypassed it in several ways; the code block holds by construction.
- **Titles.** A zero-width space (U+200B) follows the `@` of every mention and precedes the `#` of every `owner/name#N` that `Canon` refuses (`findingTitle`). The tracker's own refs stay. The security review (#128) should confirm this is enough for GitHub's titles.
- **Summary.** `findings-summary` logs "findings of ORD-0001: filed 4, dropped 1, failed 0". Workflow entries name the order and no crew member, with kind `order`; the raise's entry has kind `hail`.
- **Idempotent notes.** The no-tracker note, the triage note (with the rows `TriageFindings` writes), `plan-filing`'s notes and the summary are written once per order: `store.AppendLogOnce` marks them with `findings_once` in the payload and writes nothing when the order's log already has an entry under that mark. A step re-run after its transaction committed then logs nothing twice. A finding's notes go with its settle, which writes only while the row is `new`.
- **Store.** `findings.go` gains `GetFinding`, `CountFindings`, `TriageFindings` (with `FindingTriage`), `SetFindingRef` and `SettleFinding`, which moves a `new` row to `filed` or `failed` with its log entries in one transaction and reports whether it did, and `AppendLogOnce`. An empty ref keeps the stored one.
- **The fake.** `trackertest.Fake` gains `FailLinks`: that many Creates with blockers file the item without its last link and return it with an error. A keyed re-Create adds the blocker links the item lacks, as the GitHub adapter does.
- **Tests.**
  - Unit: `TestFindingBody` runs the review's probes (emphasis around a mention, `-@`, an HTML block, a fence desync through one, code spans across a heading and list items, `&#64;`, `&#47;`, a cross-repo URL, a markdown link, `<a href>`, tildes, unclosed and long backtick fences) and checks each stays inside the block, closed once at the end. `TestFindingTitle`, `TestParseTriage`, `TestTriageContext`, `TestTriageContextTight`, `TestPlanFiling` (a foreign ref, an unknown key, the finding's own key and a broken cycle) and `TestResolveBlockers` (dropped, duplicate, filed and failed blockers).
  - Filing: `TestFileFindingReplay` (one issue after the row settled and after a simulated crash), `TestFileFindingLinkError`, `TestFileFindingLabelsDropped`, `TestFileFindingMissingBlocker` and `TestStepError`.
  - DBOS on fake beams: `TestFindingsTriageSkipped` (Command not on deck, no `regs.yaml`), `TestFindingsTrackerDown` (failed after 2 retries each), `TestFindingsNoTracker`, `TestFindingsWorkflowRetries`, `TestFindingsFailedBlocker` (the dependent filed without the link) and `TestFindingsLinkOutOfRetries` (filed with the link error, its dependent linked to it).
  - Holodeck: `TestFindingsTriageByCommand`, `TestFindingsTriageUnused` (an unparseable answer and a consult timeout file everything), `TestFindingsOfFailedOrder`, `TestNoFindingsNoWorkflow`, and `TestHolodeckFindingsRecorded` with the no-tracker entry. Store: `TestFindingWorkflowWrites`, with re-runs that write nothing twice. Tracker: `TestFakeFailLinks`.

## Findings review (#156)

Dated 2026-11-15. Section 5 and section 8's CLI of `campaigns-stage-2-plan-2026-11-14.md`, the fifth ticket of Campaigns stage 2 (#150). It follows "Findings workflow (#155)". The console's review card is #157.

- **Raise.** After `findings-summary`, the findings workflow runs `reviewFindings` (`internal/bridge/review.go`). Its `raise-review` step reads the rows filing moved on: `filed` rows are the issues, then `dropped` and `failed`, each by key. Rows still `new` are left out, as #155's note asked, and their count is logged once: "findings of ORD-0001 left out of the review: 2 still new". With none of the three, no hail is raised, and the no-tracker path never reaches the review. Otherwise `raiseBridgeHail` raises one `tracker_review` hail with options `[keep, discard]`, logged on the order as "the captain asked to review 5 issues filed from ORD-0001's findings".
- **Context and question.** `bridge.ReviewContext` is `{order, issues, dropped, failed}`: issues are `{ref, key, title}`, dropped findings `{key, title, why, duplicate_of}` and failed ones `{key, title, error}`. The order's id is an addition to the plan's fields, for the CLI's header. The question names no titles: "Review 5 issues filed from ORD-0001's findings (1 dropped, 0 failed): keep, discard, or answer with the refs to discard and the keys to restore."
- **Waiting.** Classify sets `RemindEvery` and `Timeout` to 0 for `tracker_review`, whatever `regs.yaml` says, which removes the reminders and captain timeout #152 left. The hail workflow still re-reads the row every 24h. The findings workflow waits with `awaitBridgeHail` and no deadline. An unanswered review changes nothing, and its issues keep `bmus:unreviewed`.
- **Answers.** `AnswerHail` checks a `tracker_review` decision from a bridge hail against its context (`checkReviewAnswer`, `parseReview`). The forms are `keep`, `discard`, `discard <ref>...`, each optionally followed by `; restore <key>...`, and `restore <key>...` alone. Words split on spaces or commas, the verbs read in any case, and each ref and key is taken once. A ref matches an issue as spelled or, with a tracker, once `Canon` makes both canonical, so `#12` names `o/r#12` on GitHub. A restore key is a dropped or failed finding's.
- **Normal form.** The decision is stored as `keep`, `discard` (every issue named, or the bare word) or `discard <ref>...` in the context's order and spelling, then `; restore <key>...` in the context's order. `restore <key>...` alone is stored as `keep; restore <key>...`. `discard` on a review with no issues is stored as `keep`. Only the bare word, trimmed, discards every issue. `discard ,` names no ref and is a bad request, as is `restore` with no keys, so an empty value can never close every issue. The option in the log entry is 1 when anything is discarded, else 0.
- **Options.** `--option 0` alone is `keep`, everything kept, and `--option 1` alone is `discard`, everything discarded, since each takes its option's text as the decision. An option given with a decision must match the decision's option, or the answer is a bad request.
- **Bad requests.** Anything else names the refs and keys the hail takes: `hail H-0002: "#9" is not a ref of the review; answer keep, discard, discard <ref>..., or restore <key>..., the first three optionally followed by "; restore <key>..." (refs: #2, #3; keys: noise, lost)`.
- **Note for #157.** The review card's "Apply" with no boxes ticked sends `keep`, never a bare `discard`.
- **Triage refused.** `AnswerHail` refuses a bridge-raised `findings_triage` hail: "hail H-0008 is Command's advisory triage; review the issues in its tracker_review hail". It was a two-line check, so it moved here from #157.
- **Acting.** The workflow parses the stored decision (it parses by construction) and runs one step per issue in the context's order, `review-keep` (`Unlabel` of `bmus:unreviewed`) or `review-discard` (`Close` as `not_planned`), then `review-restore` for each key. Each moves its row with `store.ReviewFinding`, which moves a row only from the state given and logs only when it did. A step re-run reads the row first and does nothing once it moved, and `Unlabel` and `Close` are safe to repeat. Steps retry as filing does (`trackerRetries`). One out of retries runs `review-failed`, which logs "finding a not discarded: <error>" once per key, and the rest go on with the row left as it was. `review-summary` logs "review of ORD-0001's findings: kept 3, discarded 2, restored 1" once per order, adding ", failed 1" when any action failed, and the payload carries the failed count. A review closed undecided, or a decision that doesn't parse, logs one entry instead and acts on nothing.
- **Issues gone.** A discard whose `Close` returns `ErrNotFound` counts as done: the row moves to `discarded` with the error as `why`, logged as "finding a discarded: <error>". A keep whose `Unlabel` returns `ErrNotFound` fails at once with no retries: the step records the error as `Failed`, and `review-failed` logs it and counts it.
- **Restore.** `review-restore` files the finding with #155's code: `fileFinding`'s body is now `createFindingIssue`, which takes whether to add `bmus:unreviewed`, under the same marker (`findingMark`, the begin step's nonce and `Since`). Its blockers are its refs made canonical, and for each key the issue of a finding that is filed, kept or restored, or the issue a duplicate repeats. A key whose finding is discarded, dropped or failed is dropped with a note, as is one the order lacks. Restores run in key order after keep and discard, so a restore can link a finding restored before it and never one after. An issue carrying `bmus:unreviewed` (a failed finding whose issue a lost answer had filed under the marker) has the label removed, before any link error returns. The row moves to `restored` with its ref and logs "finding b restored as #7". A ref returned with a link error is stored on the row (`store.SetFindingRefIn`, for a dropped or failed row) and the step fails, and its retry adds the link through the marker. Out of retries with a ref stored, `review-failed` moves the row to `restored` with the link error as `why`, as #155's `finding-failed` files one; without a ref it logs the failure.
- **Result.** `FindingsResult` gains `Kept`, `Discarded`, `Restored` and `ReviewFailed`.
- **Store.** `SettleFinding` and the new `ReviewFinding(ctx, id, key, from, to, ref, why *string, entries...)` share `moveFinding`. A nil `why` keeps the row's, so a kept issue keeps its filing note.
- **CLI.** `bmus hails review <id>` finds the hail through `hails.list` and refuses another category. Without flags it prints the question, then N, REF/KEY, STATE, TITLE and WHY for each issue, dropped and failed finding, then the decision once there is one. N is the number Command's triage gave the finding, its key's rank among every key in the review (`ReviewContext.Numbers`), and "-" for an entry without a key. A duplicate's WHY starts with "duplicate of #1". Titles and reasons go through `oneLine`. `--keep`, `--discard <ref>` and `--restore <key>` (both repeatable) build the decision and send `hails.answer`; `--keep` with `--discard` is refused before anything is sent, as is a blank or separators-only `--discard` or `--restore` value. The refusal points to `bmus hails answer <id> --option 1` to discard everything. No RPC was added.
- **Tests.**
  - Bridge unit: `TestParseReview` (every form, its normal form and option, the normal form parsing again without `Canon`, and the refusals), `TestAnswerReviewRefused` (the bad request's text, a mismatched option, an empty review, the triage refusal), `TestReviewBatch` (new rows raise nothing), `TestReviewStepsRepeat` (each step twice, one issue restored without the label, its blockers), `TestReviewRestoreFoundByMarker`, `TestReviewIssueGone` (discard counts, keep fails for good) and `TestReviewRestoreLinkFails` (unlabelled before the link error, the ref stored, restored by `review-failed` with the error noted).
  - Holodeck: `TestFindingsReviewExit` is plan Verification 5. `TestFindingsReviewRestore` restores a finding Command dropped. `TestFindingsReviewSurvivesRestart` stops the bridge with the review open, answers `--option 1` after the restart and finds one `review-discard` step and one summary (Verification 7).
  - DBOS on fake beams: `TestFindingsReviewActionFails` (a refused `Close` logged and counted, the keep done), `TestFindingsReviewKeepGone` (one `Unlabel` call, logged once) and `TestNoReviewWithoutFiling`.
  - #155's tests answer `keep` to the review once it opens (`endFindings`), and those checking `bmus:unreviewed` look before answering. `TestBridgeHailDecided` and `TestCloseBridgeHailRaiserDone` give their review hails a context and the review's options.
  - Store: `TestReviewFinding`. CLI: `TestPrintReview`, `TestPrintReviewNumbers` (issues, dropped and failed with interleaved keys, and an issue without a key), `TestReviewDecision`, and `TestHailsReviewCLI` against a bridge subprocess holding review hails inserted before it started, covering the table, the flags, blank flag values, the bridge's and the CLI's bad requests, and `hails answer` refusing a bad decision.

## Console review card (#157)

Dated 2026-11-15. Section 8's console of `campaigns-stage-2-plan-2026-11-14.md`, the sixth ticket of Campaigns stage 2 (#150). It follows "Findings review (#156)".

- **Which card.** `hailKind` sorts an open hail: one without `deliver_to` is a crew member's and keeps `HailCard`; a bridge hail is `tracker_review` (`ReviewCard`), `findings_triage` (`TriageCard`) or any other (`HailCard`).
- **Bridge hails.** `HailCard` drops the belay control for a hail with `deliver_to`: there is no order of its own to belay. Its meta line is the hail's id, "Bridge" and the order it is about. `bridgeHailOrder` reads the order from the context's `order`, else from a `findings/<order>` `deliver_to`. The two findings cards say "findings of ORD-0001" there.
- **Review card.** It lists each issue with a Discard checkbox and each dropped, then failed, finding with a Restore checkbox. Each row is numbered as Command's triage numbers its finding (#155): the key's rank among every key in the review, in key order (`reviewNumbers`, as `ReviewContext.Numbers` in Go). The numbers can skip within a list, so a rationale such as "3 is a typo; 2 repeats #1" names the rows numbered 3 and 2. An entry without a key has no number. Command's rationale shows once under "Not filed", prefixed "Command:", since the bridge stores the one rationale on every row it drops. Each dropped row gives its own short reason ("Dropped: duplicate of #1" or "Dropped by Command"), and a failed row its error. Each checkbox has a visible "Discard" or "Restore" label whose accessible name adds the ref or key, and its title and reason as its description. A line under the lists says what Apply does ("Apply keeps 2, discards 1, restores 1"), and Apply points to it with `aria-describedby`. Buttons follow the lists in tab order: "Keep all", "Apply" and "Discard all" behind a `ConfirmAction` that names the restores ("Close all 3 issues as not planned and restore c-typo?"). A review with no issues has no Discard all. `reviewContext` reads the context leniently: a missing list is empty and an entry without its ref or key is left out.
- **The decision.** `reviewDecision` builds the answer from the boxes: `discard` with the refs ticked in the context's order, then `; restore` with the keys ticked in the context's order (dropped, then failed). No box ticked is `keep`, and restores alone are `keep; restore <key>...`. Discards always name their refs, every issue included, so an answer never reaches an issue the card didn't show; with every issue ticked, Apply asks first as Discard all does. Keep all and Discard all carry the restores ticked (`keep; restore <key>...`, `discard; restore <key>...`) and send option 0 or 1 alone without any. The page sends a decision without an option and the bridge sets it.
- **Result.** One answer at a time: while any is pending or sent, the boxes, the buttons and the confirm step's button are disabled (`ConfirmAction` honours `disabled` in its confirm step too). "Answer sent. Waiting for the bridge." appears in an `aria-live` region and takes focus, and the feed then closes the card; a confirmed answer shows `ConfirmAction`'s own "Sent" note. A refusal shows the bridge's message in a `role="alert"` paragraph, or the confirm's, with the boxes left as they were. The console's `POST /api/hails/{id}/answer` passes `option` and `decision` to `AnswerHail` unchanged, and a bad request comes back as a 400 with the bridge's message.
- **Focus.** When the hail card holding focus leaves the panel (`HailsPanel` tracks it on `focusin`), focus moves to the card now in its place, else the one before it. With no card left it moves to the panel's heading, which stays with "All hails answered." until focus leaves it. This covers every card kind.
- **Text and links.** Titles, reasons and errors render as text children. A ref links to its issue only when it is a GitHub adapter ref (`owner/name#N`) and a registered repo's URL is that github.com repo (`issueURL`, sharing `githubCompareURL`'s parsing, which refuses `.` and `..` for the owner and the name). The console knows no tracker address, and a host guessed from the ref could point anywhere. Any other ref, the fake tracker's `#12` among them, is plain text.
- **Triage.** A `findings_triage` hail shows "Command is triaging ORD-0001's findings" beside a blinking light, with no controls. `AnswerHail` already refuses one (#156).
- **Notifications.** A bridge hail's title is "Hail from the bridge" and its order, the review's "Review from the bridge" and its order. The body is the question. A triage hail raises none, since the captain has nothing to answer.
- **Styles.** The palette stays. Review rows sit on the pad background with a left border that turns red for a ticked discard, whose title is struck through, and green for a ticked restore. Checkboxes take `accent-color` and the console's focus ring, and the triage card has a dashed border.
- **Tests.** Vitest (`test/review.test.tsx`): the card's lists, numbers (a mixed batch with interleaved keys and an issue without a key), labels, buttons and summary; Command's rationale shown once; Discard all rendered as the confirm's first step only; hostile titles and reasons (`<script>`, markdown, `<img onerror>`) as text; links for a registered github.com repo only; `reviewDecision` (nothing ticked is `keep`, refs and keys in context order, every ref named when every issue is ticked); `reviewAllDecision` and the confirm prompt with restores; `reviewContext`; `issueURL`; the triage card with no controls and no notification; `HailCard` without belay for a bridge hail and with it for a crew member's; the notification titles. The console tests render to strings and have no DOM, so clicks and focus are left to Playwright. Go: `TestConsoleAnswerReview` posts a bad ref, a mismatched option, a triage answer and a good decision through the console and checks the messages and the stored normal form. Playwright: an order on holodeck files four findings to a fake GitHub (`FakeGitHub`, a loopback server the adapter reaches through `tracker.yaml`'s `api_url`, with blocker links and a 400 for a body that isn't JSON; any path it lacks is a 404, which the adapter reads as not found); Command drops one and marks one a duplicate of an existing issue; the card numbers its rows 1, 4, 2, 3 (a-leak, d-lock, b-flaky, c-typo); the page cancels a Discard all and an Apply with every issue ticked, shows a refusal on the card, ticks a discard and a restore, and Apply sends `discard acme/app#3; restore c-typo`. Focus then lands on the panel's heading. The test checks the fake (one issue closed as not planned, one unlabelled, the restored finding filed without the label), the decided hail through `bmus hails list` and the review's summary in the log. The tests that answered the last open hail now expect focus on the heading and the panel gone once focus leaves it.

## Automatic checkpoints (#145)

Dated 2026-11-16. Follows "Hail parking (#154)", "Watch change, park and resume (#100)" and "Corrections for parked orders (#167)". The issue was revised before work began: the cadence fires on turns or on time. A review then reshaped the WIP refs, the WIP bundle and carried hails; this entry records the result.

- **Work in progress.** Every checkpoint of every harness snapshots the uncommitted work as a commit whose parent is the `bmus/<order>` tip, on `refs/bmus/wip/<order>` (`snapshotWip` in `combadge/src/repos.ts`). It covers tracked and untracked files minus ignored ones. It is built in a temporary index copied from the checkout's own, so `git add --all` rehashes only what changed, and the agent's index and work tree are never touched. `bmus/<order>` never moves. With nothing uncommitted the ref is deleted. Untracked or modified files over 100 MiB stay out and are named in the checkpoint's note. With HEAD off `bmus/<order>` (another branch, detached) or a rebase, merge, cherry-pick or revert under way, no WIP is taken, the ref is deleted and the note says why. Submodules go into the WIP as gitlinks and are restored as gitlinks: their own work trees are not snapshotted.
- **The WIP bundle.** The WIP travels in a bundle of its own, `<name>.wip.bundle`, named in the new optional `work_repo.wip_bundle_path` (`bundleWip`). It holds `refs/bmus/wip/<order>` and `refs/bmus/kept-wip/<order>`, those that exist, minus the checkpoint's commit and the base commit. A large WIP therefore can't keep a checkpoint's commits off the bridge. Reports carry no WIP.
- **What the beam last emitted.** Each checkpoint records its tip and WIP commit in the checkout, on `refs/bmus/emitted/<order>/tip` and `.../wip` (`recordEmitted`).
- **Restoring.** Every start looks for a WIP before the harness runs, for every harness (`checkoutRepos`). The shipped bundle is authoritative. The existing checkout's work is used only when the bridge has nothing newer: when the shipped tip and WIP equal what that checkout last emitted (`readEmitted`). The existing checkout is either one serve kept on a re-delivery or one `clearStopped` set aside as `<order>/.<name>.prev`. Its work is then snapshotted afresh, so work done after its last checkpoint comes along, and a checkout with no WIP left (the agent dropped its changes) restores nothing. When the bridge ships older work than the checkout last had, the shipped WIP still decides the work tree, and the checkout's differing WIP moves to `refs/bmus/kept-wip/<order>`, named in the note. That happens only for a set-aside checkout, and only when the shipped WIP isn't kept already: a kept checkout's own WIP is its live work tree, which stays as it is, and of two WIPs to keep the shipped one stays on the ref. A WIP whose parent is the tip being started is restored into a clean work tree with nothing staged (`placeWip`, `git restore --worktree`). A kept checkout whose work tree already holds it is left as it is. Any other WIP moves to `refs/bmus/kept-wip/<order>`: a later snapshot never overwrites that ref, and checkpoints carry it on. The ref holds one commit (`KeptWip`). A newer kept WIP replaces it, and the note names the commit replaced, reachable only by its id from then on. A kept WIP left in place is named in the note of every start. The set-aside checkout is removed once the checkout is done, or when it fails.
- **The note.** serve appends one line per repo to the order's handover note (`wipNote`). A restore names the files: "Uncommitted work was restored in <dir> from refs/bmus/wip/<order> (<commit>, from the shipped checkpoint), unstaged; files: a.txt, b.txt. Review it and commit what you keep." A kept WIP names `refs/bmus/kept-wip/<order>`, why it wasn't applied, its files and the `git diff` to see it. A first run gains a note this way too. The holodeck records the note in its step log, as it does any handover.
- **Harness-written notes.** A note the harness writes (sdk: park, cadence, stop fallback; holodeck: park, stop, budget stop) carries forward the agent's latest note and the carried answers not yet delivered (`chainNote`; see "Trimming the chain (#174)" below). A resumed run gets only the note of the checkpoint it resumes from, so these survive any number of harness checkpoints; earlier park, stop, cadence and WIP lines do not. The notes say the commits and the WIP show where the work stands; the transcript stays in the bridge's cargo bay and never reaches the next run.
- **Trimming the chain (#174).** A harness-written note carries forward only the agent's latest note and the carried answers not yet delivered, between the lines "--- Carried forward from earlier checkpoints ---" and "--- End of what was carried forward ---" (`chainNote`). The agent's note is this run's request_handover note, else the newest one in the note the run started from: a harness note's block gives it, and any other note is the agent's own, minus its snapshot lines (`chainedFrom`, `isSnapshotNoteLine`). An answer counts as delivered once an agent note written after it exists: answers in the starting handover (the bridge's lines and those in its block) are carried only while this run's agent has written no note, which matches what the bridge sends after an agent checkpoint, since it clears `carried` once a checkpoint of the new run is pulled. The bridge's lines are found by their opening, "While the order was parked, the captain answered hail ", each on a line of its own. Earlier park, stop, cadence, WIP, kept-WIP and snapshot lines are dropped. serve gives the harness the handover as the bridge sent it (`Order.sentHandover`), "" when the bridge sent none, so the current start's WIP and kept-WIP lines reach the agent but not the chain (`startedFrom` reads `body.handover` only for a harness run without serve, in tests). The 8 KiB cap applies to the agent's note alone, cut from its end (`CHAIN_MAX_BYTES`): it ends "[...]" and a cut line gives the bytes cut from it. Undelivered answers are never cut: the order's hails bound them. An agent note can't pass for a harness note or for the bridge's answers: request_handover stores it with "> " before any line equal to the block's begin or end line or opening like a carried answer (`agentNoteOf`, sdk and holodeck). A harness note's own text goes through the same guard, so a hail question in a park note can't pass for the block either (`chainNote`). A holodeck stop after a resume from a park names that park again, so the next resume walks on from the same step. A note written by an older combadge is read as an agent note and chained once, within the cap. A carried answer taken while a stand_down or recall is stopping the run is kept for the next send: the mission records how many carried answers the latest send-order shipped and, on a pull, drops only those (`sent` in `runMission`). Before, the stop checkpoint's pull cleared them all, and such an answer was lost (a #145 bug).
- **Park checkpoints.** When the sdk harness parks, it writes a checkpoint before it aborts the parked query (`parkCheckpoint` in `parkRun`). The checkpoint holds the bundles, the transcript and a note listing the held hails' questions. It is emitted with the held refs in `held`, a new optional field of `checkpoint_ready`. The abort waits for that write, so it lands a moment after the threshold. If a call was let through meanwhile, the abort waits for its PostToolUse and `RESULT_GRACE_MS` more, as a park does (`abortParked`). A failed write is logged and the park goes on. A run without repos writes none. A park checkpoint never counts as a stop's checkpoint (`checkpointReady` with `ordinary`).
- **Cadence.** The sdk harness checkpoints on its own when the work changed and either `--checkpoint-turns` main-loop turns (default 10) or `--checkpoint-every` (default 15m) have passed since the latest checkpoint (`cadenceCheck`). A change is a different `bmus/<order>` tip or work tree, read without moving any ref (`workKey`). The baseline is the work as the run starts, read without holding the query up. When nothing changed, both counts start again. Any checkpoint, the agent's included, resets them. The cadence waits while the run is parked, stopping or ending, and runs only for an order with repos. 0 turns either trigger off. Like a park checkpoint, a cadence checkpoint never answers a stop.
- **Flags.** serve takes `--checkpoint-every` and `--checkpoint-turns` with the sdk harness only. `--checkpoint-every` is parsed like `--hail-park-after`; it must be 0 (off) or 1m up to 596h. `bmus bridge run` takes the same two flags and passes them only when set, through `Config.CheckpointEvery` and `CheckpointTurns` and every beam's `CombadgeSpec`. The transporter adds them only when serve runs sdk (`serveArgs`). The bridge checks them the same way (`CheckCheckpointEvery`, `MinCheckpointEvery`, `CheckCheckpointTurns`).
- **Incremental bundles.** After a run's first checkpoint, each checkpoint's bundle starts at the previous checkpoint's commit by excluding that commit's parents (`BundleOptions.since`). A checkpoint whose only change is in the work tree therefore sends just that one commit. The full bundle, from the base commit, is written beside it as `<name>.full.bundle` and named in the new optional `work_repo.full_bundle_path`. When git can't write the incremental bundle (the branch moved behind the previous commit), only the full one is written.
- **Landing.** pull-checkpoint fetches each pulled bundle into the bridge's clone as `refs/bmus/checkpoints/<order>` under the repo's lock, with the object check (`landCheckpoint`, `fetchBundleAs`): a later incremental bundle needs that commit. When an incremental bundle doesn't land for good (typically a missing prerequisite), the full bundle is copied from the beam over it in the cargo bay and landed instead. A full bundle that doesn't land is left for ship-repos to judge, as before. Copying the WIP bundle beside the checkpoint's bundle is best effort (`pullWip`). Over the size cap it stays on the beam (`bundle_too_large`); any other failure leaves it there too (`checkpoint_failed`), its partial copy removed. Either way the commits land without it, and pull-checkpoint's result names it, so the mission logs it once in a step of its own (`log-wip-skipped`). The checkpoint ref goes when the order ends without work (`dropCheckpointRef`), and jettison sweeps those of every ended order (`sweepCheckpointRefs`).
- **Shipping the WIP.** ship-repos fetches the WIP refs from the WIP bundle of the checkpoint it ships from (`fetchWipLocked`). When it ships from an older checkpoint or from `base_ref`, it takes the newest checkpoint's instead, when that bundle is in the cargo bay and passes the object check. If the clone lacks the newest checkpoint's commit, its bundle is fetched first into a scratch ref. A newest checkpoint rejected because its row names a commit its bundle lacks still gives its WIP; one the object check rejects gives none. The WIP refs exist in the clone only while the shipped bundle is written, so `bmus/<order>`, the report's fetch and the push never take them.
- **Carried hails.** A beam loss carries the hails that the latest checkpoint pulled from the current run held at its park. Those are the open hail (`store.CarryHail` in the park step, named as `held_hail` in the park entry) and any queued behind it, which are recorded carried (`record-carried-hail`, with their hail workflows). Only a beam loss carries hails. A stand_down's or recall's park, or an error while parking, closes the open hail as before. A carried hail is marked in the store (`hails.carried`). It never holds the mission's open-hail slot, `OpenHailForOrder` and `InsertHail`'s one-open-hail check skip it, and closing it leaves the order's and crew member's states alone. The order goes back to `away` when its hail is carried.
- **Answers to carried hails.** The mission takes an answer to a carried hail before its check of the message's crew member: the answer names the crew member that hailed, whom a recall or a resume elsewhere may have replaced. An answer that comes while no run will take it (parked, recalled, after a resume whose send found the tour ended, or while a stand_down or recall is stopping the run, whose harness drops corrections) runs `carried-answer`. That step runs decide-hail and gives the question for the next send's handover note: `While the order was parked, the captain answered hail H-0001, which the earlier session asked: "Which way?". The answer: "left".` A `tool_approval` answered with option 0 also becomes a grant in that order: the new optional `OrderBody.grants`, `[{tool, input}]` from the hail's context, when its input is a JSON object. Only an option answer grants: a free-text answer is a deny or a question's answer, and goes into the note alone. The note lines and the grants go with every send until a checkpoint from the new run is pulled, so a beam lost before then sends them again; an answer taken after that send (while the run is stopping) stays for the next one. After that the note lines live on in the new run's harness notes, which carry them until the agent writes a note of its own (#174); the grants are dropped, since sending one again could run the call twice. The sdk harness takes each order grant once, without a hail, whenever the call comes: no `GRANT_TTL_MS`, and an unused grant carries over a park to the resumed query. An answer after the resume runs decide-hail and goes to the new run as a correction naming the question (#190). When the new run raises a hail with a carried hail's category and question, the carried one is closed, "closed unanswered: superseded by hail H-0002" (`supersede-carried`), so the captain answers once. An answer to the carried hail already on its way to the mission when the new hail closes it is lost, and the captain answers the new hail as well: a known double ask, accepted. Carried hails still open when the mission ends are closed with it, "closed unanswered: order <status>" (`close-carried`), and so are those of an order the tour's backstop ends. A recall leaves carried hails open, and an answer still reaches whichever crew member resumes the order.
- **Holodeck.** A `write` step appends `<order> step <index>` to a file in each repo and leaves it uncommitted. A hail step takes `park_after_ms`. Unanswered that long, the holodeck writes a placeholder checkpoint into `<order>/<index>/` whose note ends "The holodeck parked its script at step N.", emits it with `held`, logs a `park` step and goes on waiting. A run whose handover carries that sentence walks from step N + 1, standing for an sdk agent reading its note. The step log records an order's `grants`.
- **Versions.** The bridge uploads its own combadge bundle whenever it starts serve on a beam, so new beams always match it. A serve already running is left alone (see "Serve's environment on a beam (#86)"). A beam started before a bridge upgrade therefore runs the older combadge until the beam is replaced; on tsh, `beamctl restart` reruns serve's original command, the older bundle and its flags included. That serve gets no cadence flags and writes no WIP bundles, incremental bundles or `held`. An older bridge facing a newer combadge pulls only `bundle_path`, which may then be incremental: its fetch fails for lack of a prerequisite and the order resumes from an older checkpoint. Run matching versions when using the cadence flags or incremental bundles.
- **Upgrade.** A mission recorded before this change replays as it ran. The park step's name and result are unchanged. The new steps (`carried-answer`, `record-carried-hail`, `supersede-carried`, `close-carried`) run only for carried hails.
- **Tests.**
  - combadge, "sdk harness automatic checkpoints (#145)": a park writes its checkpoint before the abort, with `held`, the WIP bundle and the note, and leaves the index and work tree as they were; a grant from the order lets a re-issued call through once, and without it the call hails; the cadence on turns and on time fires only when something changed, and a commit counts; a second cadence checkpoint is incremental with its full bundle; both triggers at 0 never fire. The cadence tests wait on `SdkDeps.onCadence` events instead of sleeping.
  - combadge, "work in progress (#145)" in `repos.test.ts`: the snapshot skips ignored and huge files and moves no ref without `commit`; HEAD off the branch; no WIP and no WIP bundle; incremental bundles, including a work-tree-only change; a restore from the shipped bundle; a kept WIP that outlives the next snapshots, travels in later checkpoints and comes back on the next start; the set-aside checkout used only when the shipped checkpoint is the one it emitted, the shipped WIP winning over a stale one; the set-aside checkout removed on a failed checkout. Also the holodeck's park and resume in process, and the flags' refusals.
  - Bridge on holodeck: `TestParkCheckpointOutlivesBeam`, answered before and after the resume (the park checkpoint and its WIP in the cargo bay, the beam killed, the hail open and carried with the order away, the resume on a new beam with the file restored and named, no second hail, the answer in the note or as a correction, no WIP commit in `bmus/<order>`, the final bundle or the pushed branch). `TestStoppedOrderRestoresWipFromCheckout`, `TestCarriedHailSupersededByResumedRun`, `TestCarriedHailClosedWhenOrderEnds`, `TestCarriedToolApprovalBecomesGrant`, `TestCarriedAnswerAfterRecall` (answered after a recall, resumed by tuvok) and `TestCarriedAnswerUntilNewRunCheckpoints` (an answer held through a resume whose send found the tour ended, then a second beam loss before the new run's checkpoint) and `TestCarriedAnswerSurvivesNewRunsPark` (the new run parks on a hail of its own, its beam goes after that checkpoint, and run 3's note still holds the first answer, but not run 1's park line). `TestCarriedAnswerDuringStopReachesNextSend` (#174: the beam paused while the recall is sent and the carried hail answered, so the answer is taken during the stop; the next run's note holds it). On a fake beam, `TestBeamLossCarriesQueuedHeldHail` (two hails held by one park checkpoint, the second queued, both carried on beam loss).
  - Bridge units: `TestBundleRepoFromShipsWip` (the shipped checkpoint's WIP and kept WIP, the newest's on a fallback to an older checkpoint whose commit the clone lacks and to `base_ref`, no refs left in the clone), `TestLandCheckpointIncrementalAndFull`, `TestPullWipBestEffort`, `TestSweepCheckpointRefs`, `TestBackstopClosesCarriedHails`, `TestProvisionPassesCheckpointCadence`, `TestRunRefusesBadCheckpointCadence`, `TestServeArgsHailParkAfter`'s cadence cases and `TestBridgeRunRefusesBadCheckpointCadence`.
  - combadge also covers: a park's abort held by a call let through while its checkpoint is written, until `RESULT_GRACE_MS` after its PostToolUse; an order grant that outlives `GRANT_TTL_MS` and a park; the beam's differing WIP kept on the side; a dropped change staying dropped; a kept WIP named on each start and its replacement named; a kept checkout with live changes keeping the shipped WIP on the kept ref with one note line; a harness note chaining the agent's latest note; in `chain.test.ts` (#174), six resumes with a cadence checkpoint each staying under the cap with the latest agent note once, an older agent note left out, an undelivered answer kept and a delivered one not repeated, the cut, and a holodeck stop after a park's resume naming the park. Also (#174): an agent note quoting the chain's markers or an answer stays the agent's note; the cut applies to the agent's note alone; a first run's kept WIP line stays out of a harness note's chain (`serve-model.test.ts`); an sdk cadence note keeps the bridge's answer its run started with. Bridge unit: `TestCarriedNoteMatchesCombadge` (carriedNote's lines open as combadge's `CARRIED_ANSWER` expects).
  - Not end to end: the note for a WIP kept after a fallback is checked in `repos.test.ts`, since no holodeck checkpoint can be made rejectable.

## Stage 3 schema (#180)

Dated 2026-11-16. The schema and store of `campaigns-stage-3-plan-2026-11-16.md` sections 1 and 3, the first ticket of Campaigns stage 3.

- **Columns.** `orders` gains `kind` (`work` or `review`, default `work`) and `task` (the task workflow's id, `''` outside a campaign), with the indexes `orders_task (task, kind)` and `orders_created_by`. `store.Order` reads both. An empty kind is work, and any other kind, or a review with no task, is refused as `ErrOrderKind` (`InsertOrder`). `hails` gains `raise_key`, and the unique index `hails_raise ON hails (deliver_to, category, raise_key) WHERE deliver_to IS NOT NULL` replaces `hails_deliver_to`.
- **Tables.** `campaigns`, `campaign_tasks` with the partial unique index `campaign_tasks_open` on `ref` for a task that hasn't landed or been dropped, and `task_workers (task, crew_id)`, all as the plan writes them. Nothing writes `campaigns` or inserts a task yet; the CLI ticket does.
- **No migration.** A stage 2 database is refused with "bridge.db predates the campaign orders (#180); delete <path> and start the bridge again, which makes a fresh database ..." (`requiredColumns`, checked by `Open`). The list gains `orders.kind`, `orders.task` and `hails.raise_key`: the new index names both order columns. A database that passes the check was made by this schema and never has `hails_deliver_to`, so Open drops nothing.
- **Raise key.** The key is a field of the hail (`store.Hail.RaiseKey`), read back with it. A bridge raise finds an earlier hail by raiser, category and key (`InsertBridgeHail`), and the existing callers pass no key and raise as before. A beam's hail ignores it.
- **Orders once.** An order inserted under a `created_by` already used returns the first order's id and writes nothing, in the transaction that would have inserted it (`InsertOrderOnce`). It reports whether it inserted. The key is required and must be the caller's own: `captain` or `hail:<id>` would find someone else's order.
- **Task workers.** A claim that moves a work order with a task away adds the claimer to `task_workers` with `INSERT OR IGNORE` (`addTaskWorker`, from `ClaimOldestDirectedOrder` and `ClaimOldestPoolOrder`). These are the only two writes that move an order to away; a route sets an assignee with no claim, an unpark keeps the assignee, and the directed claim's return of an order already away finds the row its first claim wrote. A recall clears the assignee and keeps the row. A review's claim adds no row.
- **Review bar.** Both claim queries skip a review order whose task lists the claimer in `task_workers` (`reviewBarredSQL`). The reviewer of round 1 is no worker and may claim round 2's review, and a reviewer who claims a pooled fix order is barred from then on.
- **Eligible reviewers.** The crew members of a division who are neither dismissed nor in sickbay and have no `task_workers` row for the task, in id order (`EligibleReviewers`). A busy crew member counts, and so does one whose other work is on another task. An empty division has none.
- **Task hook.** A store hook is told of each order with a task that ended or split, in the ending transaction (`Store.SetTaskHook`, called by `settleFamily` with `TaskOrder{task, order, state}`). Every ending write calls `settleFamily`: `settleOrder` (the mission's settle, the tour's backstop), `settleOpenOrder` (belay, and the mission's failure of an open or recalled order), `withdraw` (the withdraw RPC and the cascades after a failed after, a split base and a belayed parent), `BelaySplitOrder`, `belayedHere` and `settleSplit`. A split order is told twice, once split and once when its last child settles it. A hook error rolls back the ending. The bridge sets no hook yet; the task workflow's ticket wires `notifyTask`.
- **SetTask.** A task row change and its log entry go in one transaction (`SetTask`). It writes every column but the workflow id from the caller's row, logs the entry with kind `campaign` on whatever order the entry names, and adds `campaign`, `ref` and `round` to its payload. The task workflow is the row's only writer. Split children don't inherit the parent's task (`InsertChildOrders`), so a child's claimer isn't barred from review; the mission ticket (#182) refuses to split a campaign order before any task workflow runs. A row that already holds the values writes and logs nothing, so a re-run of the step that moved the task leaves one entry. `GetTask` reads a row, and the task states are constants (`TaskWorking` to `TaskDropped`).
- **Tests.** `TestOpenRejectsStage2Schema` opens a database made from `testdata/schema-before-180.sql`, a copy of `schema.sql` before this change, and gets the refusal with no new table or index added. `TestStage3Schema` checks the columns, the indexes, the open-ref index and the `task_workers` key. `TestInsertOrderOnce` inserts twice under one key and gets one id and one entry, plus the refusals. `TestInsertBridgeHailRaiseKey` raises two hails under two keys and one under one key twice, closed or open. `TestTaskHookEndings` covers settle done and failed, the tour's backstop, the mission's settle of an open order, withdraw, a belay of a recalled order, the cascades of a failed after and a belayed parent, and a split, with a re-run, an order with no task and a failing hook. `TestClaimReviewBarred` covers the pool and directed claims, a recall, the round 1 reviewer in round 2, a fix order's claimer and another task's review. `TestEligibleReviewers` leaves out dismissed, sickbay and barred crew members. `TestSetTask` covers the write, the re-run and a missing task. The older-database tests give their tables the new columns.

## Review verdicts (#181)

Dated 2026-11-16. The protocol, harness and holodeck half of `campaigns-stage-3-plan-2026-11-16.md` section 2. The bridge's side (the order command's `kind` and `review`, `no_verdict` in `order-done`, a work order's verdict dropped) is #182.

- **Order.** `OrderBody` gains `kind` (`work` or `review`, absent means work) and `review {base, round}`, in the schema, `protocol.ts` and `comms.OrderBody` (`comms.OrderReview`, `comms.OrderKindWork`, `comms.OrderKindReview`). `base` is the commit the task's first work order started from, and `round` counts from 1. The schema requires `review` when `kind` is `review` and refuses it otherwise.
- **Verdict.** `ReportBody` gains `verdict {findings: [{severity, title, body, path?, line?}]}` (`comms.Verdict`, `comms.VerdictFinding`). The schema holds the plan's limits: at most 20 findings; `severity` one of `high`, `medium`, `low`, and required; `title` one non-blank line of at most 200 characters without control characters, the finding's pattern, which both now share as `$defs.one_line_title`; `body` required, at most 8 KiB; `path` 1 to 512 with no control characters; `line` an integer from 1. A line needs a path (`dependentRequired`). An empty list is a pass. The schema counts characters, and the bridge and combadge count `body` and `path` in bytes of UTF-8, as for a finding's body.
- **Check.** The bridge holds the same limits (`comms.Verdict.Check`). Decoding a verdict never fails (`Verdict.UnmarshalJSON`): one that isn't an object, has no findings list or holds findings of the wrong shape decodes empty and keeps the reason for `Check`, so the rest of the report still reads and #182 can fail the review with that reason. A verdict built in Go with no findings is a pass. The bridge decodes leniently, as for every body: an unknown key is ignored, a missing body reads as empty, and an empty path or a line of 0 reads as none.
- **One case table.** `protocol/cases/verdict.json` holds every case once, with `{"repeat": x, "times": n}` for long strings and lists. `TestSchemaVerdict` runs each case through the Go validator and `Check`, and also checks that a verdict `Check` takes encodes into one the schema takes. combadge's `test/verdict.test.ts` runs the same cases through ajv and the report tool's zod. A case states where Go reads more leniently than the schema and zod (the unknown keys, the missing body, the empty path, line 0) and where bytes and characters differ.
- **Review prompt.** For a review order the sdk harness's instructions replace the work lines (`instructions`): the checkout holds the head under review at its commit; `git diff <base>..HEAD` and `git log <base>..HEAD` show the task's work; judge it against the order, run what helps, and change and commit nothing. When the order carries an earlier review's summary and blocking findings (decision 2: each review runs in a new session, and the bridge puts them in its body), judge the fix against them and say whether each is resolved. Then call `report` with a summary and a verdict, high for what must be fixed before landing, medium for a judgement call, low for follow-up work. Progress, AskUserQuestion, the tracker tools and `file_finding` stay, with one more line: what is wrong with the work under review goes in the verdict.
- **Report tool.** A review order's `report` takes `verdict` (`verdictInput`, the same limits, findings as strict objects, titles trimmed) and refuses a call without one with "a review's report needs a verdict; findings may be empty". A review that never calls `report` ends with its final text as the summary and no verdict. A work order's `report` has no `verdict` field: the tool's input schema leaves it out and zod strips one an agent sends, so the work report goes out without it. The bridge would drop it anyway with a warning, and keeping the field off the work tool keeps verdicts out of a work agent's view. serve carries the harness's verdict into the report.
- **Holodeck.** The `report` step takes `verdict` (an object) or `verdicts` (a non-empty array of objects), never both. An order picks `verdicts[round - 1]` by its `review.round`, the last entry past the end, and round 1 when it has none (`pickVerdict`). Either goes on any order, so a script can send a work order a verdict for the bridge to drop. A report step with neither sends no verdict. Validation checks only the shapes, as for `finding`. `fixtures/verdicts.json` blocks with one high in round 1 and passes after. Echo reports no verdict.
- **Tests.** Comms: `TestSchemaVerdict`, `TestVerdictDecodeKeepsReport`, `TestSchemaOrderReview` (review only with kind review, round from 1, a base, no unknown key) and `TestZeroBodiesValidate`'s review order and empty verdict. The golden variants `order.review.json` and `report.review.json` round-trip in Go (`TestGolden`) and TypeScript. combadge: the review prompt names the base and leaves out the work lines; `report` without a verdict is refused for a review and taken for work, a work order's verdict is left out, a review's bad verdicts are refused, an empty list passes; the holodeck picks `verdicts` by round and repeats the last, through serve and in process, plus its validation.
